### In-Context Iterative Policy Improvement for Dynamic Manipulation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15021v1)
- **Authors**: Mark Van der Merwe, Devesh Jha
- **Abstract**: Attention-based architectures trained on internet-scale language data have demonstrated state of the art reasoning ability for various language-based tasks, such as logic problems and textual reasoning. Additionally, these Large Language Models (LLMs) have exhibited the ability to perform few-shot prediction via in-context learning, in which input-output examples provided in the prompt are generalized to new inputs. This ability furthermore extends beyond standard language tasks, enabling few-shot learning for general patterns. In this work, we consider the application of in-context learning with pre-trained language models for dynamic manipulation. Dynamic manipulation introduces several crucial challenges, including increased dimensionality, complex dynamics, and partial observability. To address this, we take an iterative approach, and formulate our in-context learning problem to predict adjustments to a parametric policy based on previous interactions. We show across several tasks in simulation and on a physical robot that utilizing in-context learning outperforms alternative methods in the low data regime. Video summary of this work and experiments can be found https://youtu.be/2inxpdrq74U?si=dAdDYsUEr25nZvRn.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel approach called In-Context Policy Improvement (ICPI) for dynamic manipulation tasks.  ICPI utilizes the in-context learning capabilities of pre-trained Large Language Models (LLMs) to iteratively improve parametric policy parameters. The core idea is to formulate the problem as learning an "improvement operator" that predicts adjustments to a policy based on the outcomes of previous interactions. These interactions, along with policy parameters, are tokenized and fed into the LLM as prompts. The LLM outputs adjustments to the policy parameters, driving the system towards task success. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of ICPI on several dynamic manipulation tasks in both simulation and on a physical robot, showing it outperforms several baselines, including other in-context learning and policy optimization techniques, especially in low-data regimes.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using LLMs for *iterative policy improvement* in dynamic manipulation *via in-context learning* is novel.  While prior works have used LLMs for robotic planning, code generation, and reward function design, using them in a closed-loop, iterative policy refinement process within the realm of dynamic manipulation, particularly with a focus on low-data efficiency, distinguishes this work. Prior work on in-context trajectory improvement exists, but the framing of the problem as learning a *policy improvement operator* and showing that it can improve dynamic manipulation is novel.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in demonstrating that LLMs, pre-trained on language data, can be effectively leveraged for complex robotic tasks like dynamic manipulation *without fine-tuning*. This opens up possibilities for more adaptable and sample-efficient robotic learning systems. ICPI addresses crucial challenges in dynamic manipulation, such as high dimensionality, complex dynamics, and partial observability, by learning to refine policies through interaction history. The empirical results showing performance gains over baselines further bolster its significance.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Formulation:** The paper clearly defines the problem of iterative policy improvement and presents a well-structured approach.
    *   **Effective Use of In-Context Learning:** It demonstrates the ability to leverage LLMs for a task outside the traditional language domain.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The paper provides thorough experimental evaluation in simulation and on a real robot, comparing ICPI to several relevant baselines.
    *   **Ablation Studies:**  The ablation studies provide insight into design choices, such as the importance of LLM model choice and the tokenization strategy.
    *   **Low Data Efficiency:** The results clearly show the strength of the method is the low data regime, compared to methods that need much more data to train.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational Overhead:** Utilizing large pre-trained LLMs is computationally expensive, a point the authors acknowledge as a limitation.  This limits the speed of policy improvement, especially on the physical robot.
    *   **Limited Task Complexity:** While the tasks considered are representative of dynamic manipulation, they are still relatively simple compared to the full range of real-world manipulation challenges.
    *   **Dependence on a Good Policy Parameterization:** ICPI relies on a well-defined parametric policy; the effectiveness depends on the suitability of this parameterization. The method is less about searching for the best policy from scratch, and more about refining policies that are already reasonably good.
    *   **Black Box Nature:** The paper offers little insight into *why* ICPI works. What patterns are the LLMs picking up on? Understanding the internal reasoning would increase the paper's significance.

*   **Potential Influence:** ICPI has the potential to influence future research in several ways:
    *   **Inspiring LLM-Based Robotic Learning:** It can encourage exploration of LLMs for more complex robotic tasks beyond planning and code generation.
    *   **Promoting Sample-Efficient Methods:**  It highlights the benefits of in-context learning for robotic tasks, motivating the development of sample-efficient learning algorithms.
    *   **Combining LLMs and Traditional Methods:**  It can spur research into combining the reasoning abilities of LLMs with the strengths of more traditional control and planning methods.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel approach that addresses a significant problem in robotics - sample-efficient learning for dynamic manipulation. The use of in-context learning is well-motivated, and the experiments demonstrate its effectiveness. The results are convincing, especially in the low-data regime. While the paper has limitations related to computational overhead and task complexity, the strengths outweigh the weaknesses. The approach demonstrates the utility of LLMs outside their traditional domain, which has potential for impact. However, more insight into the LLM's internal reasoning and scaling to more complex tasks would increase the impact.

### Reversible Unfolding Network for Concealed Visual Perception with Generative Refinement
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15027v1)
- **Authors**: Chunming He, Fengyang Xiao, Rihan Zhang, Chengyu Fang, Deng-Ping Fan, Sina Farsiu
- **Abstract**: Existing methods for concealed visual perception (CVP) often leverage reversible strategies to decrease uncertainty, yet these are typically confined to the mask domain, leaving the potential of the RGB domain underexplored. To address this, we propose a reversible unfolding network with generative refinement, termed RUN++. Specifically, RUN++ first formulates the CVP task as a mathematical optimization problem and unfolds the iterative solution into a multi-stage deep network. This approach provides a principled way to apply reversible modeling across both mask and RGB domains while leveraging a diffusion model to resolve the resulting uncertainty. Each stage of the network integrates three purpose-driven modules: a Concealed Object Region Extraction (CORE) module applies reversible modeling to the mask domain to identify core object regions; a Context-Aware Region Enhancement (CARE) module extends this principle to the RGB domain to foster better foreground-background separation; and a Finetuning Iteration via Noise-based Enhancement (FINE) module provides a final refinement. The FINE module introduces a targeted Bernoulli diffusion model that refines only the uncertain regions of the segmentation mask, harnessing the generative power of diffusion for fine-detail restoration without the prohibitive computational cost of a full-image process. This unique synergy, where the unfolding network provides a strong uncertainty prior for the diffusion model, allows RUN++ to efficiently direct its focus toward ambiguous areas, significantly mitigating false positives and negatives. Furthermore, we introduce a new paradigm for building robust CVP systems that remain effective under real-world degradations and extend this concept into a broader bi-level optimization framework.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a Reversible Unfolding Network with Generative Refinement (RUN++) for concealed visual perception (CVP).  RUN++ formulates the CVP task as a mathematical optimization problem, unfolds the iterative solution into a multi-stage deep network, and integrates a diffusion model for uncertainty resolution. The network consists of Concealed Object Region Extraction (CORE), Context-Aware Region Enhancement (CARE), and Finetuning Iteration via Noise-based Enhancement (FINE) modules.  A targeted Bernoulli diffusion model (BDM) within the FINE module refines uncertain regions identified by the preceding modules.  The paper also introduces a paradigm for building robust CVP systems under real-world degradations and extends this concept into a bi-level optimization framework (BLCO). Extensive experiments across various CVP tasks demonstrate state-of-the-art performance and flexibility.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's primary novelty lies in the synergistic combination of a deep unfolding network (DUN) with a diffusion model for CVP.  While both DUNs and diffusion models are established techniques, their integration, particularly with the targeted refinement strategy using the FINE module, represents a significant advance. The application of reversible modeling across both the mask and RGB domains is also a valuable contribution. The extension to degradation-resistant CVP and the formulation of the BLCO framework add further novelty. However, the individual modules (CORE, CARE) seem to be utilizing well established principles like U-Nets, which may reduce novelty.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper stems from its performance improvements and its potential to advance CVP research. The state-of-the-art results across a diverse set of CVP tasks are compelling. The proposed framework shows strong potential for generalization. The BLCO framework offers a principled approach to combining low-level and high-level vision tasks, which could be influential in related fields. The careful design and the resulting efficiency also contribute to the significance.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   State-of-the-art performance on multiple CVP tasks.
    *   Principled integration of DUNs and diffusion models.
    *   Novel targeted refinement strategy (FINE module).
    *   Demonstrated robustness to real-world degradations.
    *   Generalizable bi-level optimization framework (BLCO).
    *   Well-written and clearly articulated methodology with thorough experimentation.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The individual modules (CORE, CARE) might leverage techniques that are incremental, rather than ground-breaking.
    *   The computational overhead of the diffusion model, even with the targeted refinement, could be a limitation in some applications. The analysis does not explicitly address this concern. The description of DDIM sampling reducing the inference cost is vague, without mentioning the impact it has on the performance (quality of the refined mask).
    *   While the BLCO framework is promising, its application is limited to the demonstrated low-light/object detection scenario. More diverse examples would strengthen this part of the work.
    *   The ablation study can be more thorough. For example, it would be interesting to see the impact of the noise schedule used in the diffusion module.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has a good potential to influence future research in CVP. The integration of DUNs and diffusion models could inspire new architectures and algorithms. The BLCO framework could lead to new approaches for collaborative vision systems. The degradation-resistant CVP adaptation is also practically relevant and may be adopted by other researchers.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

While the paper presents a significant advancement in CVP through its novel architecture and demonstrates strong performance gains and robustness, it also faces limitations in certain aspects. The individual modules are built upon existing technologies and more exploration is needed on its potential in diverse applications to increase the likelihood of it making lasting impact.

### MoEcho: Exploiting Side-Channel Attacks to Compromise User Privacy in Mixture-of-Experts LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15036v1)
- **Authors**: Ruyi Ding, Tianhong Xu, Xinyi Shen, Aidong Adam Ding, Yunsi Fei
- **Abstract**: The transformer architecture has become a cornerstone of modern AI, fueling remarkable progress across applications in natural language processing, computer vision, and multimodal learning. As these models continue to scale explosively for performance, implementation efficiency remains a critical challenge. Mixture of Experts (MoE) architectures, selectively activating specialized subnetworks (experts), offer a unique balance between model accuracy and computational cost. However, the adaptive routing in MoE architectures, where input tokens are dynamically directed to specialized experts based on their semantic meaning inadvertently opens up a new attack surface for privacy breaches. These input-dependent activation patterns leave distinctive temporal and spatial traces in hardware execution, which adversaries could exploit to deduce sensitive user data. In this work, we propose MoEcho, discovering a side channel analysis based attack surface that compromises user privacy on MoE based systems. Specifically, in MoEcho, we introduce four novel architectural side channels on different computing platforms, including Cache Occupancy Channels and Pageout+Reload on CPUs, and Performance Counter and TLB Evict+Reload on GPUs, respectively. Exploiting these vulnerabilities, we propose four attacks that effectively breach user privacy in large language models (LLMs) and vision language models (VLMs) based on MoE architectures: Prompt Inference Attack, Response Reconstruction Attack, Visual Inference Attack, and Visual Reconstruction Attack. MoEcho is the first runtime architecture level security analysis of the popular MoE structure common in modern transformers, highlighting a serious security and privacy threat and calling for effective and timely safeguards when harnessing MoE based models for developing efficient large scale AI services.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "MoEcho: Exploiting Side-Channel Attacks to Compromise User Privacy in Mixture-of-Experts LLMs":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MoEcho, a novel side-channel attack targeting the Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architecture commonly used in modern large language models (LLMs) and vision-language models (VLMs). The core idea is that the dynamic routing of input tokens to different experts in an MoE model creates exploitable temporal and spatial patterns in hardware execution. The authors identify four architectural side-channels on CPUs and GPUs (cache occupancy, pageout+reload, performance counters, and TLB eviction) that can be used to extract information about the active experts.  They then demonstrate how this information can be used to launch various privacy attacks, including prompt inference, response reconstruction, and visual attribute/reconstruction. The attacks are evaluated on several open-source MoE-based models, with a focus on the DeepSeek architecture, and achieve high success rates in inferring sensitive user data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's novelty lies in identifying and exploiting the side-channel vulnerabilities specific to the MoE architecture. While side-channel attacks on ML models are not new, previous works have largely focused on extracting model parameters or structure, rather than compromising user privacy via the input-dependent expert activation patterns. The specific side-channels proposed for CPUs and GPUs are relatively standard techniques, but their application in the context of MoE models and privacy is original.

*   **Significance:** The paper highlights a potentially serious security and privacy threat in a popular architecture used for efficient scaling of AI services. The demonstrated attacks are concerning, particularly the prompt inference and response reconstruction, as they can reveal sensitive user data in applications dealing with healthcare records or personal documents. This underscores the need for security safeguards when adopting MoE-based models.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper is well-written and provides a clear explanation of the MoE architecture, the proposed side-channels, and the attack methodologies.
    *   The evaluation is comprehensive and includes experiments on different MoE models and platforms.
    *   The attacks are shown to be effective in practice, achieving high success rates in recovering sensitive information.
    *   The authors discuss potential mitigation strategies, providing a valuable starting point for future research.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The threat model assumes a relatively strong adversary capable of co-location with the victim and access to specific hardware resources (e.g., GPU performance counters). This may not be realistic in all deployment scenarios.
    *   The paper focuses primarily on the DeepSeek architecture. While the findings likely generalize to other MoE models, further evaluations on a wider range of models would strengthen the results.
    *   The mitigation strategies discussed are somewhat high-level, and more detailed analysis and evaluation of their effectiveness would be valuable.
    *   The practical attack implementation against deep-learning model may face some extra considerations such as hardware scheduling, model versioning, or system configurations.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to have a significant impact on the field by raising awareness of the privacy risks associated with MoE architectures. It will likely spur further research on developing more robust defenses against side-channel attacks in MoE models and on secure deployment strategies.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper uses known side-channel techniques, its originality in applying these specifically to exploit MoE architectures for user privacy violations elevates the novelty, and the potential influence on future research related to the mitigation measures is significant. Still, some practical conditions that affect performance in real-world attack scenarios are not considered.

### Reward-Shifted Speculative Sampling Is An Efficient Test-Time Weak-to-Strong Aligner
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15044v1)
- **Authors**: Bolian Li, Yanran Wu, Xinyu Luo, Ruqi Zhang
- **Abstract**: Aligning large language models (LLMs) with human preferences has become a critical step in their development. Recent research has increasingly focused on test-time alignment, where additional compute is allocated during inference to enhance LLM safety and reasoning capabilities. However, these test-time alignment techniques often incur substantial inference costs, limiting their practical application. We are inspired by the speculative sampling acceleration, which leverages a small draft model to efficiently predict future tokens, to address the efficiency bottleneck of test-time alignment. We introduce the reward-Shifted Speculative Sampling (SSS) algorithm, in which the draft model is aligned with human preferences, while the target model remains unchanged. We theoretically demonstrate that the distributional shift between the aligned draft model and the unaligned target model can be exploited to recover the RLHF optimal solution without actually obtaining it, by modifying the acceptance criterion and bonus token distribution. Our algorithm achieves superior gold reward scores at a significantly reduced inference cost in test-time weak-to-strong alignment experiments, thereby validating both its effectiveness and efficiency.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Reward-Shifted Speculative Sampling (SSS), a novel approach that combines test-time alignment and speculative sampling to efficiently align large language models (LLMs) with human preferences. SSS aligns a smaller "draft" model with human preferences and uses it to speculatively generate high-reward tokens, while the larger "target" model ensures fluency through verification. This method eliminates the need for external reward models, theoretically guarantees recovery of the RLHF optimal solution, and demonstrates superior performance compared to existing test-time alignment baselines at a significantly reduced inference cost.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The idea of combining speculative sampling with test-time alignment to reduce reliance on external reward models is a genuinely innovative step.  Instead of using an external RM *during* inference, they *align* a smaller draft model, then leverage speculative sampling.  This circumvents the computationally expensive interaction with an external RM during the *critical* inference phase. The theoretical guarantee of recovering the RLHF optimal solution, while based on assumptions, is a valuable contribution. Redefining the speculative sampling objective from simply recovering the target distribution to recovering the RLHF optimal solution is a key innovative insight.
* **Significance:** Reducing the computational cost of aligning LLMs with human preferences is a highly relevant and impactful problem. Test-time alignment approaches are gaining increasing attention as a training-free alternative to RLHF. SSS directly addresses the efficiency bottlenecks of these approaches by eliminating the need for frequent interaction with an external reward model. The empirical results, demonstrating improved alignment quality and reduced inference costs, significantly bolster the significance of the work. If the method translates well to other LLMs, it could have a noticeable impact on the practical deployment of aligned LLMs. The ability to achieve strong alignment using relatively weak draft models is also valuable for resource-constrained scenarios. The ability to attain high gold reward scores with a 5x speedup demonstrates an important practical improvement.

* **Strengths:**
    * The core idea is novel and addresses a key bottleneck in LLM alignment.
    * Strong theoretical justification for the proposed approach.
    * Clear and well-structured presentation.
    * Thorough empirical evaluation and comparison with existing baselines.
    * Demonstrates substantial improvements in both alignment quality and inference efficiency.
    * Addresses a timely and significant problem in the LLM field.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * The theoretical guarantee relies on the assumption that the shifted draft model closely approximates the RLHF optimal solution. This assumption is not always easy to verify in practice and requires careful hyperparameter tuning during draft model post-training.
    * While the method reduces the reliance on external reward models, it doesn't entirely eliminate them.  The draft model still needs to be aligned using DPO and a dataset, which is inherently a form of reward shaping.
    * The experiments are conducted on a specific dataset (HH-RLHF) and model sizes. Generalizability to other datasets, architectures, and significantly larger models needs further exploration. The practical tricks to handle the gap between the idealized and realistic assumptions of the draft model will require a careful treatment to enable the method in other contexts.
    * Limited discussion about the limitations of using a small draft model for capturing complex human preferences. How well does this approach scale to more nuanced or subjective preferences?

* **Impact:** The paper presents a technically sound and well-validated approach that makes a tangible contribution to the field. While the method has certain assumptions and limitations, it provides a valuable step towards more efficient and practical LLM alignment. The method has potential to improve and streamline the deployment of safer and more helpful LLMs.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**
The paper delivers a significant improvement on current alignment methods. Its major strengths lie in the innovative combination of test-time alignment with speculative sampling. A key weakness involves reliance on a good approximation via DPO, but nonetheless, the resulting improvements are significant enough to merit a high score. I am cautiously optimistic about the practicality given the significant speedups, but the requirement for hyperparameter tuning and the limitations due to the draft model prevent a higher rating.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Emergent Crowds Dynamics from Language-Driven Multi-Agent Interactions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15047v1)
- **Authors**: Yibo Liu, Liam Shatzel, Brandon Haworth, Teseo Schneider
- **Abstract**: Animating and simulating crowds using an agent-based approach is a well-established area where every agent in the crowd is individually controlled such that global human-like behaviour emerges. We observe that human navigation and movement in crowds are often influenced by complex social and environmental interactions, driven mainly by language and dialogue. However, most existing work does not consider these dimensions and leads to animations where agent-agent and agent-environment interactions are largely limited to steering and fixed higher-level goal extrapolation. We propose a novel method that exploits large language models (LLMs) to control agents' movement. Our method has two main components: a dialogue system and language-driven navigation. We periodically query agent-centric LLMs conditioned on character personalities, roles, desires, and relationships to control the generation of inter-agent dialogue when necessitated by the spatial and social relationships with neighbouring agents. We then use the conversation and each agent's personality, emotional state, vision, and physical state to control the navigation and steering of each agent. Our model thus enables agents to make motion decisions based on both their perceptual inputs and the ongoing dialogue. We validate our method in two complex scenarios that exemplify the interplay between social interactions, steering, and crowding. In these scenarios, we observe that grouping and ungrouping of agents automatically occur. Additionally, our experiments show that our method serves as an information-passing mechanism within the crowd. As a result, our framework produces more realistic crowd simulations, with emergent group behaviours arising naturally from any environmental setting.
- **Summary**: Okay, I'll provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "Emergent Crowds Dynamics from Language-Driven Multi-Agent Interactions."

**Summary:**

This paper proposes a novel method for simulating crowd behavior by integrating Large Language Models (LLMs) into agent-based simulations.  Instead of relying solely on pre-defined goals and steering mechanisms, the agents in the simulation use LLMs to generate dialogues and make decisions based on these conversations, their personalities, relationships with other agents, and visual perception of the environment. The authors validate their method through two scenarios: a university club fair and an accident in a museum. The experiments show that this approach allows for emergent behaviors like grouping, ungrouping, and information propagation within the crowd, leading to more realistic and nuanced crowd simulations.  The paper highlights the potential of using LLMs to bridge the gap between high-level social interactions and low-level locomotion control in crowd simulations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The core novelty lies in the seamless integration of LLMs to drive multi-agent dynamics in crowd simulation. While LLMs have been used in multi-agent settings, the paper's contribution is in connecting social interactions, through language, directly to the agents' physical movement and decision-making processes in a decentralized manner. Existing works that leverage LLMs often focus either solely on the language aspect or use them for high-level planning, whereas this paper attempts to control all locomotion phases, even steering, through the LLM, enabling more dynamic emergent behaviour. The coupling of vision, dialogue, individual agent states and LLM decisions in each simulation step is novel, although the individual techniques of crowd simulation and LLM application have been well-established.

**Significance:**

The significance of this work stems from the potential to create more realistic and engaging crowd simulations. By incorporating social interactions and agent personalities, the simulated crowds exhibit more human-like behaviors, making them more valuable for applications in architecture, animation, and human behavior analysis. The paper demonstrates that this approach allows for the emergence of complex behaviors like information propagation, grouping and dispersal without the need for explicit scripting, suggesting the framework's scalability and adaptability to different scenarios.

**Strengths:**

*   **Integration:** The integration of LLMs to control various locomotion phases like navigation and steering.
*   **Emergence:** Demonstration of emergent behaviors (grouping, ungrouping, information flow) resulting from the LLM-driven interactions.
*   **Decentralization:** Decentralized control allows for more scalable and realistic simulations.
*   **Rich Agent States:** Agent states that consider personalities, relationships, and emotions contribute to more nuanced and believable behavior.
*   **Experimental Validation:** The two scenarios, while somewhat simple, effectively demonstrate the method's capabilities.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Cost:** The heavy reliance on LLMs is computationally expensive, potentially limiting the size and complexity of simulations. The paper acknowledges the slowness (5 seconds per query) of the distributed LLM API as a concern and calls for an investigation into faster, local models.
*   **LLM Reliability:** The paper notes rare occasions where the LLM produces invalid outputs, necessitating frame skipping. This highlights a dependency on the LLM's consistency and reliability, which can be unpredictable.
*   **Limited Evaluation:** The evaluation, while illustrative, is limited in scope. It would be valuable to compare the results with existing crowd simulation techniques using quantitative metrics (e.g., crowd density, average speed, flow rate) to rigorously assess the improvements offered by the proposed method. There isn't a clear comparison to baselines, and the observed behaviors can only be assessed subjectively.
*   **Over-reliance on LLM black-box nature:** While the framework introduces sophisticated behaviour, it also leads to simplification and black-box modelling through LLMs. The LLM's decision-making process is not always transparent, which can make it difficult to understand and control the simulation.
*   **Generalizability:** the LLM is prompting is crucial, and while simple instructions are reported to be used, it is difficult to assess if the setup could be generalized to other settings.

**Potential Influence:**

This paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of crowd simulation by demonstrating the power of LLMs to create more realistic and engaging simulations. It can inspire researchers to explore new ways of integrating language and social interactions into agent-based models, leading to advancements in areas such as virtual environments, animation, and social behavior modeling.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper presents a promising and novel approach, it is not without its limitations. The computational cost, lack of quantitative evaluation, and reliance on the LLM's reliability are significant concerns that need to be addressed in future work. Furthermore, the limitations stemming from the over-reliance on black-box modelling impact the ability to reason about behaviours. Considering the strengths (novel integration, emergent behaviors, decentralization) and weaknesses (computational cost, limited evaluation, potential reliance on LLM black-box nature) and the potential influence on the field, I believe a score of 7 is justified.
