### Don't Think Twice! Over-Reasoning Impairs Confidence Calibration
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15050v1)
- **Authors**: Romain Lacombe, Kerrie Wu, Eddie Dilworth
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models deployed as question answering tools require robust calibration to avoid overconfidence. We systematically evaluate how reasoning capabilities and budget affect confidence assessment accuracy, using the ClimateX dataset (Lacombe et al., 2023) and expanding it to human and planetary health. Our key finding challenges the "test-time scaling" paradigm: while recent reasoning LLMs achieve 48.7% accuracy in assessing expert confidence, increasing reasoning budgets consistently impairs rather than improves calibration. Extended reasoning leads to systematic overconfidence that worsens with longer thinking budgets, producing diminishing and negative returns beyond modest computational investments. Conversely, search-augmented generation dramatically outperforms pure reasoning, achieving 89.3% accuracy by retrieving relevant evidence. Our results suggest that information access, rather than reasoning depth or inference budget, may be the critical bottleneck for improved confidence calibration of knowledge-intensive tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Don't Think Twice! Over-Reasoning Impairs Confidence Calibration":

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the relationship between reasoning capabilities, computational budget (inference budget), and confidence calibration in Large Language Models (LLMs), particularly in knowledge-intensive domains like climate science and public health. Using the CLIMATEX dataset (expert-labeled climate statements) and a novel dataset on carcinogenicity from the IARC, the authors evaluate how well LLMs can assess human expert confidence in statements.  The surprising key finding is that increasing the reasoning budget (e.g., longer chains of thought) *impairs* confidence calibration, leading to systematic overconfidence.  Conversely, retrieval-augmented generation (RAG), which incorporates external evidence, significantly improves calibration accuracy.  The authors conclude that information access is a more critical bottleneck for improved confidence calibration than reasoning depth or inference budget.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper challenges a commonly held belief about LLMs – that more reasoning always leads to better results.  Demonstrating that increasing reasoning budget can *harm* confidence calibration is a novel and counterintuitive finding. The comparison between increased reasoning versus RAG is also a key contribution. While other studies have looked at calibration, this work specifically investigates the impact of "test-time scaling" and specifically contrasts it with the addition of knowledge through retrieval. The application to high-stakes domains (climate and health) enhances the paper's relevance. However, the prompt-engineering methodology within DSPy is relatively standard.

* **Significance:** This work has significant implications for how LLMs are deployed in critical applications. If more reasoning leads to *worse* calibration, relying on LLMs for high-stakes decision-making becomes even riskier without careful consideration.  The study highlights the importance of focusing on knowledge retrieval mechanisms to enhance reliability, rather than simply increasing computational resources for reasoning. The findings can influence the design of LLM-based systems, particularly those used in scientific or decision-support contexts. The exploration of transferability across the datasets (climate and IARC) also strengthens the claims. The use of human-expert data is vital and adds robustness to the evaluation.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear Research Question:** The paper's central questions about reasoning and calibration are clearly defined.
    * **Rigorous Methodology:** The authors employ a systematic evaluation using established datasets (CLIMATEX) and a newly constructed dataset (IARC). The experimental setup with varying inference budgets is well-designed.
    * **Counterintuitive Findings:** The results are surprising and challenge conventional wisdom about LLMs.
    * **Practical Implications:** The paper provides actionable insights for developing more reliable LLM applications.
    * **Use of RAG as baseline:** The contrasting of increased reasoning with RAG highlights the most effective approach in improving confidence.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Model Exploration:** Although several models are listed, there is not a rigorous investigation of why some models are affected worse by increasing "thinking budget" as other models.
    * **Domain Specificity:**  While the paper addresses two important domains (climate and health), further generalization to other knowledge-intensive areas would strengthen the findings.
    * **Prompt Engineering Detail:** The description of prompt engineering, while noted with DSPy, could be slightly enhanced.
    * **Baselines:** While the fine-tuned RoBERTa classifier and human baseline are provided, stronger few-shot baseline would strengthen results.

* **Potential Influence:** This paper is likely to influence the research community by shifting focus towards information access and retrieval mechanisms for improving LLM reliability and calibration. It provides valuable insights that can inform the design of future LLM architectures and deployment strategies. It may prompt researchers to re-evaluate the emphasis on reasoning capabilities in isolation, urging them to consider the crucial role of knowledge retrieval in achieving accurate and trustworthy AI systems.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents novel and significant findings that challenge a widely held assumption about LLMs. The rigorous methodology and practical implications contribute significantly to the field. While there is always room for further generalization and deeper model analysis, the core message is clear and well-supported by the experimental results. It is an important contribution that will likely influence future research directions. The score reflects the paper's robust methodology, counterintuitive findings, and practical relevance, tempered by the potential for further exploration of specific model behavior and further generalizability across more domains.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### S3LoRA: Safe Spectral Sharpness-Guided Pruning in Adaptation of Agent Planner
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15068v1)
- **Authors**: Shuang Ao, Gopal Rumchurn
- **Abstract**: Adapting Large Language Models (LLMs) using parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) techniques such as LoRA has enabled powerful capabilities in LLM-based agents. However, these adaptations can unintentionally compromise safety alignment, leading to unsafe or unstable behaviors, particularly in agent planning tasks. Existing safety-aware adaptation methods often require access to both base and instruction-tuned model checkpoints, which are frequently unavailable in practice, limiting their applicability. We propose S3LoRA (Safe Spectral Sharpness-Guided Pruning LoRA), a lightweight, data-free, and model-independent framework that mitigates safety risks in LoRA-adapted models by inspecting only the fine-tuned weight updates. We first introduce Magnitude-Aware Spherically Normalized SVD (MAS-SVD), which robustly analyzes the structural properties of LoRA updates while preserving global magnitude information. We then design the Spectral Sharpness Index (SSI), a sharpness-aware metric to detect layers with highly concentrated and potentially unsafe updates. These layers are pruned post-hoc to reduce risk without sacrificing task performance. Extensive experiments and ablation studies across agent planning and language generation tasks show that S3LoRA consistently improves safety metrics while maintaining or improving utility metrics and significantly reducing inference cost. These results establish S3LoRA as a practical and scalable solution for safely deploying LLM-based agents in real-world, resource-constrained, and safety-critical environments.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces S³LORA (Safe Spectral Sharpness-Guided Pruning LoRA), a novel, data-free framework designed to improve the safety alignment of Large Language Models (LLMs) fine-tuned with Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA). S³LORA aims to mitigate safety risks introduced during LoRA adaptation by analyzing only the fine-tuned weights, without needing access to the original base or instruction-tuned models.  The core of S³LORA is the Magnitude-Aware Spherically Normalized SVD (MAS-SVD), a spectral decomposition method that enhances robustness to outliers while preserving magnitude information.  The Spectral Sharpness Index (SSI) derived from MAS-SVD is used to identify and prune layers exhibiting highly concentrated, potentially unsafe updates.  Experiments across agent planning and language generation tasks demonstrate that S³LORA enhances safety metrics (lower ASR, HS) while maintaining or improving utility metrics and reducing inference cost.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates novelty in several key aspects:

    *   **Data-free safety alignment:** S³LORA's ability to improve safety alignment *without* requiring the base or instruction-tuned models is a significant advantage over existing methods like SafeLoRA and SPLORA. This makes it applicable in scenarios where paired models are unavailable, which is often the case in practice.
    *   **MAS-SVD:** The proposed MAS-SVD is a novel spectral decomposition method tailored to the specific characteristics of LoRA updates.  It enhances robustness and preserves magnitude information, which are critical for accurate identification of risky layers. The spherical normalization component addresses sensitivity to outliers, while magnitude restoration helps to ensure that the most substantial changes are not prematurely pruned.
    *   **Spectral Sharpness Index (SSI):** The SSI is a novel sharpness-aware metric specifically designed to identify potentially unsafe layers based on the concentration of updates along dominant directions.

*   **Significance:** The work addresses a significant problem: the potential for PEFT methods like LoRA to compromise the safety alignment of LLMs, particularly in the context of agent planning. By providing a practical and scalable solution to this problem, S³LORA contributes to the safe deployment of LLM-based agents in real-world environments.  The reduction in inference cost due to pruning further enhances its practical value.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Practical Applicability:** The data-free and model-independent nature of S³LORA makes it highly practical and applicable to a wider range of scenarios than existing methods.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experiments demonstrate consistent improvements in safety metrics while maintaining or improving utility metrics across diverse tasks and models.  The ablation studies provide evidence for the effectiveness of the individual components of S³LORA.
    *   **Clear and Well-Written:** The paper is clearly written and well-structured, making it easy to understand the proposed method and the experimental results.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Heuristic Pruning Threshold:** The selection of the pruning threshold (the number of layers to prune) is based on an ablation study, which is not necessarily optimal.  A more adaptive or theoretically grounded approach to threshold selection could potentially improve performance. While the paper explores the sensitivity to this parameter, an automatic approach to setting this is not presented.
    *   **Generalization of SSI:**  The assumption that a high SSI always correlates with safety risks might not hold in all scenarios. Domain-specific nuances might influence the relationship between spectral sharpness and model behavior. Future research could focus on adapting the SSI or incorporating additional factors to improve its accuracy.
    * Limited theoretical guarantee: While the Wedin's theorem suggests that high singular values can lead to unstable deviations, the connection between the SSI score and downstream task safety is only empirically validated and lacks a strong theoretical grounding.

*   **Potential Influence:** S³LORA has the potential to influence the development of safer and more efficient LLM-based agents.  The MAS-SVD and SSI could be adopted in other safety alignment methods. The focus on analyzing only fine-tuned weights could inspire new approaches to model analysis and intervention.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the novelty in approach, the clear significance, the strong empirical results, and the good practical value, but considering the limited theoretical backing, the heuristic tuning of pruning, and the assumption about the relation between SSI and safety, I rate this paper with a score of 7.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### CurveFlow: Curvature-Guided Flow Matching for Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15093v1)
- **Authors**: Yan Luo, Drake Du, Hao Huang, Yi Fang, Mengyu Wang
- **Abstract**: Existing rectified flow models are based on linear trajectories between data and noise distributions. This linearity enforces zero curvature, which can inadvertently force the image generation process through low-probability regions of the data manifold. A key question remains underexplored: how does the curvature of these trajectories correlate with the semantic alignment between generated images and their corresponding captions, i.e., instructional compliance? To address this, we introduce CurveFlow, a novel flow matching framework designed to learn smooth, non-linear trajectories by directly incorporating curvature guidance into the flow path. Our method features a robust curvature regularization technique that penalizes abrupt changes in the trajectory's intrinsic dynamics.Extensive experiments on MS COCO 2014 and 2017 demonstrate that CurveFlow achieves state-of-the-art performance in text-to-image generation, significantly outperforming both standard rectified flow variants and other non-linear baselines like Rectified Diffusion. The improvements are especially evident in semantic consistency metrics such as BLEU, METEOR, ROUGE, and CLAIR. This confirms that our curvature-aware modeling substantially enhances the model's ability to faithfully follow complex instructions while simultaneously maintaining high image quality. The code is made publicly available at https://github.com/Harvard-AI-and-Robotics-Lab/CurveFlow.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "CurveFlow: Curvature-Guided Flow Matching for Image Generation" addresses a key limitation of existing rectified flow (RF) models, which rely on linear trajectories between noise and data distributions. The authors argue that this linearity forces image generation through low-probability regions of the data manifold and negatively impacts semantic consistency (instructional compliance) in text-to-image generation. To overcome this, the paper introduces CurveFlow, a novel flow matching framework that learns smooth, non-linear trajectories by explicitly incorporating curvature guidance into the flow path. CurveFlow includes a robust curvature regularization technique that penalizes abrupt changes in trajectory dynamics. Experimental results on MS COCO demonstrate that CurveFlow achieves state-of-the-art performance in text-to-image generation, outperforming both standard RF variants and other non-linear baselines, especially in semantic consistency metrics.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of introducing curvature into flow-based generative models is a valuable contribution.  The core novelty lies in:

    *   Identifying the limitation of linear trajectories in RF models in relation to semantic consistency.
    *   Formulating CurveFlow, incorporating curvature guidance through learned coefficients and addressing the instability of empirical curvature estimation with a robust regularization term based on intrinsic coefficient dynamics.
*   **Significance:**
    *   **Improved Semantic Consistency:** CurveFlow demonstrates improvements in semantic consistency metrics (BLEU, METEOR, ROUGE, and CLAIR) compared to existing rectified flow models and other baselines. This is a significant step forward in improving the quality and controllability of generated images, especially in text-to-image tasks. This confirms that trajectory curvature has an importance on generated images' semantically consistency.
    *   **State-of-the-art Performance:** The experimental results indicate that CurveFlow achieves state-of-the-art performance on MS COCO for text-to-image generation.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Motivation:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing RF models.
    *   **Well-Defined Approach:** CurveFlow is a well-defined and technically sound approach with clear mathematical formulations.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experiments are comprehensive, using multiple metrics and comparing against relevant baselines on the MS COCO dataset.
    *   **Qualitative Results:** The qualitative results provide intuitive examples of how CurveFlow improves semantic accuracy and detail alignment.
    *   **Ablation study:** The ablation study shows the performance change with different parameters, further confirming the importance of them.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Cost:** While CurveFlow offers a performance advantage, the paper should elaborate more clearly on the practical implications of the training time, relative to the baselines.
    *   **Dataset Limitation:** The paper mainly uses MS COCO for evaluation. It could have included experiments on other datasets, potentially challenging the model's generalization ability.
    *   **Parameter Sensitivity:** While the paper investigates the impact of lambda, the performance is sensitive to the choice of lambda and this is worth highlighting as a limitation of this approach. More investigation might be needed to identify robust hyperparameters for this model.

*   **Potential Influence:** CurveFlow has the potential to significantly influence the field of flow-based generative models by opening up new avenues for incorporating geometric information into the flow path. This could lead to more controllable and semantically rich image generation.

**Justification of Score:**

CurveFlow presents a novel approach to address a fundamental limitation of rectified flow models. The experiments provide compelling evidence of the method's effectiveness in improving semantic consistency in text-to-image generation. However, a few factors limit the paper's impact: the computational cost compared to rectified flow models, and the dataset limitation.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Evaluating Sparse Autoencoders for Monosemantic Representation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15094v1)
- **Authors**: Moghis Fereidouni, Muhammad Umair Haider, Peizhong Ju, A. B. Siddique
- **Abstract**: A key barrier to interpreting large language models is polysemanticity, where neurons activate for multiple unrelated concepts. Sparse autoencoders (SAEs) have been proposed to mitigate this issue by transforming dense activations into sparse, more interpretable features. While prior work suggests that SAEs promote monosemanticity, there has been no quantitative comparison with their base models. This paper provides the first systematic evaluation of SAEs against base models concerning monosemanticity. We introduce a fine-grained concept separability score based on the Jensen-Shannon distance, which captures how distinctly a neuron's activation distributions vary across concepts. Using Gemma-2-2B and multiple SAE variants across five benchmarks, we show that SAEs reduce polysemanticity and achieve higher concept separability. However, greater sparsity of SAEs does not always yield better separability and often impairs downstream performance. To assess practical utility, we evaluate concept-level interventions using two strategies: full neuron masking and partial suppression. We find that, compared to base models, SAEs enable more precise concept-level control when using partial suppression. Building on this, we propose Attenuation via Posterior Probabilities (APP), a new intervention method that uses concept-conditioned activation distributions for targeted suppression. APP outperforms existing approaches in targeted concept removal.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Evaluating Sparse Autoencoders for Monosemantic Representation" presents a systematic evaluation of Sparse Autoencoders (SAEs) in promoting monosemanticity in Large Language Models (LLMs). The authors compare SAEs against their base models using a novel concept separability score based on Jensen-Shannon distance, quantifying how distinctly a neuron's activation distributions vary across concepts. They find that SAEs reduce polysemanticity and achieve higher concept separability, but increased sparsity doesn't always improve separability and can impair downstream performance. They evaluate concept-level interventions (masking and suppression) and introduce Attenuation via Posterior Probabilities (APP), a new intervention method for targeted suppression, demonstrating its superiority over existing methods in concept removal.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its **quantitative assessment** of SAEs' effectiveness in promoting monosemanticity *relative to their base models*.  Prior work largely relied on qualitative assessments or isolated SAE analysis. The introduction of the Jensen-Shannon distance-based concept separability score is also a valuable contribution, offering a more nuanced and distribution-aware metric than simple overlap statistics. The APP method for targeted concept removal builds on existing intervention strategies, but its use of concept-conditioned activation distributions is a unique aspect.

*   **Significance:** The findings contribute significantly to our understanding of SAEs. By quantifying their monosemanticity benefits, the paper provides empirical evidence supporting their potential for enhancing model interpretability. The findings concerning the trade-offs between sparsity, separability, and downstream performance are valuable for future SAE design. The APP intervention method presents a promising approach for concept erasure, potentially improving model control and safety. The critical evaluation of prior art such as range masking and full masking is significant as the paper empirically shows the trade-offs and limitations of simpler methods.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Rigorous methodology:** The paper employs a well-defined methodology, using Gemma-2-2B and multiple SAE variants across five benchmarks, providing a comprehensive evaluation.
    *   **Novel metrics:** The introduction and use of Jensen-Shannon distance-based concept separability improves upon previous metrics for evaluating monosemanticity.
    *   **Practical evaluation:** The use of concept-level interventions, including the novel APP method, provides practical insights into SAEs' utility for model control.
    *   **Clear presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly presents its findings with appropriate visualizations.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Model Scope:** The core experiments are done with one base model. While useful, evaluating against multiple models could better provide generalizability.
    *   **Focus on Sentence-Level Concepts:** The use of sentence-level concepts can limit generalizability to other concept types or granularities. Word-level or more complex conceptual relationships could be evaluated to further strengthen conclusions.
    *   **Potential for hyperparameter sensitivity:** The reliance of methods like Range Masking and Adaptive Dampening on a saliency threshold *p* introduces a hyperparameter that could affect the results.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research on SAEs by providing a strong empirical foundation for their monosemanticity benefits and trade-offs. The concept separability score and APP intervention method could become valuable tools for evaluating and controlling LLMs.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field by providing a rigorous, quantitative assessment of SAEs' monosemanticity benefits and by introducing a novel intervention method. The weaknesses, while present, do not significantly detract from the paper's overall contribution.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Nemotron-CC-Math: A 133 Billion-Token-Scale High Quality Math Pretraining Dataset
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15096v1)
- **Authors**: Rabeeh Karimi Mahabadi, Sanjeev Satheesh, Shrimai Prabhumoye, Mostofa Patwary, Mohammad Shoeybi, Bryan Catanzaro
- **Abstract**: Pretraining large language models (LLMs) on high-quality, structured data such as mathematics and code substantially enhances reasoning capabilities. However, existing math-focused datasets built from Common Crawl suffer from degraded quality due to brittle extraction heuristics, lossy HTML-to-text conversion, and the failure to reliably preserve mathematical structure. In this work, we introduce Nemotron-CC-Math, a large-scale, high-quality mathematical corpus constructed from Common Crawl using a novel, domain-agnostic pipeline specifically designed for robust scientific text extraction. Unlike previous efforts, our pipeline recovers math across various formats (e.g., MathJax, KaTeX, MathML) by leveraging layout-aware rendering with lynx and a targeted LLM-based cleaning stage. This approach preserves the structural integrity of equations and code blocks while removing boilerplate, standardizing notation into LaTeX representation, and correcting inconsistencies. We collected a large, high-quality math corpus, namely Nemotron-CC-Math-3+ (133B tokens) and Nemotron-CC-Math-4+ (52B tokens). Notably, Nemotron-CC-Math-4+ not only surpasses all prior open math datasets-including MegaMath, FineMath, and OpenWebMath-but also contains 5.5 times more tokens than FineMath-4+, which was previously the highest-quality math pretraining dataset. When used to pretrain a Nemotron-T 8B model, our corpus yields +4.8 to +12.6 gains on MATH and +4.6 to +14.3 gains on MBPP+ over strong baselines, while also improving general-domain performance on MMLU and MMLU-Stem. We present the first pipeline to reliably extract scientific content--including math--from noisy web-scale data, yielding measurable gains in math, code, and general reasoning, and setting a new state of the art among open math pretraining corpora. To support open-source efforts, we release our code and datasets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "NEMOTRON-CC-MATH: A 133 BILLION-TOKEN-SCALE HIGH QUALITY MATH PRETRAINING DATASET":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Nemotron-CC-Math, a large-scale (133B tokens) math-focused pretraining dataset built from Common Crawl.  It addresses limitations in existing math datasets, which often suffer from degraded quality due to brittle extraction heuristics and lossy HTML-to-text conversion. The paper proposes a novel pipeline leveraging layout-aware rendering with lynx and an LLM-based cleaning stage to extract math content from diverse formats (MathJax, KaTeX, MathML), preserving the structural integrity of equations and code blocks while standardizing notation.  The authors demonstrate that pretraining an 8B model on Nemotron-CC-Math yields significant gains in math reasoning (MATH), code generation (MBPP+), and general knowledge (MMLU, MMLU-Stem) compared to models trained on existing open datasets, establishing a new state-of-the-art among open math pretraining corpora.  The dataset and code are released to support open-source research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its data extraction and cleaning pipeline for mathematical content from web-scale data.  While prior work has created math datasets from Common Crawl, this paper presents a domain-agnostic, robust pipeline using layout-aware rendering (Lynx) and LLM-based cleaning to preserve the structure of equations and code. This is a significant improvement over heuristic-based methods used in previous work (OWM, FineMath), which often fail to accurately extract or preserve mathematical content. The combination of Lynx and LLM-based cleaning is a key innovation. Furthermore, the curated and thorough approach is a clear improvement over previous attempts, which failed to address various HTML math variability and the lack of complete formatting information in many Common Crawl snapshots.
* **Significance:** The creation of a large-scale, high-quality math pretraining dataset is highly significant for advancing mathematical reasoning capabilities in LLMs. The experimental results convincingly demonstrate that Nemotron-CC-Math improves performance across various benchmarks, including math, code, and general knowledge tasks. The fact that their dataset outperforms existing open datasets, including the previously best FineMath-4+, is a substantial contribution.  The release of the dataset and code promotes reproducibility and encourages further research in this area. Moreover, the domain-agnostic nature of the extraction pipeline makes this paper valuable for building datasets in other scientific domains, highlighting its broader potential impact.
* **Strengths:**
    *   **Robust Pipeline:** The proposed extraction pipeline is a key strength, addressing the limitations of prior approaches for handling diverse mathematical notations and HTML structures.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides a thorough evaluation across a diverse set of benchmarks, demonstrating the benefits of pretraining on Nemotron-CC-Math.
    *   **Open-Source Contribution:** Releasing both the dataset and code makes this work highly valuable for the research community.
    *   **Domain-Agnostic Approach**: Makes the extraction pipeline valuable for many scientific domains.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The experiments mainly center around an 8B model. While the improvements are clear, evaluations on larger models would further strengthen the findings.
    *   The paper acknowledges that a portion of the content is scraped from datasets that had some kind of pre-decontamination already. A clearer statement about the effectiveness of prior and current decontamination procedures would be beneficial.
    *   The choice of Phi-4 as the LLM in their cleaning steps is surprising. While the experiments on this model show that smaller models are effective in boilerplate removal, the overall results are a bit conflicting; it would be nice to see some results using even larger cleaning models.
    *   While good, the numbers on some tasks, e.g., GMS8K, are not outstanding. This could indicate that more math diverse data might be needed.
*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of LLMs for scientific applications.  It sets a new standard for data quality in math pretraining and provides a practical pipeline for building similar datasets in other scientific domains. It likely leads to increased research and development in creating and utilizing specialized pretraining datasets, resulting in more capable and reliable LLMs for scientific problem-solving. The method could even be used in the creation of better data for code pretraining.
* **Score Justification**:

Given the novelty of the extraction pipeline, the significance of the resulting dataset, the comprehensive evaluation, and the open-source contribution, balanced by the limitations on model scale evaluation and prior art, the paper earns a solid score.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### LLMs and Agentic AI in Insurance Decision-Making: Opportunities and Challenges For Africa
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15110v1)
- **Authors**: Graham Hill, JingYuan Gong, Thulani Babeli, Moseli Mots'oehli, James Gachomo Wanjiku
- **Abstract**: In this work, we highlight the transformative potential of Artificial Intelligence (AI), particularly Large Language Models (LLMs) and agentic AI, in the insurance sector. We consider and emphasize the unique opportunities, challenges, and potential pathways in insurance amid rapid performance improvements, increased open-source access, decreasing deployment costs, and the complexity of LLM or agentic AI frameworks. To bring it closer to home, we identify critical gaps in the African insurance market and highlight key local efforts, players, and partnership opportunities. Finally, we call upon actuaries, insurers, regulators, and tech leaders to a collaborative effort aimed at creating inclusive, sustainable, and equitable AI strategies and solutions: by and for Africans.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "LLMs and Agentic AI in Insurance Decision-Making: Opportunities and Challenges For Africa."

**Summary:**

The paper explores the potential of Large Language Models (LLMs) and agentic AI to revolutionize the African insurance industry. It highlights the unique opportunities and challenges presented by this technology, considering the context of rapid technological advancements, open-source availability, and decreasing deployment costs. The paper identifies critical gaps in the African insurance market and emphasizes the need for collaborative efforts among actuaries, insurers, regulators, and tech leaders to develop inclusive, sustainable, and equitable AI strategies tailored to the African context. It delves into global LLM and agentic AI use cases, examines specific African AI research and initiatives, and addresses the crucial relationship between technology adoption and regulatory frameworks within the highly regulated financial markets of Africa. The authors conclude with a call to action for African stakeholders to actively participate in the development and deployment of AI-based insurance solutions, emphasizing the importance of leveraging local data and expertise.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents a relevant and timely discussion on the application of LLMs and agentic AI in the African insurance sector, a topic that has received relatively little attention in the existing literature. The paper effectively bridges the gap between global AI trends and the specific needs and challenges of the African market. The inclusion of local research efforts, startups, and established incumbents adds a valuable layer of context and realism. Furthermore, the emphasis on the interplay between technological advancements and regulatory considerations is crucial, particularly in the context of sensitive financial markets. The call for collaboration among various stakeholders is a practical and sensible recommendation.

**Strengths:**

*   **Focus on a Underexplored Area:** The primary strength of the paper lies in its focused examination of LLMs and agentic AI within the African insurance context. This is a relatively underexplored area, making the paper's insights particularly valuable.
*   **Practical Relevance:** The paper provides a practical perspective by outlining specific challenges and opportunities within the African insurance market.
*   **Comprehensive Scope:** The paper covers a wide range of relevant topics, including AI fundamentals, global use cases, African initiatives, and regulatory considerations.
*   **Emphasis on Collaboration:** The paper's call for collaboration among various stakeholders is a strong point, highlighting the importance of a multi-faceted approach to AI adoption.
*   **Clear Call to Action:** Concluding with a call for increased local participation in developing and deploying AI solutions provides a clear direction for future efforts.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Depth in Technical Analysis:** While the paper provides an overview of LLMs and agentic AI, it lacks a deeper technical analysis of specific models, algorithms, or implementation strategies.
*   **Lack of Empirical Data:** The paper would benefit from incorporating empirical data or case studies to support its claims and provide concrete examples of AI applications in African insurance.
*   **Potential for Over-Generalization:** Some statements regarding the African insurance market may be over-generalized, as the continent encompasses diverse countries with varying levels of technological development and regulatory environments.
*   **Limited Discussion on Ethical Concerns:** While the paper touches on regulatory aspects, it could delve deeper into the ethical considerations surrounding AI adoption in insurance, such as bias, fairness, and transparency.

**Novelty and Significance:**

The paper's novelty lies primarily in its application of existing AI concepts (LLMs and agentic AI) to a specific geographic and industry context (African insurance). While the individual AI technologies discussed are not groundbreaking, the paper's synthesis of these technologies with the specific needs and challenges of the African insurance market represents a novel contribution. The paper's significance stems from its potential to spark a conversation and stimulate collaborative efforts among African stakeholders to harness the benefits of AI for insurance, while addressing the ethical and regulatory implications.

The novelty is more in the application of well known technologies, rather than new discoveries. This makes the significance important for driving progress in Africa, but lower in terms of fundamentally changing the state of the art.

**Score:** 6/10

**Rationale:**

The paper is a valuable contribution to the field due to its focus on a relatively underexplored topic and its practical insights into the application of AI in the African insurance market. It identifies the gaps that must be addressed. It is well-written, comprehensive in scope, and includes a call to action. However, it lacks technical depth, empirical data, and a thorough discussion of ethical concerns, which limit its overall impact. The novelty is primarily in the application of existing technologies rather than the development of new ones. This means that, while the paper will have a tangible effect, it is more of a commentary and call to arms, rather than a fundamental step change in the AI space.

- **Classification**: cs.CE
- **Score**: 6/10

### Side Effects of Erasing Concepts from Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15124v1)
- **Authors**: Shaswati Saha, Sourajit Saha, Manas Gaur, Tejas Gokhale
- **Abstract**: Concerns about text-to-image (T2I) generative models infringing on privacy, copyright, and safety have led to the development of Concept Erasure Techniques (CETs). The goal of an effective CET is to prohibit the generation of undesired ``target'' concepts specified by the user, while preserving the ability to synthesize high-quality images of the remaining concepts. In this work, we demonstrate that CETs can be easily circumvented and present several side effects of concept erasure. For a comprehensive measurement of the robustness of CETs, we present Side Effect Evaluation (\see), an evaluation benchmark that consists of hierarchical and compositional prompts that describe objects and their attributes. This dataset and our automated evaluation pipeline quantify side effects of CETs across three aspects: impact on neighboring concepts, evasion of targets, and attribute leakage. Our experiments reveal that CETs can be circumvented by using superclass-subclass hierarchy and semantically similar prompts, such as compositional variants of the target. We show that CETs suffer from attribute leakage and counterintuitive phenomena of attention concentration or dispersal. We release our dataset, code, and evaluation tools to aid future work on robust concept erasure.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper introduces the Side Effect Evaluation (SEE) benchmark to comprehensively evaluate concept erasure techniques (CETs) in text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models.  The authors argue that existing evaluation methods are insufficient, focusing only on the accuracy of erasing the target concept and preserving others, without considering potential side effects. SEE consists of a large, hierarchical dataset of compositional prompts describing objects and their attributes, designed to assess three types of side effects: impact on neighboring concepts, evasion of erasure via superclass/subclass relationships, and attribute leakage.  The authors evaluate four state-of-the-art CETs using SEE, revealing vulnerabilities and limitations regarding the techniques’ ability to safely remove concepts without introducing unintended consequences.  The paper also analyzes attention maps, showing how they change with successful and unsuccessful erasure, and demonstrates that progressive erasure is more effective than simultaneous erasure.  The authors release the SEE dataset, code, and evaluation tools.

**Critical Evaluation**

*Novelty and Significance:*

The paper's primary contribution is the formalization and quantification of "side effects" in concept erasure. This is a crucial step because the initial focus on accuracy alone was leading to a flawed understanding of CET robustness.  Identifying and categorizing these side effects (neighboring concept impact, evasion, leakage) provides a more nuanced understanding of the problem space.  The SEE dataset itself is a valuable resource for the community.  The analysis of attention maps provides further insight into the inner workings of the models, albeit on a somewhat qualitative level.

*Strengths:*

*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The SEE benchmark provides a much more comprehensive assessment of CET performance than previous methods, moving beyond simple accuracy measurements.
*   **Well-Defined Metrics:** The paper clearly defines metrics for quantifying the three side effects, facilitating automated and reproducible evaluations.
*   **Large-Scale Experiments:** The experiments are conducted on a large scale, using a diverse set of prompts and multiple CETs, lending statistical significance to the findings.
*   **Practical Contribution:**  The release of the dataset, code, and evaluation tools empowers further research and development in this area.
*   **Attention Map Analysis**: The investigation into attention maps offers valuable insights into the internal mechanism of concept erasure.

*Weaknesses:*

*   **Limited Scope of "Concept":** The paper limits the definition of "concept" to object categories and supercategories along with attributes. This choice simplifies the problem, but other aspects, such as the artistic styles, are not tackled. The definition is acknowledged in the limitations section but could benefit from a larger exploration of potential generalization abilities.
*   **Limited Novelty in Certain Findings:** While the comprehensive evaluation is novel, some of the specific vulnerabilities discovered (e.g., evasion via subclass prompts) are intuitively obvious.  The impact of the findings would be higher if they revealed truly surprising or unexpected limitations.  The discovery that progress erasure works better than all at once may not be surprising.
*   **Dependence on Imperfect "Verifiers":** The evaluation relies on VQA and CLIP models to assess the presence/absence of concepts, a potential source of error and bias. The effectiveness of the SEE benchmark is limited by the ability of CLIP and VQA to identify a specific concept is in an image. The VQA and CLIP models are imperfect which limits the conclusion regarding "attribute leakage."
*   **Qualitative Analysis of Attention Maps:** The analysis of attention maps is primarily qualitative.  A more quantitative analysis could provide stronger support for the conclusions.
*   **Incremental Contribution**: The work feels incremental, extending the previous work.

*Significance and Potential Influence:*

The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of concept erasure by shifting the focus from simple accuracy to a more holistic evaluation framework. The SEE benchmark can serve as a standard for future CET development, driving the creation of more robust and safe techniques. The identification of specific vulnerabilities can guide future research directions.

*Justification of Score:*

While the paper has valuable contributions, particularly in its comprehensive evaluation framework, some weaknesses temper the novelty and overall significance. The results tend to show some known issues and the attention maps offer qualitative analysis only. However, the scale and rigorous evaluation conducted by the researchers should help drive the direction of research.

Score: 7

The rigor in the experiment and future potential of the findings, and availability of tools, and dataset help justify the score of 7.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### aiXiv: A Next-Generation Open Access Ecosystem for Scientific Discovery Generated by AI Scientists
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15126v1)
- **Authors**: Pengsong Zhang, Xiang Hu, Guowei Huang, Yang Qi, Heng Zhang, Xiuxu Li, Jiaxing Song, Jiabin Luo, Yijiang Li, Shuo Yin, Chengxiao Dai, Eric Hanchen Jiang, Xiaoyan Zhou, Zhenfei Yin, Boqin Yuan, Jing Dong, Guinan Su, Guanren Qiao, Haiming Tang, Anghong Du, Lili Pan, Zhenzhong Lan, Xinyu Liu
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in large language models (LLMs) have enabled AI agents to autonomously generate scientific proposals, conduct experiments, author papers, and perform peer reviews. Yet this flood of AI-generated research content collides with a fragmented and largely closed publication ecosystem. Traditional journals and conferences rely on human peer review, making them difficult to scale and often reluctant to accept AI-generated research content; existing preprint servers (e.g. arXiv) lack rigorous quality-control mechanisms. Consequently, a significant amount of high-quality AI-generated research lacks appropriate venues for dissemination, hindering its potential to advance scientific progress. To address these challenges, we introduce aiXiv, a next-generation open-access platform for human and AI scientists. Its multi-agent architecture allows research proposals and papers to be submitted, reviewed, and iteratively refined by both human and AI scientists. It also provides API and MCP interfaces that enable seamless integration of heterogeneous human and AI scientists, creating a scalable and extensible ecosystem for autonomous scientific discovery. Through extensive experiments, we demonstrate that aiXiv is a reliable and robust platform that significantly enhances the quality of AI-generated research proposals and papers after iterative revising and reviewing on aiXiv. Our work lays the groundwork for a next-generation open-access ecosystem for AI scientists, accelerating the publication and dissemination of high-quality AI-generated research content. Code is available at https://github.com/aixiv-org. Website is available at https://forms.gle/DxQgCtXFsJ4paMtn8.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper "aiXiv: A Next-Generation Open Access Ecosystem for Scientific Discovery Generated by AI Scientists":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces aiXiv, a next-generation open-access platform designed to facilitate scientific discovery by both human and AI scientists.  It addresses the limitations of current publication ecosystems, like traditional journals and preprint servers, in handling the surge of AI-generated research. aiXiv features a multi-agent architecture enabling autonomous submission, review, refinement, and publication of scientific proposals and papers. It incorporates a structured review system, iterative refinement pipelines, and safeguards against prompt-injection attacks. The platform integrates with external resources like Semantic Scholar and provides interfaces for seamless interaction between human and AI scientists.  Experiments demonstrate that the review-refine process on aiXiv significantly improves the quality of AI-generated research content.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The concept of an open-access platform specifically designed to handle AI-generated research is novel and timely. The multi-agent architecture, closed-loop review process, and prompt-injection defenses are innovative features. The paper acknowledges the existing Agents4Science conference, but differentiates itself with its focus on revision and rebuttal stages. However, it's crucial to acknowledge that the core components (LLM-based review, multi-agent systems) are individually explored elsewhere; the *integration* and application to the scientific publishing bottleneck is the key novelty.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a growing problem: the increasing volume of AI-generated research and the limited capacity of existing systems to evaluate and disseminate it effectively.  If aiXiv can be realized as described, it could have a significant impact by: (1) accelerating scientific discovery by providing a venue for AI-generated content, (2) encouraging collaboration between human and AI researchers, and (3) fostering transparency in the use of AI in scientific research.  However, the significance hinges on real-world adoption, addressing ethical considerations regarding AI-generated content, and maintaining quality standards.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Addresses a clear and pressing need in the scientific community.
    *   Proposes a comprehensive and well-structured platform architecture.
    *   Includes important safeguards (prompt injection, multiple AI reviewers) to mitigate potential risks.
    *   Provides empirical evidence showing improvement in research quality through the review process.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper remains high-level and lacks details on the implementation and scalability of the platform. The multi-agent system requires careful orchestration to avoid inefficiencies or bias.
    *   Ethical considerations, although acknowledged, are not thoroughly addressed. Concerns about hallucinated content, evaluation bias, and transparency need further elaboration.
    *   The experimental validation is limited to synthetic datasets and specific tasks (proposal writing, paper authoring). Broader validation across more domains and scientific tasks would be beneficial.
    *   Success depends heavily on the quality of the LLMs used for review and other tasks. How the platform adapts to emerging LLMs and mitigates biases is unclear.
    *   The paper does not address the long-term impact of a potentially large influx of AI-generated content on the direction and focus of scientific research.

*   **Potential Influence:**  aiXiv has the potential to be a catalyst for AI-driven scientific discovery. By facilitating access to AI-generated research, it could accelerate the pace of innovation. However, it also carries risks, such as promoting low-quality content or altering the research landscape in unforeseen ways. Its success depends on careful management and adherence to ethical guidelines.

**Score:** 7/10

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and potentially significant contribution to the scientific community by addressing the challenges posed by AI-generated research. The design includes several innovative features and safeguards that contribute to its potential impact. However, the lack of implementation details, limited experimental validation, and insufficient discussion of ethical considerations prevent a higher score. The paper's significance lies in its potential to reshape the scientific research landscape, but this potential hinges on successful implementation and adoption, which are not yet proven. The 7 reflects a combination of strong conceptual novelty, real-world relevance, and the risks and uncertainties related to its implementation and long-term impact. A higher score would require a more detailed implementation and more in-depth discussion of ethical considerations and long-term societal impacts.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Identifying and Answering Questions with False Assumptions: An Interpretable Approach
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15139v1)
- **Authors**: Zijie Wang, Eduardo Blanco
- **Abstract**: People often ask questions with false assumptions, a type of question that does not have regular answers. Answering such questions require first identifying the false assumptions. Large Language Models (LLMs) often generate misleading answers because of hallucinations. In this paper, we focus on identifying and answering questions with false assumptions in several domains. We first investigate to reduce the problem to fact verification. Then, we present an approach leveraging external evidence to mitigate hallucinations. Experiments with five LLMs demonstrate that (1) incorporating retrieved evidence is beneficial and (2) generating and validating atomic assumptions yields more improvements and provides an interpretable answer by specifying the false assumptions.
- **Summary**: This paper addresses the challenging task of identifying and answering questions with false assumptions. The authors propose an approach that leverages external evidence retrieval and the generation/validation of atomic assumptions to provide more interpretable answers. The core idea is to break down the identification of false assumptions into smaller, verifiable components. Experiments are conducted on three existing datasets, and the results demonstrate improvements over previous approaches. The authors also perform error analysis to identify limitations of their method and provide insights for future research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its approach to answering questions with false assumptions by generating and validating *atomic* assumptions. While leveraging external knowledge and fact verification is not entirely new, applying it specifically to the decomposition and validation of question assumptions is a unique angle. Previous works focused on end-to-end generation.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a real-world problem: questions with false assumptions often lead to misleading or incorrect answers from LLMs. By explicitly identifying the false assumptions, the proposed method offers more accurate and interpretable answers. This improves the reliability and trustworthiness of LLM outputs. The experimental results demonstrate state-of-the-art performance on three benchmark datasets, indicating practical significance. The method's ability to provide human-readable interpretations and specific false assumptions gives the answers more utility and context than a simple yes/no.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The method's interpretable nature is a significant advantage, providing users with insight into why an answer is incorrect.
    *   The comprehensive experimental evaluation across multiple datasets strengthens the findings.
    *   The error analysis identifies areas for improvement, guiding future research.
    *   The breakdown of questions into atomic assumptions is simple yet elegant and effective.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The approach relies heavily on the quality of external evidence retrieval. If the retrieved evidence is irrelevant or incomplete, the results may suffer.
    *   The method depends on LLMs (primarily GPT-4) for generating and validating assumptions. While LLMs are powerful, they are also prone to biases and hallucinations. The use of smaller models is promising but still behind.
    *   The performance varies depending on the model used to generate the atomic assumptions.
    *   Computational cost is a concern, particularly when using large LLMs and retrieving evidence. The cost-benefit analysis is useful, but the cost is still greater than other approaches.
    *   The evaluation of the *generated interpretations* relies on the judgment of a single annotator, which poses questions about consistency.

**Justification:**

The paper introduces a novel approach to a challenging problem. The experimental results and the interpretable nature of the answers justify a positive assessment. However, the reliance on external evidence and LLMs, as well as the computational cost, limit its potential. The impact of this paper lies in its potential to improve the accuracy and trustworthiness of question answering systems by addressing questions with false assumptions, and also by explaining *why* such answers are incorrect. Further research to improve evidence retrieval and reduce computational costs can elevate the significance of this work.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### QueryGenie: Making LLM-Based Database Querying Transparent and Controllable
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15146v1)
- **Authors**: Longfei Chen, Shenghan Gao, Shiwei Wang, Ken Lin, Yun Wang, Quan Li
- **Abstract**: Conversational user interfaces powered by large language models (LLMs) have significantly lowered the technical barriers to database querying. However, existing tools still encounter several challenges, such as misinterpretation of user intent, generation of hallucinated content, and the absence of effective mechanisms for human feedback-all of which undermine their reliability and practical utility. To address these issues and promote a more transparent and controllable querying experience, we proposed QueryGenie, an interactive system that enables users to monitor, understand, and guide the LLM-driven query generation process. Through incremental reasoning, real-time validation, and responsive interaction mechanisms, users can iteratively refine query logic and ensure alignment with their intent.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces QueryGenie, an interactive system designed to enhance the transparency and controllability of LLM-based database querying.  The system addresses issues such as misinterpretation of user intent and generation of hallucinated content by providing an interface that allows users to monitor, understand, and guide the LLM-driven query generation process. QueryGenie operates in three stages: Intention Confirmation, Query Generation (using Chain-of-Thought), and Query Validation. It incorporates features like visual mappings between natural language and database schema, real-time SQL execution with feedback, and a hierarchical tree-based visualization of query logic. A preliminary user study suggests that QueryGenie improves query accuracy compared to existing tools like Vanna, although it may require more time per task.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its focus on *user interaction* to improve the reliability and transparency of LLM-based database querying, rather than solely relying on improving the LLM itself. The integration of incremental reasoning, real-time validation, and responsive interaction is a valuable contribution. The visualizations, such as the tree-based structure for query logic and color coding for SQL code, show promise in improving user understanding. However, components like the database panel are not completely new, but the integration of all the aspects into one tool is novel.

*   **Significance:** Making LLM-based querying more reliable and transparent is crucial for its wider adoption. The ability for users to actively participate in the query generation process, understand the underlying logic, and correct errors is a significant step towards building trust in these systems. The user study, although preliminary, supports the claim that this interactive approach leads to more accurate queries. The system makes an important step in empowering the user and giving them control over an increasingly powerful technology.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Addresses a critical problem: lack of transparency and controllability in LLM-based querying.
    *   User-centric design: The focus on interactive elements and visualizations to improve user understanding is valuable.
    *   Integrates multiple techniques effectively: Chain-of-Thought, real-time validation, and schema linking are combined in a cohesive system.
    *   Preliminary user study provides initial evidence of effectiveness.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Limited user study: The study involved only 12 participants and a specific set of tasks. A more extensive evaluation with diverse users and real-world datasets is needed.
    *   Potential for user fatigue: The interactive nature of the system may become tedious for complex queries if the LLM consistently makes errors.
    *   Efficiency trade-off: While accuracy improves, task completion time increases. Further optimization is needed to strike a better balance.
    *   The study only compares QueryGenie to one other system (Vanna). Comparing to other tools would be beneficial.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper can influence the design of future LLM-based data querying systems by highlighting the importance of user interaction and transparency.  It provides a valuable framework for incorporating these elements into existing and new tools. Other researchers and developers could learn from the three-stage framework.

*   **Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel approach to a significant problem, with a well-designed system and promising initial results. The focus on interactive elements to improve transparency and accuracy is a valuable contribution. The weaknesses identified primarily relate to the limited scope of the evaluation and the potential for user fatigue. However, the core idea is strong and has the potential to significantly impact the field.

- **Score**: 7/10

### Zero-shot Volumetric CT Super-Resolution using 3D Gaussian Splatting with Upsampled 2D X-ray Projection Priors
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15151v1)
- **Authors**: Jeonghyun Noh, Hyun-Jic Oh, Byungju Chae, Won-Ki Jeong
- **Abstract**: Computed tomography (CT) is widely used in clinical diagnosis, but acquiring high-resolution (HR) CT is limited by radiation exposure risks. Deep learning-based super-resolution (SR) methods have been studied to reconstruct HR from low-resolution (LR) inputs. While supervised SR approaches have shown promising results, they require large-scale paired LR-HR volume datasets that are often unavailable. In contrast, zero-shot methods alleviate the need for paired data by using only a single LR input, but typically struggle to recover fine anatomical details due to limited internal information. To overcome these, we propose a novel zero-shot 3D CT SR framework that leverages upsampled 2D X-ray projection priors generated by a diffusion model. Exploiting the abundance of HR 2D X-ray data, we train a diffusion model on large-scale 2D X-ray projection and introduce a per-projection adaptive sampling strategy. It selects the generative process for each projection, thus providing HR projections as strong external priors for 3D CT reconstruction. These projections serve as inputs to 3D Gaussian splatting for reconstructing a 3D CT volume. Furthermore, we propose negative alpha blending (NAB-GS) that allows negative values in Gaussian density representation. NAB-GS enables residual learning between LR and diffusion-based projections, thereby enhancing high-frequency structure reconstruction. Experiments on two datasets show that our method achieves superior quantitative and qualitative results for 3D CT SR.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a novel zero-shot super-resolution (SR) framework for 3D Computed Tomography (CT) volumes.  The key idea is to leverage the relative abundance of high-resolution 2D X-ray projection images to enhance the SR of 3D CT scans. The approach involves: 1) training a diffusion model on a large set of 2D X-ray projections; 2) using this diffusion model to upsample 2D X-ray projections derived from the low-resolution 3D CT volume, employing a per-projection adaptive sampling strategy (PAS) to mitigate artifacts; and 3) reconstructing the 3D CT volume from the upsampled 2D projections using 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS). A negative alpha blending extension to 3DGS (NAB-GS) is introduced to allow for residual learning between the upsampled projections and the original low-resolution data, thus capturing high-frequency details. The method is evaluated on two public datasets, demonstrating superior quantitative and qualitative performance compared to existing zero-shot and supervised SR approaches.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper combines several existing techniques in a novel way to address a significant problem (CT super-resolution with limited paired data). The core novelty lies in using 2D X-ray projections as external priors to guide 3D CT super-resolution in a zero-shot setting. The adaptive sampling strategy and the negative alpha blending Gaussian splatting are also notable contributions. Previous zero-shot methods for CT SR have struggled to recover fine details due to reliance on internal information alone, making the external 2D projection prior a valuable innovation.

*   **Significance:** CT super-resolution has important clinical implications, potentially allowing for higher-quality diagnostics with reduced radiation exposure. Zero-shot approaches are particularly valuable in the medical domain where large, paired LR-HR datasets are difficult and costly to acquire. The proposed framework offers a practical solution by leveraging the availability of 2D X-ray data, making it potentially attractive for clinical translation. The method improves the performance compared to the existing zero-shot methods like CuNeRF.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The use of abundant 2D X-ray data as external priors is a clever and effective strategy.
    *   The adaptive sampling strategy (PAS) is essential for handling the artifacts introduced by the diffusion model.
    *   The NAB-GS extends 3DGS to enable residual learning, contributing to enhanced high-frequency detail recovery.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate superior performance on multiple datasets compared to existing methods.
    *   The method addresses a critical problem in medical imaging with practical applications.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The method relies on a pre-trained diffusion model. Performance will heavily rely on the quality and domain adaptation ability of that model. While leveraging pre-trained models can be a strength, dependence on them can also limit the adaptability of the method.
    *   The sensitivity to the negative slope hyperparameter (γ) necessitates careful tuning for different datasets and resolutions. A more robust and adaptive mechanism for setting γ would improve the practical applicability of the approach.
    *   The paper would benefit from a more detailed discussion of the limitations of the diffusion model used for 2D X-ray upsampling and potential solutions to address them. While the method avoids the need for paired 3D data, it still relies on a large dataset for 2D diffusion model training.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence research in medical image super-resolution and reconstruction.  It could inspire further work on incorporating external priors into zero-shot SR methods and on improving the robustness and efficiency of 3DGS-based medical volume reconstruction. The NAB-GS component could have broader applications in 3D rendering tasks. The use of adaptive sampling in conjunction with diffusion models to mitigate artifacts may be of broader interest beyond the specific application in this paper.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a clearly articulated and technically sound approach to zero-shot CT super-resolution. The method exhibits substantial improvements over existing zero-shot methods. The method offers a practical solution to the challenge of CT SR by leveraging available 2D X-ray data and introducing novel techniques for 3D reconstruction and diffusion model integration. While there are some limitations with hyperparameter tuning and reliance on a pre-trained diffusion model, the overall contribution is significant and promising for clinical translation. The potential impact on CT SR and medical imaging reconstruction makes it a valuable addition to the field.

- **Classification**: eess.IV
- **Score**: 7/10

### ContextualLVLM-Agent: A Holistic Framework for Multi-Turn Visually-Grounded Dialogue and Complex Instruction Following
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15164v1)
- **Authors**: Seungmin Han, Haeun Kwon, Ji-jun Park, Taeyang Yoon
- **Abstract**: Despite significant advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs) and Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs), current models still face substantial challenges in handling complex, multi-turn, and visually-grounded tasks that demand deep reasoning, sustained contextual understanding, entity tracking, and multi-step instruction following. Existing benchmarks often fall short in capturing the dynamism and intricacies of real-world multi-modal interactions, leading to issues such as context loss and visual hallucinations. To address these limitations, we introduce MMDR-Bench (Multi-Modal Dialogue Reasoning Benchmark), a novel dataset comprising 300 meticulously designed complex multi-turn dialogue scenarios, each averaging 5-7 turns and evaluated across six core dimensions including visual entity tracking and reasoning depth. Furthermore, we propose CoLVLM Agent (Contextual LVLM Agent), a holistic framework that enhances existing LVLMs with advanced reasoning and instruction following capabilities through an iterative "memory-perception-planning-execution" cycle, requiring no extensive re-training of the underlying models. Our extensive experiments on MMDR-Bench demonstrate that CoLVLM Agent consistently achieves superior performance, attaining an average human evaluation score of 4.03, notably surpassing state-of-the-art commercial models like GPT-4o (3.92) and Gemini 1.5 Pro (3.85). The framework exhibits significant advantages in reasoning depth, instruction adherence, and error suppression, and maintains robust performance over extended dialogue turns, validating the effectiveness of its modular design and iterative approach for complex multi-modal interactions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ContextualLVLM-Agent (CoLVLM Agent), a framework designed to enhance Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) for complex, multi-turn, visually-grounded dialogue and instruction following. It addresses limitations of current models in maintaining context, tracking entities, and executing multi-step instructions. The CoLVLM Agent employs an iterative "memory-perception-planning-execution" cycle without requiring extensive re-training of underlying LVLMs.  To evaluate the framework, the authors introduce MMDR-Bench (Multi-Modal Dialogue Reasoning Benchmark), a new dataset comprising 300 meticulously designed complex multi-turn dialogue scenarios. Experimental results on MMDR-Bench demonstrate that CoLVLM Agent outperforms state-of-the-art commercial and open-source LVLMs in key areas like reasoning depth, instruction adherence, and error suppression. The paper includes ablation studies to validate the contribution of each component of the proposed framework.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the architecture of the CoLVLM Agent and the creation of the MMDR-Bench dataset. The "memory-perception-planning-execution" cycle is conceptually sound and mirrors human cognitive processes.  The MMDR-Bench fills a significant gap in the existing benchmarks by providing a challenging evaluation for multi-turn, visually grounded dialogues. However, the modular components (memory module, perception module, etc.) are somewhat standard components utilized in these architectures. The dynamic visual attention and tool integration, though, are key improvements. The iterative nature also offers improvements for self-correction.

*   **Significance:** The paper is significant because it directly addresses a recognized weakness in current LVLMs: handling complex, interactive dialogues. The MMDR-Bench dataset will likely become a valuable resource for researchers working on multi-modal dialogue systems.  The CoLVLM Agent framework provides a practical approach for improving existing LVLMs without requiring extensive re-training, which is a major advantage. The experimental results convincingly demonstrate the superiority of the CoLVLM Agent over existing state-of-the-art models. This performance improvement, along with an analysis to error reduction, highlight several benefits. Furthermore, by integrating the visual information, tracking, and reasoning modules it showcases effective system design.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The MMDR-Bench is a valuable contribution that addresses limitations in existing datasets.
    *   The CoLVLM Agent framework is well-designed and modular, making it relatively easy to integrate with existing LVLMs.
    *   The experimental results are thorough and convincing, demonstrating the effectiveness of the framework.
    *   The ablation studies provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component.
    *   The self-correction mechanism is a valuable addition for robustness.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The components of the CoLVLM Agent (memory, perception, planning, execution) are individually not entirely novel. The value stems from their integration and iterative process.
    *   The paper does not extensively explore the computational efficiency of the CoLVLM Agent, beyond a simple latency measure. A more detailed analysis of the computational cost of each component would be beneficial.
    *   The paper could benefit from a deeper exploration of the types of errors that the CoLVLM Agent is still unable to correct. This would provide insights into potential areas for future research.

*   **Potential Influence:** The MMDR-Bench dataset is likely to become a standard benchmark for evaluating multi-modal dialogue systems. The CoLVLM Agent framework could inspire other researchers to explore modular architectures and iterative approaches for improving LVLMs. The findings are also useful for creating human-like agents.

**Justification:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of LVLMs by addressing the critical challenge of handling complex, multi-turn dialogue. The novelty lies primarily in the specific architecture of the CoLVLM Agent and the creation of the MMDR-Bench dataset. The significance stems from the practical benefits of the framework (improving existing LVLMs without extensive re-training) and the potential impact of the dataset on future research. While the individual components of the CoLVLM Agent are not entirely novel, their integration into an iterative "memory-perception-planning-execution" cycle is a significant advancement. The thorough experimental results and ablation studies provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of the framework. A good balance of the system design with a strong methodology is achieved. The error analysis and demonstration of robustness, furthermore, strengthens the paper.
