### MeSS: City Mesh-Guided Outdoor Scene Generation with Cross-View Consistent Diffusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15169v1)
- **Authors**: Xuyang Chen, Zhijun Zhai, Kaixuan Zhou, Zengmao Wang, Jianan He, Dong Wang, Yanfeng Zhang, mingwei Sun, Rüdiger Westermann, Konrad Schindler, Liqiu Meng
- **Abstract**: Mesh models have become increasingly accessible for numerous cities; however, the lack of realistic textures restricts their application in virtual urban navigation and autonomous driving. To address this, this paper proposes MeSS (Meshbased Scene Synthesis) for generating high-quality, styleconsistent outdoor scenes with city mesh models serving as the geometric prior. While image and video diffusion models can leverage spatial layouts (such as depth maps or HD maps) as control conditions to generate street-level perspective views, they are not directly applicable to 3D scene generation. Video diffusion models excel at synthesizing consistent view sequences that depict scenes but often struggle to adhere to predefined camera paths or align accurately with rendered control videos. In contrast, image diffusion models, though unable to guarantee cross-view visual consistency, can produce more geometry-aligned results when combined with ControlNet. Building on this insight, our approach enhances image diffusion models by improving cross-view consistency. The pipeline comprises three key stages: first, we generate geometrically consistent sparse views using Cascaded Outpainting ControlNets; second, we propagate denser intermediate views via a component dubbed AGInpaint; and third, we globally eliminate visual inconsistencies (e.g., varying exposure) using the GCAlign module. Concurrently with generation, a 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) scene is reconstructed by initializing Gaussian balls on the mesh surface. Our method outperforms existing approaches in both geometric alignment and generation quality. Once synthesized, the scene can be rendered in diverse styles through relighting and style transfer techniques.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MeSS, a novel pipeline for generating high-quality, style-consistent outdoor scenes from city mesh models. MeSS uses a cascade of ControlNets for sparse keyframe generation, appearance-guided inpainting (AGInpaint) to densify the scene, and a global consistency alignment (GCA) module to eliminate visual inconsistencies.  It initializes a 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) scene based on the mesh geometry and then optimizes it using generated views. The system aims to address the limitations of existing methods that struggle with geometric accuracy, cross-view consistency, and the inability to directly texture complex city-scale meshes. It uses the geometric information from the meshes as a prior for image generation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the combination of several existing techniques into a coherent pipeline specifically designed for city-scale mesh texturing. Key aspects of its novelty are:
    *   The cascaded ControlNet approach, conditioned on preceding frames for consistent keyframe generation.
    *   The Appearance-Guided Inpainting (AGInpaint) module to address silhouettes and improve intermediate view synthesis.
    *   The Global Consistency Alignment (GCA) for reducing visual artifacts and ensuring overall scene coherence.
    *   Adapting the 3DGS to leverage the generated views and optimizing the scene representation.

    While each of these components builds on prior work (e.g., ControlNets, latent diffusion, 3DGS), their integration and adaptation to the specific problem of city mesh texturing represent a meaningful contribution. A potential weakness in its novelty is the reliance on existing frameworks; it's more of an *integrative* innovation rather than a completely ground-breaking one.

*   **Significance:**
    *   The problem MeSS addresses is significant. Realistically texturing city-scale meshes has several practical applications in VR, autonomous driving simulation, and urban planning.
    *   The method offers a cost-effective and customizable alternative to expensive and inflexible real-world data collection.
    *   The experimental results, while based on a simulated dataset, demonstrate a clear improvement over existing methods in terms of both geometric accuracy and visual quality, particularly regarding long-range consistency.
    *   The ablation studies effectively isolate the contributions of each component of the pipeline.
    *   A potential limitation is the reliance on high-quality city meshes as input. The method's effectiveness would depend on the availability and accuracy of such data.
    *   Another weakness stems from training and evaluating on synthetically rendered data. While this is common, the leap to real-world performance isn't guaranteed. Issues like domain gaps, handling occlusions, and lighting variations in real imagery could pose significant challenges.
    *   The comparison to prior work on perpetual view generation and video synthesis, especially when these methods are adapted with improved depth estimation or are trained on substantially larger datasets, weakens the claims about substantial improvement.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem statement and well-defined approach.
    *   The modular pipeline design is a strong feature that enables the isolation and improvement of components.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation, including quantitative metrics and qualitative comparisons.
    *   Well-written and clearly presented paper.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Reliance on synthetic data for training and evaluation raises concerns about real-world applicability.
    *   Limited exploration of stylistic variations and customization beyond the default City Sample style.
    *   The comparisons to prior work need to be more rigorous given the adaptations included in the compared approaches, and the fact that these adaptations substantially improved the compared algorithms.
    *   Lack of generalizability analysis; experiments should've included more diverse settings.

* **Potential Influence:** If the approach proves robust in real-world settings, it could become a standard technique for enriching city mesh models, thereby facilitating a range of applications. The modular design might also inspire further research on improving individual components, such as the AGInpaint module or the global consistency alignment.

**Score:** 7

**Rationale:**

A score of 7 reflects the paper's solid contribution to the field, acknowledging both its strengths and weaknesses. The paper addresses a practical problem with a well-engineered solution, demonstrating clear improvements over prior methods. The modularity and clear design are commendable. However, the heavy reliance on synthetic data and the incremental nature of the innovation compared to a few specific prior works (the comparisons being made unfair through modifications) temper the assessment of its overall impact. A higher score would be warranted with evidence of real-world robustness and further exploration of stylistic control.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### PuzzleClone: An SMT-Powered Framework for Synthesizing Verifiable Data
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15180v1)
- **Authors**: Kai Xiong, Yanwei Huang, Rongjunchen Zhang, Kun Chen, Haipang Wu
- **Abstract**: High-quality mathematical and logical datasets with verifiable answers are essential for strengthening the reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs). While recent data augmentation techniques have facilitated the creation of large-scale benchmarks, existing LLM-generated datasets often suffer from limited reliability, diversity, and scalability. To address these challenges, we introduce PuzzleClone, a formal framework for synthesizing verifiable data at scale using Satisfiability Modulo Theories (SMT). Our approach features three key innovations: (1) encoding seed puzzles into structured logical specifications, (2) generating scalable variants through systematic variable and constraint randomization, and (3) ensuring validity via a reproduction mechanism. Applying PuzzleClone, we construct a curated benchmark comprising over 83K diverse and programmatically validated puzzles. The generated puzzles span a wide spectrum of difficulty and formats, posing significant challenges to current state-of-the-art models. We conduct post training (SFT and RL) on PuzzleClone datasets. Experimental results show that training on PuzzleClone yields substantial improvements not only on PuzzleClone testset but also on logic and mathematical benchmarks. Post training raises PuzzleClone average from 14.4 to 56.2 and delivers consistent improvements across 7 logic and mathematical benchmarks up to 12.5 absolute percentage points (AMC2023 from 52.5 to 65.0). Our code and data are available at https://github.com/puzzleclone.
- **Summary**: Here is a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper "PuzzleClone: An SMT-Powered Framework for Synthesizing Verifiable Data":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PuzzleClone, a framework for synthesizing mathematical and logical datasets using Satisfiability Modulo Theories (SMT).  It addresses limitations in existing LLM-generated datasets, such as limited reliability, diversity, and scalability. The framework encodes seed puzzles into logical specifications, generates variants through variable and constraint randomization, and ensures validity via a reproduction mechanism.  The authors create a benchmark of over 83K puzzles using PuzzleClone and demonstrate that post-training on this dataset significantly improves LLM performance on both PuzzleClone and other logic/mathematical benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using SMT solvers for generating verifiable datasets is not entirely new, but PuzzleClone's specific approach has several novel aspects:
    *   **Structured DSL:** The design of a domain-specific language for encoding puzzles, capturing both logic and data augmentation constraints, is a significant contribution. This level of structured control is generally lacking in LLM-based data augmentation.
    *   **Reproduction Mechanism:** The validation step, where the original seed puzzle can be reproduced from the DSL specification, provides a strong guarantee of data integrity, a key advantage over LLM-based validation.
    *   **Scalability & Automation:** The framework's reliance on SMT solvers and deterministic processes rather than LLMs for the core data generation allows for much greater scalability compared to existing approaches.
*   **Significance:** The research addresses a crucial challenge in the field of LLMs: the scarcity of high-quality, reliable reasoning datasets. The PuzzleClone benchmark, with its focus on verifiable data and controlled diversity, fills an important gap.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong Technical Foundation:** The framework is well-engineered, leveraging SMT solvers and a custom DSL to achieve verifiable data generation.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The authors conduct extensive experiments, evaluating a wide range of models and benchmarks. The results clearly demonstrate the effectiveness of post-training on PuzzleClone.
    *   **Focus on Verifiability:**  This is a major strength; verifiable data is of immense importance to building trust and understanding of LLM reasoning.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Puzzle Scope:** The framework is primarily focused on SMT-solvable puzzles, restricting the diversity of problems it can generate. While this is acceptable as a start, expanding the puzzle set to include more non-SMT-solvable problems that require general understanding of logical reasoning could improve its use.
    *   **Difficulty Distribution Bias:** As acknowledged by the authors, the generated puzzles tend to cluster in the easy-to-medium range. This limits the dataset's ability to challenge and improve LLM reasoning capabilities at the higher end of the difficulty spectrum.
    *   **Dependency on Seed Puzzles:**  The system is limited to variations of the seed puzzles. While the DSL allows for substantial variation, there is an inherent limit in the kinds of problems that can be generated based on the initial seeds. The seed puzzles need to be comprehensive.
    *   **Manual Encoding Effort:**  Encoding the seed puzzles into the DSL specification requires manual effort, which can be a bottleneck in scaling the dataset.
*   **Potential Influence:** The PuzzleClone framework has the potential to significantly impact the field by providing a reliable and scalable approach to generating reasoning datasets. It can be used to:
    *   Train LLMs for improved logical and mathematical reasoning.
    *   Evaluate and benchmark LLMs on challenging and diverse puzzle sets.
    *   Investigate the reasoning capabilities of LLMs and identify areas for improvement.
*   **Improvement suggestions:** The paper could be improved by:
    *   Exploring techniques for generating harder puzzles within the framework.
    *   Developing methods for automating the encoding of seed puzzles into the DSL.
    *   Investigating ways to expand the puzzle space beyond SMT-solvable problems.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

PuzzleClone offers a novel and significant contribution to the field of LLM reasoning by providing a framework for generating verifiable and scalable datasets. The strengths of the technical foundation and the focus on verifiability are substantial. The limitations in puzzle scope and difficulty distribution hold it back from achieving a higher score. Expanding its capabilities and addressing the acknowledged weaknesses would increase its impact and solidify its position as a valuable tool for the LLM community. While it is not a paradigm shift, it is a well-executed advancement with considerable practical value.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### SafeLLM: Unlearning Harmful Outputs from Large Language Models against Jailbreak Attacks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15182v1)
- **Authors**: Xiangman Li, Xiaodong Wu, Qi Li, Jianbing Ni, Rongxing Lu
- **Abstract**: Jailbreak attacks pose a serious threat to the safety of Large Language Models (LLMs) by crafting adversarial prompts that bypass alignment mechanisms, causing the models to produce harmful, restricted, or biased content. In this paper, we propose SafeLLM, a novel unlearning-based defense framework that unlearn the harmful knowledge from LLMs while preserving linguistic fluency and general capabilities. SafeLLM employs a three-stage pipeline: (1) dynamic unsafe output detection using a hybrid approach that integrates external classifiers with model-internal evaluations; (2) token-level harmful content tracing through feedforward network (FFN) activations to localize harmful knowledge; and (3) constrained optimization to suppress unsafe behavior without degrading overall model quality. SafeLLM achieves targeted and irreversible forgetting by identifying and neutralizing FFN substructures responsible for harmful generation pathways. Extensive experiments on prominent LLMs (Vicuna, LLaMA, and GPT-J) across multiple jailbreak benchmarks show that SafeLLM substantially reduces attack success rates while maintaining high general-purpose performance. Compared to standard defense methods such as supervised fine-tuning and direct preference optimization, SafeLLM offers stronger safety guarantees, more precise control over harmful behavior, and greater robustness to unseen attacks. Moreover, SafeLLM maintains the general performance after the harmful knowledge unlearned. These results highlight unlearning as a promising direction for scalable and effective LLM safety.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SafeLLM, a novel framework designed to mitigate jailbreak attacks on Large Language Models (LLMs) by unlearning harmful outputs. The framework dynamically detects unsafe outputs using a hybrid approach combining external classifiers and internal model evaluations. It then traces harmful content at the token level using feedforward network (FFN) activations to locate the knowledge responsible for harmful generation. Finally, it uses constrained optimization to suppress unsafe behavior without degrading overall model quality. The paper presents experimental results demonstrating that SafeLLM reduces attack success rates on various jailbreak benchmarks while maintaining general-purpose performance. It also compares SafeLLM against standard defense methods, like supervised fine-tuning and direct preference optimization, claiming stronger safety guarantees, precise control, and robustness to unseen attacks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The concept of unlearning in LLMs to combat jailbreak attacks isn't entirely new; the paper cites other work in this area. The novelty lies in the specific three-stage pipeline: (1) the *dynamic* combination of external and internal classifiers, (2) *token-level* harmful content tracing through FFN activations, and (3) *constrained optimization* to balance safety and performance. The *token-level analysis* for unlearning does show some degree of innovation.
*   **Significance:** Jailbreak attacks pose a real and growing threat to LLM safety and reliability. Effective defenses are critical for deploying LLMs in sensitive applications. SafeLLM's approach, if proven robust and scalable, could contribute significantly to improving LLM security and trustworthiness.  The claim of achieving irreversible forgetting, even against adversarial prompts, is a substantial claim that warrants serious investigation.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The combination of dynamic detection, token-level tracing, and constrained optimization creates a potentially powerful framework.
    *   The token-level analysis provides a more fine-grained approach to unlearning than simply removing entire datasets or fine-tuning with safe data.
    *   The experimental results seem promising, showing reductions in attack success rates while maintaining general performance.
    *   The comparison to other defense methods highlights the potential advantages of SafeLLM's approach.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Complexity:** The framework appears complex to implement, potentially limiting its adoption. The paper does not adequately detail the computational overhead of its tracing and optimization steps.
    *   **Scalability:** The experimental results are based on relatively small models.  The claims of scalability need to be validated on larger, more modern LLMs (e.g., models with hundreds of billions of parameters) and across diverse modalities.
    *   **Black-box assumption:** While the paper suggests defense without needing internal weights access, how the method will be adapted to the black-box environment still needs to be addressed.
    *   **Lack of extensive testing:** The experimental setup focuses on *known* attacks used during training. The evaluation of *unseen* or evolving attack strategies requires much deeper examination. A significant challenge for all security research is demonstrating robustness to attacks that are not yet developed.
    *   **Overstated claims:** The claims regarding irreversibility, fine-grained control, and greater robustness seem a bit strong based on the current evidence. The framework, while promising, likely has limitations that are not adequately explored.
    *   **PPL metric concerns:** The paper claims lower perplexity (PPL) on harmful knowledge, implying successful unlearning. However, lower PPL could also simply mean the model is less likely to use harmful language, even if it retains the underlying concepts. This distinction is crucial but isn't thoroughly investigated.

*   **Potential Influence:** If the claims of irreversibility and robustness are validated and the scalability issues addressed, SafeLLM could influence the direction of LLM security research.  The integration of internal and external evaluations, as well as the token-level analysis, provides a valuable framework that other researchers could build upon.

**Justification of Score:**

While the paper presents a potentially valuable contribution, several limitations temper its overall significance.  The complexity of the framework, the lack of extensive testing on diverse attack strategies, and the potential for overstated claims warrant a cautious evaluation. The biggest weakness is the lack of a deeper investigation of how the model can perform in a black-box scenario. The novelty lies in the combination of existing techniques and the focus on token-level tracing. The lack of evaluation in a black box scenario also undermines the significance.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### SemToken: Semantic-Aware Tokenization for Efficient Long-Context Language Modeling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15190v1)
- **Authors**: Dong Liu, Yanxuan Yu
- **Abstract**: Tokenization plays a critical role in language modeling, yet existing approaches such as Byte-Pair Encoding (BPE) or WordPiece operate purely on frequency statistics, ignoring the underlying semantic structure of text. This leads to over-tokenization of semantically redundant spans and underutilization of contextual coherence, particularly in long-context scenarios. In this work, we propose \textbf{SemToken}, a semantic-aware tokenization framework that jointly reduces token redundancy and improves computation efficiency. SemToken first extracts contextual semantic embeddings via lightweight encoders and performs local semantic clustering to merge semantically equivalent tokens. Then, it allocates heterogeneous token granularity based on semantic density, allowing finer-grained tokenization in content-rich regions and coarser compression in repetitive or low-entropy spans. SemToken can be seamlessly integrated with modern language models and attention acceleration methods. Experiments on long-context language modeling benchmarks such as WikiText-103 and LongBench show that SemToken achieves up to $2.4\times$ reduction in token count and $1.9\times$ speedup, with negligible or no degradation in perplexity and downstream accuracy. Our findings suggest that semantic structure offers a promising new axis for optimizing tokenization and computation in large language models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "SemToken" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SemToken, a novel semantic-aware tokenization framework for improving the efficiency of long-context language models.  Unlike traditional tokenization methods like BPE or WordPiece that rely solely on frequency statistics, SemToken incorporates semantic understanding to dynamically adjust token granularity. It works in two main stages: first, it uses lightweight encoders to generate contextual embeddings and clusters semantically equivalent tokens for merging. Second, it estimates semantic density per span, allocating finer-grained tokens to content-rich regions and coarser-grained tokens to repetitive or low-entropy areas. The authors demonstrate that SemToken can significantly reduce token count and inference latency with minimal or no degradation in perplexity or downstream accuracy, even showing improvements in some cases.  The paper emphasizes SemToken's model-agnostic nature and its compatibility with other optimization techniques like FlashAttention and memory-aware caching.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of incorporating semantic awareness into tokenization is a compelling and relatively novel approach. While previous work has explored semantic compression or adaptive granularity in related fields (summarization, video processing), SemToken brings these concepts specifically to the *tokenization stage* of long-context language modeling. This is a key distinction. The method's two-stage process (semantic clustering + density-based granularity) is a logical and well-articulated design.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in addressing the computational bottleneck created by long contexts in LLMs. By intelligently reducing token count *before* the attention mechanism, SemToken offers a complementary approach to existing attention acceleration techniques. The empirical results demonstrate substantial gains in efficiency (token reduction, speedup, memory savings) while maintaining or improving model quality.  This has direct implications for the scalability and deployment of LLMs in resource-constrained environments.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies and quantifies the redundancy problem in traditional tokenization for long contexts.
    *   **Well-Defined Method:**  The SemToken framework is well-defined and theoretically motivated.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:**  The authors conduct thorough experiments across diverse tasks, models, and benchmarks. The ablation studies and visualizations provide valuable insights into the contribution of each component and the behavior of the system. The compatibility with FlashAttention and memory-aware caching is a significant positive.
    *   **Model Agnosticity:** The plug-and-play design of the method is highly beneficial because the model doesn't need to be retrained to work with SemToken.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Encoder Overhead:** While the paper emphasizes the lightweight nature of the encoders, the additional computational cost of generating contextual embeddings should be considered. While efficient models like SimCSE are used, they still introduce some overhead compared to purely statistical methods. The paper could include a more explicit discussion of this trade-off, and potentially benchmark alternative encoders to explore this cost further.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:**  The performance of SemToken likely depends on the careful tuning of hyperparameters (similarity threshold, entropy threshold, budget). The paper doesn't fully explore the sensitivity to these parameters.
    *   **Scalability to extremely long contexts:** While the results presented are promising, the paper lacks extensive experiments on contexts exceeding 64k or 1M tokens, leaving uncertainty about SemToken's effectiveness in such scenarios.

*   **Impact:** SemToken has the potential to influence the design of future tokenization schemes and long-context LLM systems. Its semantic-aware approach offers a promising direction for optimizing both efficiency and model performance. The method can potentially be extended to other tasks such as code understanding or machine translation. Further, it will influence future researches in integrating retrieval-augmented generation and reinforcement learning pipelines.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** It is critical to consider the computational cost of SemToken and also its sensitivity to its parameters as factors that could affect the adoption of the method. However, the extensive experimental validation across diverse tasks, model architectures and benchmarks combined with the plug-and-play design of the method indicates its potential as a fundamental element for improving the efficiency of long-context LLMs. The findings that SemToken performs in tandem with other optimization techniques such as FlashAttention2 further solidifies its impact on long-context LLMs.

**Score: 8**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of long-context language modeling. The core idea is well-motivated, the method is well-defined, and the experimental results are compelling. While there are some limitations regarding encoder overhead, hyperparameter tuning, and extremely long context performance, the overall impact and potential influence of SemToken justify a score of 8.
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### LLM4Sweat: A Trustworthy Large Language Model for Hyperhidrosis Support
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15192v1)
- **Authors**: Wenjie Lin, Jin Wei-Kocsis
- **Abstract**: While large language models (LLMs) have shown promise in healthcare, their application for rare medical conditions is still hindered by scarce and unreliable datasets for fine-tuning. Hyperhidrosis, a disorder causing excessive sweating beyond physiological needs, is one such rare disorder, affecting 2-3% of the population and significantly impacting both physical comfort and psychosocial well-being. To date, no work has tailored LLMs to advance the diagnosis or care of hyperhidrosis. To address this gap, we present LLM4Sweat, an open-source and domain-specific LLM framework for trustworthy and empathetic hyperhidrosis support. The system follows a three-stage pipeline. In the data augmentation stage, a frontier LLM generates medically plausible synthetic vignettes from curated open-source data to create a diverse and balanced question-answer dataset. In the fine-tuning stage, an open-source foundation model is fine-tuned on the dataset to provide diagnosis, personalized treatment recommendations, and empathetic psychological support. In the inference and expert evaluation stage, clinical and psychological specialists assess accuracy, appropriateness, and empathy, with validated responses iteratively enriching the dataset. Experiments show that LLM4Sweat outperforms baselines and delivers the first open-source LLM framework for hyperhidrosis, offering a generalizable approach for other rare diseases with similar data and trustworthiness challenges.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LLM4Sweat, an open-source LLM framework designed to provide trustworthy support for individuals with hyperhidrosis, a condition characterized by excessive sweating. The framework addresses the challenge of limited data availability for rare medical conditions by employing a three-stage pipeline: 1) Data Augmentation: A frontier LLM generates medically plausible synthetic vignettes from curated open-source data to create a diverse question-answer dataset. 2) Fine-tuning: An open-source foundation model is fine-tuned on this dataset to provide diagnosis, personalized treatment recommendations, and empathetic psychological support. 3) Inference and Expert Evaluation: Clinical and psychological specialists assess the accuracy, appropriateness, and empathy of the model's responses, with validated feedback iteratively enriching the dataset. Experiments demonstrate that LLM4Sweat outperforms baseline models, offering a generalizable approach for other rare diseases with similar data and trustworthiness challenges.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel approach by specifically tailoring LLMs to address the challenge of data scarcity in the context of a rare medical condition, hyperhidrosis.  The idea of using a frontier LLM to augment a dataset for a less-common condition is valuable.  The combination of synthetic data generation, fine-tuning, and a closed-loop expert evaluation process represents a significant contribution.  It's the first open-source LLM framework designed for hyperhidrosis support.  While the individual components (data augmentation, fine-tuning) are not entirely new, the specific application and integration within this context are innovative.

* **Significance:**  The work is significant for several reasons: 1) It directly addresses the limitations of applying general-purpose LLMs to rare diseases where data is limited and potentially unreliable. 2) It provides a practical framework that can be adapted for other low-resource medical domains. 3) It emphasizes trustworthiness and ethical considerations by incorporating expert feedback into the training loop, which is crucial for medical applications. 4) The empirical results demonstrate the effectiveness of the framework, showing substantial improvements over baseline models. 5) The paper also details a path to deployment, thus addressing practical challenges and ethical considerations of deployment.

* **Strengths:**
    * Well-defined methodology with clear steps for data augmentation, fine-tuning, and expert evaluation.
    * Demonstrable improvements in performance compared to baseline models.
    * Focus on trustworthy and empathetic responses, essential for medical applications.
    * Open-source nature of the framework, making it accessible to other researchers.
    * Thoughtful discussion of limitations and future work.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * The evaluation is based on a relatively small curated dataset of 80 multiple-choice questions.  This may not fully capture the complexity of real-world clinical interactions. More thorough assessments with larger datasets and patient evaluations would further strengthen the results.
    * The expert evaluation, while valuable, is conducted in limited cycles and with a specific frontier LLM, which might present limitations. Expanded evaluation across diverse clinicians and patients is necessary to ensure robustness.
    * The reliance on a single type of synthetic data generation limits the study. The incorporation of additional data augmentation techniques could increase the dataset’s diversity.

* **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence the field of medical AI by providing a blueprint for developing trustworthy LLMs for rare and underrepresented medical conditions. The framework can be used as a starting point for developing similar applications in other medical domains and it could encourage more research on data augmentation techniques for low-resource medical datasets.

**Justification:**

The LLM4Sweat framework addresses a key challenge in applying LLMs to healthcare – data scarcity for rare diseases – with a practical, well-defined, and empirically validated approach. The novelty lies in the integrated pipeline and the specific application to hyperhidrosis. While the evaluation is limited by the size and nature of the dataset, the results are promising and the discussion of limitations is thorough. The framework promotes the development of more trustworthy and ethical medical AI systems, therefore meriting a high score.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Fin-PRM: A Domain-Specialized Process Reward Model for Financial Reasoning in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15202v1)
- **Authors**: Yuanchen Zhou, Shuo Jiang, Jie Zhu, Junhui Li, Lifan Guo, Feng Chen, Chi Zhang
- **Abstract**: Process Reward Models (PRMs) have emerged as a promising framework for supervising intermediate reasoning in large language models (LLMs), yet existing PRMs are primarily trained on general or Science, Technology, Engineering, and Mathematics (STEM) domains and fall short in domain-specific contexts such as finance, where reasoning is more structured, symbolic, and sensitive to factual and regulatory correctness. We introduce \textbf{Fin-PRM}, a domain-specialized, trajectory-aware PRM tailored to evaluate intermediate reasoning steps in financial tasks. Fin-PRM integrates step-level and trajectory-level reward supervision, enabling fine-grained evaluation of reasoning traces aligned with financial logic. We apply Fin-PRM in both offline and online reward learning settings, supporting three key applications: (i) selecting high-quality reasoning trajectories for distillation-based supervised fine-tuning, (ii) providing dense process-level rewards for reinforcement learning, and (iii) guiding reward-informed Best-of-N inference at test time. Experimental results on financial reasoning benchmarks, including CFLUE and FinQA, demonstrate that Fin-PRM consistently outperforms general-purpose PRMs and strong domain baselines in trajectory selection quality. Downstream models trained with Fin-PRM yield substantial improvements with baselines, with gains of 12.9\% in supervised learning, 5.2\% in reinforcement learning, and 5.1\% in test-time performance. These findings highlight the value of domain-specialized reward modeling for aligning LLMs with expert-level financial reasoning. Our project resources will be available at https://github.com/aliyun/qwen-dianjin.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Fin-PRM, a domain-specialized process reward model (PRM) specifically designed for financial reasoning in large language models (LLMs). Recognizing the limitations of existing general-purpose PRMs in handling the structured, symbolic, and factually sensitive nature of financial tasks, Fin-PRM integrates both step-level and trajectory-level reward supervision. This enables a fine-grained evaluation of reasoning traces aligned with financial logic.  The authors create a new financial reasoning dataset (3k samples) and demonstrate Fin-PRM's effectiveness in three key applications: selecting high-quality reasoning trajectories for distillation-based supervised fine-tuning, providing dense process-level rewards for reinforcement learning, and guiding reward-informed Best-of-N inference. Experimental results on financial reasoning benchmarks (CFLUE and FinQA) show that Fin-PRM consistently outperforms general-purpose PRMs and strong domain baselines, leading to significant improvements in downstream model performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the *domain-specialization* of a PRM. While PRMs themselves aren't entirely new, applying the concept to finance and tailoring the reward mechanism to incorporate financial knowledge verification is a valuable contribution. The dual-level training paradigm (step-wise and trajectory-wise) and the integration of knowledge verification into the reward labeling process also add to the paper's novelty. The novel dataset is valuable, and contributes to reproducibility.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical gap in applying LLMs to finance, where accuracy, factuality, and adherence to regulations are paramount. By improving the alignment of LLM reasoning with expert-level financial analysis, Fin-PRM has the potential to improve the reliability and trustworthiness of LLMs in financial applications. The three applications (SFT data selection, Best-of-N scaling, RL) are well-chosen and demonstrate the versatility of the approach.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the motivation, methodology, and experimental setup.
    *   The approach is well-motivated by the specific challenges of financial reasoning.
    *   The empirical results are strong and consistently demonstrate the superiority of Fin-PRM over baselines. The ablation studies provide insights into the importance of different components of the framework.
    *   The authors comprehensively compare again domain based and non-domain based models, offering insight in the advantage of finance models.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The dataset, while valuable, is relatively small (3k samples) and Chinese-specific. Scaling the dataset and extending it to other languages and financial contexts would further increase the impact.
    *   The knowledge base is static and may not capture the dynamic nature of financial regulations and market conditions. The paper suggests integrating dynamic knowledge sources as future work, which is important.
    *   While the ablation study on the ranking score weight is insightful, a more comprehensive ablation study exploring the impact of different reward components (importance, quality, accuracy, coverage) would provide a deeper understanding of the framework. It would be useful to assess what components are most important and when.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence future research on aligning LLMs with expert-level reasoning in specific domains. It highlights the importance of domain-specialized reward modeling and provides a blueprint for developing trustworthy AI evaluators in high-stakes fields. Future work building upon Fin-PRM could explore more sophisticated knowledge integration techniques, more efficient data synthesis methods, and applications to other financial tasks (e.g., fraud detection, risk management).

The rigorous experiments and solid results, along with the promise for real-world applications, justifies the overall evaluation.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### R-ConstraintBench: Evaluating LLMs on NP-Complete Scheduling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15204v1)
- **Authors**: Raj Jain, Marc Wetter
- **Abstract**: Effective scheduling under tight resource, timing, and operational constraints underpins large-scale planning across sectors such as capital projects, manufacturing, logistics, and IT fleet transitions. However, the reliability of large language models (LLMs) when reasoning under high-constraint regimes is insufficiently characterized. To address this gap, we present R-ConstraintBench, a scalable framework that evaluates models on Resource-Constrained Project Scheduling Problems (RCPSP), an NP-Complete feasibility class, while difficulty increases via linear growth in constraints. R-ConstraintBench incrementally increases non-redundant precedence constraints in Directed Acyclic Graphs (DAGs) and then introduces downtime, temporal windows, and disjunctive constraints. As an illustrative example, we instantiate the benchmark in a data center migration setting and evaluate multiple LLMs using feasibility and error analysis, identifying degradation thresholds and constraint types most associated with failure. Empirically, strong models are near-ceiling on precedence-only DAGs, but feasibility performance collapses when downtime, temporal windows, and disjunctive constraints interact, implicating constraint interaction, not graph depth, as the principal bottleneck. Performance on clean synthetic ramps also does not guarantee transfer to domain-grounded scenarios, underscoring limited generalization.
- **Summary**: Here is a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper "R-ConstraintBench: Evaluating LLMs on NP-Complete Scheduling":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces R-ConstraintBench, a benchmarking framework designed to evaluate the reliability of Large Language Models (LLMs) in handling Resource-Constrained Project Scheduling Problems (RCPSP), an NP-Complete problem class. The framework systematically increases the complexity of RCPSP instances by layering on constraints such as precedence dependencies, resource downtimes, temporal windows, and disjunctive constraints. The paper then assesses the performance of various state-of-the-art LLMs, analyzes their failure modes, and benchmarks transferability using a data center migration scenario. The authors find that while LLMs perform well on simple precedence-based scheduling, their performance deteriorates sharply when additional constraints are introduced and especially when transferring to a domain-grounded setting.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a valuable and novel contribution to the field by specifically focusing on evaluating LLMs on NP-complete scheduling problems. Benchmarking LLMs for planning tasks is not entirely new, but the controlled and layered approach to constraint addition and the domain grounding are significant improvements over existing evaluations. The breakdown of failure modes is also a valuable component.

*   **Significance:** The paper highlights a critical gap in LLM capabilities – the ability to reliably reason under complex constraints in scheduling scenarios. This is a significant finding, as it questions the practical applicability of using LLMs for automated planning and scheduling in real-world scenarios where those constraints are an unavoidable part of the problem.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Controlled Experimental Design:** R-ConstraintBench provides a well-defined framework for systematically evaluating LLMs under increasingly complex constraint scenarios.
    *   **Real-World Relevance:** The focus on RCPSP and the data center migration scenario makes the evaluation relevant to real-world applications.
    *   **Comprehensive Analysis:** The paper offers a detailed analysis of the LLM performance, including feasibility rates, breakpoint analysis, and infeasibility classification.
    *   **Clear Identification of Limitations:** The authors openly discuss the limitations of their study, such as the single-shot prompting approach and the specific domain grounding in data center migration.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Single-Shot Prompting:** The evaluation utilizes only a single-shot prompting approach, which might not fully represent the potential of LLMs that could potentially be enhanced with multi-step reasoning or tool use.
    *   **Limited Domain Grounding:** The domain grounding is limited to data center migration. While relevant, extending to other domains would strengthen the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Lack of comparison with traditional OR methods:** While the purpose of the research is understanding LLMs, it would have been useful to have some benchmark performance from existing constraint satisfaction methods like CP-SAT solvers, especially with regard to time and cost.

*   **Potential Influence:** The R-ConstraintBench framework could become a valuable tool for researchers and practitioners evaluating LLMs for scheduling and planning tasks. It could also inspire further research on improving LLM reasoning capabilities under complex constraints. The analysis of failure modes provides useful insights for developing strategies to mitigate these issues. The analysis is very detailed, thus, this paper may be useful for future studies.

**Score:** 7.5

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel benchmark and performs a rigorous evaluation of LLMs on a relevant and challenging NP-complete problem. The systematic approach and detailed analysis make a valuable contribution to the field, highlighting the limitations of current LLMs in constraint-rich scheduling scenarios. The single-shot prompting and limited domain grounding are the primary weaknesses, but the overall significance of the findings and the potential influence of the R-ConstraintBench framework justify a score of 7.5.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### SparK: Query-Aware Unstructured Sparsity with Recoverable KV Cache Channel Pruning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15212v1)
- **Authors**: Huanxuan Liao, Yixing Xu, Shizhu He, Guanchen Li, Xuanwu Yin, Dong Li, Emad Barsoum, Jun Zhao, Kang Liu
- **Abstract**: Long-context inference in large language models (LLMs) is increasingly constrained by the KV cache bottleneck: memory usage grows linearly with sequence length, while attention computation scales quadratically. Existing approaches address this issue by compressing the KV cache along the temporal axis through strategies such as token eviction or merging to reduce memory and computational overhead. However, these methods often neglect fine-grained importance variations across feature dimensions (i.e., the channel axis), thereby limiting their ability to effectively balance efficiency and model accuracy. In reality, we observe that channel saliency varies dramatically across both queries and positions: certain feature channels carry near-zero information for a given query, while others spike in relevance. To address this oversight, we propose SPARK, a training-free plug-and-play method that applies unstructured sparsity by pruning KV at the channel level, while dynamically restoring the pruned entries during attention score computation. Notably, our approach is orthogonal to existing KV compression and quantization techniques, making it compatible for integration with them to achieve further acceleration. By reducing channel-level redundancy, SPARK enables processing of longer sequences within the same memory budget. For sequences of equal length, SPARK not only preserves or improves model accuracy but also reduces KV cache storage by over 30% compared to eviction-based methods. Furthermore, even with an aggressive pruning ratio of 80%, SPARK maintains performance with less degradation than 5% compared to the baseline eviction method, demonstrating its robustness and effectiveness. Our code will be available at https://github.com/Xnhyacinth/SparK.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and evaluation of the paper "SPARK: Query-Aware Unstructured Sparsity with Recoverable KV Cache Channel Pruning":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SPARK, a novel training-free plug-and-play method designed to compress the KV cache in large language models (LLMs) during inference. SPARK achieves this by employing unstructured sparsity at the channel level, pruning less important feature channels based on query-aware saliency measurement. Crucially, SPARK incorporates a dynamic recovery mechanism that restores pruned entries during attention computation, mitigating information loss, especially under high pruning ratios. The method is orthogonal to existing KV compression techniques (e.g., token eviction, quantization), allowing for synergistic integration. Through extensive experiments, the authors demonstrate that SPARK enhances performance and reduces memory consumption compared to token eviction methods. Even at aggressive pruning ratios, SPARK maintains accuracy while significantly reducing KV cache storage.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its introduction of unstructured sparsity at the channel level within the KV cache, combined with a recoverable pruning approach. Unlike previous structured pruning methods, SPARK acknowledges and addresses the dynamic and token-specific nature of attention, leading to improved flexibility and performance. The idea of recovering pruned channels based on learned distribution statistics represents a practical and effective solution to information loss inherent in aggressive pruning. While query-aware pruning isn't entirely new, its specific application within the KV cache, coupled with the recovery mechanism and unstructured sparsity, contributes a unique approach. The ratio-free variants (group-based and top-p pruning) add another layer of flexibility and innovation.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from its potential to address the KV cache bottleneck, a major constraint in deploying long-context LLMs. By enabling effective compression and recovery, SPARK offers a way to process longer sequences within similar memory budgets, expanding the applicability of LLMs to tasks requiring extended contextual understanding. The plug-and-play nature of the method facilitates its integration into existing LLM pipelines without requiring retraining. The experimental results demonstrate a clear improvement in both efficiency and accuracy, making it potentially impactful.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Strong experimental results:** The paper presents a thorough evaluation across various benchmarks, LLMs, and KV cache budgets. It effectively demonstrates the superiority of SPARK over existing methods, especially at high pruning ratios.
    *   **Practical and Efficient Design:** The proposed recovery mechanism is designed to be lightweight and introduce minimal computational overhead, enhancing the practical applicability of the method.
    *   **Orthogonality and Composability:** SPARK is designed to be compatible with other KV compression techniques, allowing for synergistic optimization and further performance improvements.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies help to understand the impact of each component of SPARK and further demonstrate the method's robustness.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Value Cache Optimization:** The approach optimizes the value cache using a simplistic norm-based heuristic which, while demonstrating promising results, indicates room for future sophistication.
    *   **Recovery Mechanism Limitations**: Although novel, the approximation performed by the recovery mechanism may yield sub-optimal performance compared to storing all the channels, and is also dependent on the prefill distribution of the original values. While effective, it is unclear how effective these distributions are with large sequences.
    *   **Increased computational overhead:** As stated in the paper, this comes with increased computational overhead, but does not give a comparison in total inference time compared to other approaches.

*   **Potential Influence:** SPARK's focus on channel-level sparsity and recoverable pruning has the potential to influence future research in KV cache compression and efficient LLM inference. It may encourage further exploration of dynamic and token-specific sparsity patterns, leading to more advanced compression and recovery techniques.

**Justification of Score:**

SPARK presents a valuable contribution to the field of efficient LLM inference. Its novelty in using unstructured sparsity with recoverable pruning at the channel level, combined with strong experimental results and a practical design, makes it a significant step forward. However, the limited value cache optimization and inherent limitations of the recovery approximation prevent it from achieving an even higher score.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Select to Know: An Internal-External Knowledge Self-Selection Framework for Domain-Specific Question Answering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15213v1)
- **Authors**: Bolei He, Xinran He, Run Shao, Shanfu Shu, Xianwei Xue, Mingquan Cheng, Haifeng Li, Zhenhua Ling
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) perform well in general QA but often struggle in domain-specific scenarios. Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) introduces external knowledge but suffers from hallucinations and latency due to noisy retrievals. Continued pretraining internalizes domain knowledge but is costly and lacks cross-domain flexibility. We attribute this challenge to the long-tail distribution of domain knowledge, which leaves partial yet useful internal knowledge underutilized. We further argue that knowledge acquisition should be progressive, mirroring human learning: first understanding concepts, then applying them to complex reasoning. To address this, we propose Selct2Know (S2K), a cost-effective framework that internalizes domain knowledge through an internal-external knowledge self-selection strategy and selective supervised fine-tuning. We also introduce a structured reasoning data generation pipeline and integrate GRPO to enhance reasoning ability. Experiments on medical, legal, and financial QA benchmarks show that S2K consistently outperforms existing methods and matches domain-pretrained LLMs with significantly lower cost.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Select to Know: An Internal-External Knowledge Self-Selection Framework for Domain-Specific Question Answering":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of domain-specific question answering (DSQA) using large language models (LLMs). It argues that LLMs struggle in DSQA due to the long-tail distribution of domain knowledge, leading to underutilized, partially internalized knowledge. The authors propose Select2Know (S2K), a cost-effective post-training framework. S2K leverages a token-level internal-external knowledge self-selection strategy to fuse internal parametric knowledge with external domain knowledge.  It also incorporates selective supervised fine-tuning (SFT) that focuses on unmastered domain knowledge.  Finally, it introduces a structured reasoning data generation pipeline and integrates GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) to enhance reasoning capabilities. Experiments on medical, law, and financial QA benchmarks demonstrate that S2K outperforms existing methods and matches the performance of domain-pretrained LLMs at a significantly lower cost.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The novelty of the paper lies in the specific combination of several existing ideas and the introduction of a few refinements. The core concept of combining internal and external knowledge is not entirely new, as Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) already does this. However, the token-level self-selection mechanism for fusing knowledge *is* a novel contribution, addressing the limitations of standard RAG by being more selective in which external knowledge to use. Selective SFT is also a good idea for training, to focus on learning things the model has not already learned. The structured data generation pipeline for reasoning, while helpful, doesn't seem particularly novel on its own, but complements the other aspects of the approach.

**Significance:**  The paper's significance comes from its practical approach to improving DSQA performance. The method's relatively low cost makes it appealing, especially compared to the resource-intensive pre-training of domain-specific LLMs. The consistent improvements across different domains (medicine, law, finance) suggest good generalization capabilities. By demonstrating comparable or better performance than domain-pretrained models with much less data, the work highlights the potential for efficient domain adaptation. A weakness here is that domain-pretrained models are generally expected to be state-of-the-art. By comparing against these, and not specifically beating their results, the significance of the paper is somewhat reduced.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of LLMs in DSQA and motivates the need for a more efficient domain adaptation strategy.
*   **Technically Sound Approach:** The S2K framework is well-defined and integrates several techniques logically.
*   **Empirical Validation:** Extensive experiments across diverse domains provide strong evidence for the effectiveness of S2K.
*   **Cost-Effectiveness:** Demonstrating comparable performance to domain-pretrained models at a lower cost is a significant advantage.
*   **Reproducibility:** The paper contains substantial details on experimental configurations, thus enhancing reproducibility.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Novelty:** While the combination of techniques is innovative, each component, by itself, is not ground-breaking. The token-level knowledge selection is the most novel component, but further in-depth analysis and comparison to other knowledge fusion techniques are needed.
*   **Reasoning Data Generation:** The paper designs a data generation pipeline to efficiently produce high-quality reasoning data, which is useful, but is not necessarily a novel contribution by itself.
*   **Comparison to State-of-the-art Domain-Pretrained LLMs:** The comparisons to the domain-pretrained LLMs serve as a double edged sword. If S2K cannot surpass these LLMs in quality, then one may decide to just pretrain, which is the typical best-practice.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper has the potential to influence the field of DSQA by providing a practical and cost-effective alternative to domain-specific pre-training. The S2K framework could be adopted by practitioners seeking to improve the performance of LLMs in specialized domains without incurring significant computational costs. The ideas of token-level knowledge selection and selective SFT may also inspire future research in knowledge fusion and domain adaptation.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7**.

*   **Novelty (2/3):** Token level knowledge selection and focus SFT are the two novel contributions, but the remaining aspects build upon the pre-existing RAG literature. The data generation is useful, but does not have novelty.
*   **Significance (3/4):** The paper offers a practical and efficient approach to DSQA, validated across multiple domains, but the improvement over pre-trained LLMs is not substantially higher.
*   **Completeness (2/3):** The study is thorough in its experimentation and ablation analyses, demonstrating the benefits of each component. The inclusion of failure cases or limitations would further strengthen the analysis.

Overall, the paper makes a valuable contribution by combining existing and novel techniques into a cohesive and effective framework for DSQA. While the individual components may not be revolutionary, their integration and the demonstrated cost-effectiveness make this work worthwhile.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Self-Guided Function Calling in Large Language Models via Stepwise Experience Recall
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15214v1)
- **Authors**: Sijia Cui, Aiyao He, Shuai Xu, Hongming Zhang, Yanna Wang, Qingyang Zhang, Yajing Wang, Bo Xu
- **Abstract**: Function calling enables large language models (LLMs) to interact with external systems by leveraging tools and APIs. When faced with multi-step tool usage, LLMs still struggle with tool selection, parameter generation, and tool-chain planning. Existing methods typically rely on manually designing task-specific demonstrations, or retrieving from a curated library. These approaches demand substantial expert effort and prompt engineering becomes increasingly complex and inefficient as tool diversity and task difficulty scale. To address these challenges, we propose a self-guided method, Stepwise Experience Recall (SEER), which performs fine-grained, stepwise retrieval from a continually updated experience pool. Instead of relying on static or manually curated library, SEER incrementally augments the experience pool with past successful trajectories, enabling continuous expansion of the pool and improved model performance over time. Evaluated on the ToolQA benchmark, SEER achieves an average improvement of 6.1\% on easy and 4.7\% on hard questions. We further test SEER on $\tau$-bench, which includes two real-world domains. Powered by Qwen2.5-7B and Qwen2.5-72B models, SEER demonstrates substantial accuracy gains of 7.44\% and 23.38\%, respectively.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper introduces Stepwise Experience Recall (SEER), a novel self-guided function calling method designed to enhance Large Language Model (LLM) performance in multi-step tool-use scenarios.  SEER incrementally builds and leverages an experience pool of successful tool-use trajectories. It employs a fine-grained retrieval mechanism considering task similarity, toolchain coverage, and user intent to select relevant examples for in-context learning. The experience pool is dynamically updated with successful trajectories, which eliminates the need for manually designed examples or curated libraries and enables the model to self-improve. Experiments on the ToolQA and T-bench benchmarks demonstrate that SEER outperforms existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation**

* **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the stepwise experience recall and the online experience accumulation. Existing methods often rely on static datasets or manual prompt engineering. The idea of dynamically updating the experience pool based on the model's own successful trajectories is a strong contribution.  The multi-dimensional retrieval strategy (task similarity, toolchain coverage, intent alignment) seems well-motivated and useful.
* **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial challenge in LLM tool usage: effectively handling multi-step tool interactions. By dynamically adapting and improving its experience, SEER promises better scalability and adaptability than methods relying on fixed demonstrations. The results on ToolQA are solid, showing clear improvements over existing methods.  The T-bench results are also encouraging, demonstrating the real-world applicability of SEER. The fact that it enhances the performance of open-source models (particularly Qwen2.5-72B) is important.
* **Strengths:**
    * The self-guided learning approach is a key strength, reducing the need for manual effort and allowing the system to adapt over time.
    * The combination of different factors in the retrieval mechanism (trajectory similarity, toolchain coverage, intent alignment) appears to be more effective than relying on any single factor.
    * The experimental results are comprehensive and demonstrate the effectiveness of SEER across multiple benchmarks.
    * The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of each component of SEER.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * The description of the LLM-as-judge mechanism for evaluating task completion is somewhat brief. More details on how potential biases are mitigated would be beneficial.
    * The approach inherently depends on the base LLM's ability to generate successful trajectories in the first place.  The paper could discuss limitations related to this more thoroughly.
    * While the experiments demonstrate improvements, the absolute performance on the T-bench benchmark, particularly on the Qwen2.5-7B model, is still relatively low.  This suggests there's still room for improvement in real-world scenarios.
    * The reliance on a pre-defined, discrete intent set limits the model's ability to handle novel or ambiguous user intentions. A continuous intent representation could improve adaptability.
* **Potential Influence:**  SEER has the potential to influence future research in LLM tool usage by promoting dynamic experience-based approaches.  The stepwise retrieval and the emphasis on toolchain coverage could be adopted in other systems.  The paper may also motivate further research into self-improvement mechanisms for LLMs in tool-use contexts.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

The paper presents a genuinely novel method for self-guided tool usage in LLMs. The strengths of SEER, including its dynamic experience pool and multi-faceted retrieval strategy, significantly contribute to its effectiveness.  The improvements demonstrated in the experimental evaluations are substantial and consistent across multiple benchmarks. However, the reliance on a potentially biased evaluation mechanism and limitations in the absolute performance on real-world tasks, indicate areas for future work. While the paper builds on existing work in in-context learning and self-improvement, the way it addresses the specific challenges of multi-step tool use in LLMs is sufficiently unique and impactful to merit a high score.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Are Checklists Really Useful for Automatic Evaluation of Generative Tasks?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15218v1)
- **Authors**: Momoka Furuhashi, Kouta Nakayama, Takashi Kodama, Saku Sugawara
- **Abstract**: Automatic evaluation of generative tasks using large language models faces challenges due to ambiguous criteria. Although automatic checklist generation is a potentially promising approach, its usefulness remains underexplored. We investigate whether checklists should be used for all questions or selectively, generate them using six methods, evaluate their effectiveness across eight model sizes, and identify checklist items that correlate with human evaluations. Through experiments on pairwise comparison and direct scoring tasks, we find that selective checklist use tends to improve evaluation performance in pairwise settings, while its benefits are less consistent in direct scoring. Our analysis also shows that even checklist items with low correlation to human scores often reflect human-written criteria, indicating potential inconsistencies in human evaluation. These findings highlight the need to more clearly define objective evaluation criteria to guide both human and automatic evaluations. \footnote{Our code is available at~https://github.com/momo0817/checklist-effectiveness-study
- **Summary**: **Summary:**

The paper "Are Checklists Really Useful for Automatic Evaluation of Generative Tasks?" explores the utility of checklists in automatic evaluation of text generation using large language models (LLMs). Automatic evaluation, while scalable, can struggle with ambiguous criteria and inconsistent alignment with human judgment. Checklists, which decompose evaluation criteria into specific items, offer a potential solution. The study investigates whether checklists are always beneficial or selectively useful, examines different methods for checklist generation, evaluates their impact on models of varying sizes, and identifies checklist items correlating with human evaluations. Through pairwise comparisons and direct scoring tasks, the authors find that selective checklist use can improve performance in pairwise settings, but its impact is less consistent in direct scoring. The analysis reveals that even checklist items with low correlation to human scores often reflect human-written criteria, suggesting inconsistencies in human evaluation.

**Rigorous and Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty (5/10):** The paper addresses a timely issue: the difficulty of automatic evaluation in generative tasks. While checklist-based evaluation is not entirely new (previous work is referenced), the study's focus on *when* checklists are necessary, how to generate *useful* checklists, and the *alignment* of checklist items with human evaluations provides a more nuanced perspective. This offers some novelty, but it is incremental rather than revolutionary. The exploration of selective application and the analysis of individual checklist item contributions are valuable additions.

*   **Significance (6/10):** The findings are significant because they challenge the assumption that checklist-based evaluation is universally beneficial. The study highlights the task-dependent nature of checklist effectiveness and reveals the importance of aligning checklist generation methods with specific evaluation models and use cases. Furthermore, the observation that low-correlation checklist items can still reflect valid human criteria points to the need for clearer, more objective evaluation design. These insights have practical implications for researchers and practitioners developing automatic evaluation systems for generative tasks.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Analysis:** The study employs a thorough experimental design, considering a range of models, tasks, and checklist generation methods.
    *   **Nuanced Findings:** The paper presents a more complex picture of checklist effectiveness, moving beyond simple adoption.
    *   **Practical Implications:** The findings offer actionable insights for improving automatic evaluation systems, including guidance on selective checklist use and the need for clearer evaluation criteria.
    *   **Well-reasoned arguments**

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **English language dataset limitation.** Lack of generalizability across tasks and languages.
    *   **Checklist generaltion policy: limitations include** excluding methods based on predetermined evaluation criteria.
    *   **Confounding factors**. There is possibility of excluding the full feature set captured by state of the art LLMs.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence the development and deployment of automatic evaluation systems for generative tasks. The insights on selective checklist application and checklist generation methods could guide researchers in designing more effective evaluation systems. The findings on the need for clearer evaluation criteria could encourage a more critical examination of human evaluation protocols and a greater emphasis on objectivity.

**Score: 6**

The paper offers a valuable, nuanced investigation of checklist-based evaluation for generative tasks. While not groundbreaking, the study provides several practical insights and challenges the assumption that checklists are always beneficial. The comprehensive analysis and practical implications warrant a moderate level of significance within the field. The weaknesses described above prevent a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### See it. Say it. Sorted: Agentic System for Compositional Diagram Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15222v1)
- **Authors**: Hantao Zhang, Jingyang Liu, Ed Li
- **Abstract**: We study sketch-to-diagram generation: converting rough hand sketches into precise, compositional diagrams. Diffusion models excel at photorealism but struggle with the spatial precision, alignment, and symbolic structure required for flowcharts. We introduce See it. Say it. Sorted., a training-free agentic system that couples a Vision-Language Model (VLM) with Large Language Models (LLMs) to produce editable Scalable Vector Graphics (SVG) programs. The system runs an iterative loop in which a Critic VLM proposes a small set of qualitative, relational edits; multiple candidate LLMs synthesize SVG updates with diverse strategies (conservative->aggressive, alternative, focused); and a Judge VLM selects the best candidate, ensuring stable improvement. This design prioritizes qualitative reasoning over brittle numerical estimates, preserves global constraints (e.g., alignment, connectivity), and naturally supports human-in-the-loop corrections. On 10 sketches derived from flowcharts in published papers, our method more faithfully reconstructs layout and structure than two frontier closed-source image generation LLMs (GPT-5 and Gemini-2.5-Pro), accurately composing primitives (e.g., multi-headed arrows) without inserting unwanted text. Because outputs are programmatic SVGs, the approach is readily extensible to presentation tools (e.g., PowerPoint) via APIs and can be specialized with improved prompts and task-specific tools. The codebase is open-sourced at https://github.com/hantaoZhangrichard/see_it_say_it_sorted.git.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "See it. Say it. Sorted.", a novel, training-free agentic system for converting hand-drawn sketches into precise, editable SVG diagrams. The system combines a Vision-Language Model (VLM) and Large Language Models (LLMs) within an iterative Critic-Candidates-Judge loop. The VLM acts as a "Critic" to identify discrepancies between the sketch and the current diagram, prompting multiple LLMs to generate diverse SVG modifications ("Candidates"). Finally, another VLM acts as a "Judge" to select the best candidate, ensuring stable improvement towards the target diagram. The system prioritizes qualitative reasoning and global constraint preservation, supports human-in-the-loop correction, and outputs editable SVG code. The authors demonstrate that their method outperforms frontier image generation LLMs (GPT-5 and Gemini-2.5-Pro) in reconstructing diagram structure and characteristics.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the architecture of the system: combining VLMs and LLMs in a specific iterative loop designed for programmatic graphics generation without requiring task-specific training.  The use of qualitative relational reasoning over brittle numerical estimates is a sound design choice. While other works have explored agentic systems using VLMs and LLMs, applying it to sketch-to-diagram generation and prioritizing editability and structured output is a clear contribution. The fact that this is achieved *without* task-specific training is a significant advantage, showing good generalization capabilities.

**Significance:** The ability to generate editable SVG diagrams from sketches is a significant step towards more flexible and user-centric graphics design workflows. The output format is critical:  pixel-based images are far less useful than editable vector graphics. The potential to integrate the system into existing graphics applications (PowerPoint, Google Slides) further increases its practical value. The improvements in spatial reasoning and precise composition compared to standard image generation models (as demonstrated in the comparison against GPT-5 and Gemini-2.5-Pro) make this a potentially influential paper in the field of diagram generation and potentially more broadly in agentic systems and structured outputs.

**Strengths:**

*   **Training-free approach:** This is a substantial strength. Avoiding the need for large training datasets makes the system more adaptable and accessible.
*   **Emphasis on qualitative reasoning:** This addresses a known limitation of VLMs in precise numerical estimation.
*   **Iterative refinement loop:** This enables gradual improvements and avoids drastic, potentially detrimental modifications.
*   **Editable SVG output:**  This allows for easy integration and further refinement in existing design tools.
*   **Clear experimental results:** The qualitative comparison with GPT-5 and Gemini-2.5-Pro highlights the strengths of the proposed method.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Reliance on VLMs:** The performance of the system is heavily dependent on the capabilities of the VLMs used. Limitations in VLM understanding or reasoning can affect the quality of the generated diagrams. This is acknowledged in the paper.
*   **Limited primitive set:** The system is constrained by the minimal set of primitive shapes and colors.  More complex or nuanced diagrams may require additional primitives or more sophisticated composition strategies.
*   **Qualitative evaluation:** The evaluation primarily relies on visual comparison.  While these comparisons are informative, a more quantitative evaluation (e.g., measuring alignment accuracy, shape similarity) would strengthen the results.
*   **Lack of broader tasks for evaluation**: The paper only evaluates its approach using a relatively narrow set of flowcharts derived from papers. Further evaluations for more complex diagrams, or application on other domains are needed to show its robustness.

**Justification of Score:**

While the paper is not without its limitations, the novelty of the agentic architecture, the focus on editable output, and the solid experimental results justify a relatively high score. The fact that the method requires no training is also a big plus. The identified weaknesses (VLM dependence, limited primitives) are valid points, but do not negate the overall contribution. The work represents a meaningful advance in diagram generation and has the potential to influence future research in structured output generation and agentic systems.

Score: 7.5
