- **Score**: 7/10

### GenTune: Toward Traceable Prompts to Improve Controllability of Image Refinement in Environment Design
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15227v1)
- **Authors**: Wen-Fan Wang, Ting-Ying Lee, Chien-Ting Lu, Che-Wei Hsu, Nil Ponsa Campany, Yu Chen, Mike Y. Chen, Bing-Yu Chen
- **Abstract**: Environment designers in the entertainment industry create imaginative 2D and 3D scenes for games, films, and television, requiring both fine-grained control of specific details and consistent global coherence. Designers have increasingly integrated generative AI into their workflows, often relying on large language models (LLMs) to expand user prompts for text-to-image generation, then iteratively refining those prompts and applying inpainting. However, our formative study with 10 designers surfaced two key challenges: (1) the lengthy LLM-generated prompts make it difficult to understand and isolate the keywords that must be revised for specific visual elements; and (2) while inpainting supports localized edits, it can struggle with global consistency and correctness. Based on these insights, we present GenTune, an approach that enhances human--AI collaboration by clarifying how AI-generated prompts map to image content. Our GenTune system lets designers select any element in a generated image, trace it back to the corresponding prompt labels, and revise those labels to guide precise yet globally consistent image refinement. In a summative study with 20 designers, GenTune significantly improved prompt--image comprehension, refinement quality, and efficiency, and overall satisfaction (all $p < .01$) compared to current practice. A follow-up field study with two studios further demonstrated its effectiveness in real-world settings.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "GenTune: Toward Traceable Prompts to Improve Controllability of Image Refinement in Environment Design":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GenTune, a human-centered AI system designed to improve controllability in image refinement for environment design. Addressing challenges identified in a formative study with environment designers, GenTune focuses on traceable prompts and semantic-guided refinement. The system allows designers to select elements in a generated image, trace them back to the corresponding prompt labels, and revise those labels to guide precise image refinement. The system offers three refinement modes: refining the selected label, modifying only the selected region, or comparing both. The system utilizes LLMs to expand initial prompts, extract labels, and refine prompts based on natural language or reference images. A summative study with 20 designers demonstrates that GenTune significantly improves prompt-image comprehension, refinement quality, efficiency, and overall satisfaction compared to current practices.  A field study with two studios further supports its effectiveness in real-world settings, highlighting improved efficiency and communication.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the combination of traceability (prompt-to-image element mapping) with semantic-guided refinement, specifically tailored to the needs of environment designers. While existing tools offer either prompt editing or image editing features like inpainting, GenTune uniquely links the two through a traceable prompt interface that addresses the "black box" problem of LLM-generated prompts. The focus on maintaining global coherence during local edits is also a valuable contribution, as existing inpainting techniques can often introduce inconsistencies.
*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the problem it tackles – the difficulties faced by environment designers in controlling AI-generated images for pre-production tasks. By offering more granular control and improved understanding of the AI generation process, GenTune has the potential to streamline workflows, reduce trial-and-error cycles, and enhance creative exploration. The user studies, particularly the field study, provide compelling evidence of the system's practical benefits and impact on professional workflows. The findings offer valuable insights into the design considerations for human-AI collaboration in creative domains.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper is well-structured and clearly articulates the problem, solution, and evaluation.
    *   The formative study effectively grounds the design of GenTune in the real-world needs of environment designers.
    *   The traceable prompt and semantic-guided refinement approach offers a novel and intuitive way to control AI image generation.
    *   The summative study and field study provide strong evidence of the system's effectiveness and usability.
    *   The paper explicitly addresses ethical considerations around AI adoption in creative fields.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper acknowledges limitations regarding label accuracy and the potential instability of prompt refinement, where T2I models struggle with structural consistency when introducing even small changes. The paper could expand discussion by including more cases of refinements where it performed poorly, and include a detailed qualitative evaluation.
    *   While the study involves professional environment designers, it is limited to the scope of the team, or studios the research team has direct access to. A broader participant pool in the field study would increase generalizability.

*   **Potential Influence:** GenTune's concept of traceable prompts could influence the design of future AI-assisted tools for creative tasks, particularly in domains involving complex visual environments. It sets a direction for creating more transparent and controllable AI systems that better align with human creative intentions.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:**

GenTune presents a significant contribution by addressing a real-world problem faced by environment designers using generative AI. The system offers a novel combination of traceable prompts and semantic-guided refinement, enhancing user control and understanding. The user studies provide compelling evidence of its effectiveness and usability. While acknowledging limitations related to label accuracy and structural consistency. However, the system's novelty, practical benefits demonstrated through rigorous evaluations, and potential influence on future HCI design for creative AI justify this rating.

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 8/10

### Collaborative Multi-Modal Coding for High-Quality 3D Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15228v1)
- **Authors**: Ziang Cao, Zhaoxi Chen, Liang Pan, Ziwei Liu
- **Abstract**: 3D content inherently encompasses multi-modal characteristics and can be projected into different modalities (e.g., RGB images, RGBD, and point clouds). Each modality exhibits distinct advantages in 3D asset modeling: RGB images contain vivid 3D textures, whereas point clouds define fine-grained 3D geometries. However, most existing 3D-native generative architectures either operate predominantly within single-modality paradigms-thus overlooking the complementary benefits of multi-modality data-or restrict themselves to 3D structures, thereby limiting the scope of available training datasets. To holistically harness multi-modalities for 3D modeling, we present TriMM, the first feed-forward 3D-native generative model that learns from basic multi-modalities (e.g., RGB, RGBD, and point cloud). Specifically, 1) TriMM first introduces collaborative multi-modal coding, which integrates modality-specific features while preserving their unique representational strengths. 2) Furthermore, auxiliary 2D and 3D supervision are introduced to raise the robustness and performance of multi-modal coding. 3) Based on the embedded multi-modal code, TriMM employs a triplane latent diffusion model to generate 3D assets of superior quality, enhancing both the texture and the geometric detail. Extensive experiments on multiple well-known datasets demonstrate that TriMM, by effectively leveraging multi-modality, achieves competitive performance with models trained on large-scale datasets, despite utilizing a small amount of training data. Furthermore, we conduct additional experiments on recent RGB-D datasets, verifying the feasibility of incorporating other multi-modal datasets into 3D generation.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary of the paper and a critical evaluation, including a novelty/significance score.

**Summary**

The paper introduces TriMM, a collaborative multi-modal coding framework for high-quality 3D generation from basic multi-modalities (RGB, RGBD, and point clouds). The framework addresses the limitations of existing 3D-native generative architectures, which often operate in single-modality paradigms or are restricted to specific 3D structures. TriMM leverages the complementary strengths of different modalities: RGB for texture, point clouds for geometry, and RGBD for spatial understanding.  The framework integrates modality-specific features using collaborative multi-modal coding while preserving unique representational strengths.  Auxiliary 2D and 3D supervision is also employed to enhance robustness. A triplane latent diffusion model then generates high-quality 3D assets conditioned on the multi-modal code. The authors demonstrate through experiments on multiple datasets that TriMM achieves competitive performance, even with smaller training datasets, compared to models trained on much larger single-modality datasets. They also show the feasibility of incorporating new RGB-D datasets into the 3D generation process.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel approach to 3D generation by holistically integrating multiple modalities in a feed-forward manner.  The idea of collaborative multi-modal coding is the central contribution.  While multi-modal 3D generation has been explored before, explicitly designing a feed-forward architecture that leverages the strengths of RGB, RGBD, and point clouds in a unified framework is a valuable advancement. The inclusion of a reconstruction loss that attends to the unique features of each modality is also novel. The auxiliary 2D and 3D supervision further improves the performance. This method of early fusion of multiple modalities improves training and results.

*   **Significance:** The ability to generate high-quality 3D assets from limited and diverse data sources is highly significant, given the relative scarcity of 3D training data compared to 2D image datasets. TriMM's approach can potentially unlock the use of many existing datasets for 3D generative modeling which would make the models more robust and generalizable to new scenarios. The results show the model achieves comparable or better performance with less data compared to state-of-the-art methods trained on large datasets. This could lead to more efficient and scalable 3D generation pipelines.  The framework's extensible design supports the tokenization of real-world multi-modal inputs, enhancing its practical applicability.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem statement and well-defined solution.
    *   Novel collaborative multi-modal coding approach.
    *   Demonstrated ability to leverage limited data effectively.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation with quantitative and qualitative results.
    *   Extensible design that allows for incorporation of new modalities.
    *   The method is relatively simple, which makes it easy to adopt.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The method still depends on a diffusion model, which are known to be computationally expensive to train and sample.
    *   The use of a triplane latent diffusion model is not particularly novel.
    *   While the experimental results are impressive, they are still limited to object-centric generation and have not demonstrated the framework on more complex scenarios.

*   **Potential Influence:** TriMM has the potential to influence the field of 3D generation by demonstrating the effectiveness of multi-modal learning and offering a practical framework for integrating diverse data sources. It provides a strong baseline for future research on multi-modal 3D generation and highlights the importance of data efficiency. Future studies could explore more sophisticated ways of combining the modalities, possibly learning attention weights between them, and extend the generation targets beyond objects.

**Rigorous Rationale for the Score:**

TriMM addresses a significant bottleneck in 3D generation – the scarcity of training data – by effectively utilizing multiple modalities. The novelty lies in the architectural design that explicitly captures and integrates information from different data types, as well as the training methodology that takes their unique strengths into account. While the reliance on a diffusion model is a potential limitation, the overall contribution is significant enough to warrant a high score. However, the reliance on diffusion models diminishes novelty because most new works incorporate existing diffusion models. Additionally, more complex experiments on scenes or more complicated objects would significantly improve the impact of the paper.

### Pretrained Diffusion Models Are Inherently Skipped-Step Samplers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15233v1)
- **Authors**: Wenju Xu
- **Abstract**: Diffusion models have been achieving state-of-the-art results across various generation tasks. However, a notable drawback is their sequential generation process, requiring long-sequence step-by-step generation. Existing methods, such as DDIM, attempt to reduce sampling steps by constructing a class of non-Markovian diffusion processes that maintain the same training objective. However, there remains a gap in understanding whether the original diffusion process can achieve the same efficiency without resorting to non-Markovian processes. In this paper, we provide a confirmative answer and introduce skipped-step sampling, a mechanism that bypasses multiple intermediate denoising steps in the iterative generation process, in contrast with the traditional step-by-step refinement of standard diffusion inference. Crucially, we demonstrate that this skipped-step sampling mechanism is derived from the same training objective as the standard diffusion model, indicating that accelerated sampling via skipped-step sampling via a Markovian way is an intrinsic property of pretrained diffusion models. Additionally, we propose an enhanced generation method by integrating our accelerated sampling technique with DDIM. Extensive experiments on popular pretrained diffusion models, including the OpenAI ADM, Stable Diffusion, and Open Sora models, show that our method achieves high-quality generation with significantly reduced sampling steps.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a "skipped-step" sampling technique for diffusion models, aiming to accelerate the inference process.  The core idea is that pretrained diffusion models inherently possess the capability to denoise inputs by skipping multiple intermediate steps.  The authors theoretically prove that this skipped-step sampling is derived from the same training objective as standard diffusion models, indicating it's an intrinsic property rather than requiring specific training modifications. They also introduce an enhanced generation method by integrating skipped-step sampling with DDIM.  Extensive experiments on popular diffusion models (ADM, Stable Diffusion, Open Sora) demonstrate high-quality generation with significantly reduced sampling steps.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The concept of skipped-step sampling itself isn't entirely new in the context of diffusion model acceleration.  Methods like DDIM already aim to reduce sampling steps. However, the paper's central claim—that standard, *pretrained* diffusion models inherently support such skipping *without* requiring alterations to the training process or specific architectural designs—is a significant contribution.  The theoretical justification for this claim strengthens the novelty. Proving the equivalence to the original objective function is key. The application to a wide variety of pretrained models (ADM, Stable Diffusion, Open Sora) and showing consistent improvements is also a strong point.  The integration with DDIM is a relatively straightforward extension of the core idea.

* **Significance:** The potential impact of this work is high. The major bottleneck in diffusion models is inference speed.  A technique that can substantially reduce sampling steps without sacrificing quality directly addresses this problem. If widely adopted, it could make diffusion models more practical for real-time applications and resource-constrained environments. The fact that it works with existing, pretrained models makes it immediately useful without requiring retraining.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Theoretical Justification:** The mathematical derivation showing the equivalence of the skipped-step objective with the standard diffusion training objective is the paper's strongest point.
    * **Empirical Validation:** Extensive experiments across multiple large-scale, state-of-the-art models lend strong support to the claims.
    * **Practicality:** The technique is easily implemented and applicable to pretrained models.
    * **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and the concepts are clearly explained, making it accessible to a broad audience.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Incremental Improvement (DDIM Integration):** While the DDIM integration enhances performance, it is not a revolutionary idea. It is more of an engineering trick to further improve the results.
    * **Limited Ablation Studies:** While a DDIM integration study is present, more in-depth analysis on design choices for skipping steps or adaptive skipping schedules could strengthen the paper.
    * **Focus on Established Models:** The models used are primarily well-established. While this demonstrates broad applicability, exploration on more recent architectures or novel domains would add further value.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to be influential. It provides a simple and effective way to accelerate diffusion model inference, which is a critical issue in the field.  Other researchers may build upon this work by developing more sophisticated skipping schedules, adaptive strategies, or combining it with other acceleration techniques.

**Rationale for Score:**

The paper's strength lies in its theoretical justification and empirical validation of the skipped-step sampling. The idea is significant because it directly tackles the key bottleneck of diffusion models - slow inference. The fact that it can be applied to existing pretrained models makes it very useful. While the DDIM integration is incremental, the core idea itself is a substantial contribution. Given that the concept of skipping steps isn't entirely novel *in general* but the proof that standard pretrained models support it *inherently* is, and considering the practical benefits and strong empirical evidence, a high score is warranted, but not at the top end.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Pathology-Informed Latent Diffusion Model for Anomaly Detection in Lymph Node Metastasis
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15236v1)
- **Authors**: Jiamu Wang, Keunho Byeon, Jinsol Song, Anh Nguyen, Sangjeong Ahn, Sung Hak Lee, Jin Tae Kwak
- **Abstract**: Anomaly detection is an emerging approach in digital pathology for its ability to efficiently and effectively utilize data for disease diagnosis. While supervised learning approaches deliver high accuracy, they rely on extensively annotated datasets, suffering from data scarcity in digital pathology. Unsupervised anomaly detection, however, offers a viable alternative by identifying deviations from normal tissue distributions without requiring exhaustive annotations. Recently, denoising diffusion probabilistic models have gained popularity in unsupervised anomaly detection, achieving promising performance in both natural and medical imaging datasets. Building on this, we incorporate a vision-language model with a diffusion model for unsupervised anomaly detection in digital pathology, utilizing histopathology prompts during reconstruction. Our approach employs a set of pathology-related keywords associated with normal tissues to guide the reconstruction process, facilitating the differentiation between normal and abnormal tissues. To evaluate the effectiveness of the proposed method, we conduct experiments on a gastric lymph node dataset from a local hospital and assess its generalization ability under domain shift using a public breast lymph node dataset. The experimental results highlight the potential of the proposed method for unsupervised anomaly detection across various organs in digital pathology. Code: https://github.com/QuIIL/AnoPILaD.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AnoPILAD, a novel approach for unsupervised anomaly detection in lymph node metastasis within digital pathology images.  AnoPILAD combines a Latent Diffusion Model (LDM) with a vision-language model (VLM). The LDM learns a compact representation of normal images, while the VLM selects pathology-specific keywords to generate textual prompts. These prompts guide the LDM's reconstruction process, aiming to enhance the differentiation between normal and abnormal tissues.  The method is evaluated on a gastric lymph node dataset (from a local hospital) and a breast lymph node dataset (Camelyon16) to assess its generalization ability and robustness to domain shift. The results demonstrate that AnoPILAD outperforms existing anomaly detection methods in terms of AUC and AUPR, particularly in cross-organ anomaly detection scenarios.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's novelty lies in the integration of a VLM to generate pathology-specific text prompts for guiding the reconstruction process of an LDM. While diffusion models have been used for anomaly detection before (as acknowledged in the paper itself referencing [2]), the use of targeted textual guidance derived from pathology knowledge represents a potentially valuable extension. The idea of using pathology-specific keywords to drive the generation process is interesting. However, the improvement in performance is somewhat incremental. The concept is reasonable, but whether it's a genuine breakthrough is questionable. The reliance on a pre-trained vision-language model (CONCH) is also a limiting factor on the novelty.

*   **Significance:** The detection of lymph node metastasis is a critical task in cancer diagnosis and prognosis.  Unsupervised anomaly detection offers a way to overcome the limitations of data scarcity associated with fully annotated digital pathology datasets. Improving the accuracy and robustness of unsupervised methods contributes to the potential for wider adoption of AI in this domain.  The ability to handle cross-organ variations, as demonstrated by the evaluation on both gastric and breast lymph node datasets, is a valuable strength. However, the experimental results and overall gains are somewhat incremental, suggesting that the actual clinical impact may be smaller. While the improvements in the results might be statistically significant, the practical significance isn't very high. The lack of any analysis of potential biases is a weakness of the work.
* **Strengths:** The integration of domain knowledge, through the textual prompts, is a strength that allows the model to perform significantly better compared to models that do not leverage this information.

*   **Weaknesses:** The overall architecture is complex, and heavily relies on pre-trained models (Stable Diffusion, CONCH). The performance increase is also not massive, and the work could have benefited from more ablation studies.
    The choice of keywords and the process of validating them with a pathologist are not described in sufficient detail.
    A key limitation of the evaluation is the lack of true positives in the TNR calculations. The baseline methods used for comparisons are also not carefully chosen as more recent and sophisticated methods are now available for unsupervised anomaly detection.

*   **Impact:**  The paper's impact is likely to be moderate. It introduces a potentially useful technique that could be built upon by other researchers.  The results demonstrate the value of incorporating domain knowledge in anomaly detection, which could encourage further exploration of similar approaches in other medical imaging applications. However, the incremental nature of the improvements and the complexity of the approach may limit its widespread adoption.

**Score: 6**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a reasonably novel approach to unsupervised anomaly detection by integrating pathology-specific text prompts into a diffusion model framework. While the improvements demonstrated are appreciable (around 2-5% increase in overall accuracy metrics), they are incremental. The paper relies on existing pre-trained models, and the choice of baseline methods for comparison could have been more comprehensive, with greater inclusion of recent state-of-the-art techniques. The improvements shown are very small in magnitude and not very impactful. The method's complexity and reliance on domain-specific prompts may also limit its practical adoption. Therefore, while the work is a valuable contribution and the idea is sound, the relatively small gains and some limitations prevent it from being considered a truly exceptional or groundbreaking contribution.

- **Classification**: eess.IV
- **Score**: 6/10

### WangchanThaiInstruct: An instruction-following Dataset for Culture-Aware, Multitask, and Multi-domain Evaluation in Thai
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15239v1)
- **Authors**: Peerat Limkonchotiwat, Pume Tuchinda, Lalita Lowphansirikul, Surapon Nonesung, Panuthep Tasawong, Alham Fikri Aji, Can Udomcharoenchaikit, Sarana Nutanong
- **Abstract**: Large language models excel at instruction-following in English, but their performance in low-resource languages like Thai remains underexplored. Existing benchmarks often rely on translations, missing cultural and domain-specific nuances needed for real-world use. We present WangchanThaiInstruct, a human-authored Thai dataset for evaluation and instruction tuning, covering four professional domains and seven task types. Created through a multi-stage quality control process with annotators, domain experts, and AI researchers, WangchanThaiInstruct supports two studies: (1) a zero-shot evaluation showing performance gaps on culturally and professionally specific tasks, and (2) an instruction tuning study with ablations isolating the effect of native supervision. Models fine-tuned on WangchanThaiInstruct outperform those using translated data in both in-domain and out-of-domain benchmarks. These findings underscore the need for culturally and professionally grounded instruction data to improve LLM alignment in low-resource, linguistically diverse settings.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces WangchanThaiInstruct, a new human-authored Thai dataset designed for both evaluating and instruction-tuning large language models (LLMs). It addresses the limitations of existing Thai LLM benchmarks that often rely on translated English data and lack cultural and domain-specific relevance. WangchanThaiInstruct covers four professional domains (Medical, Law, Finance, Retail) and seven task types, emphasizing Thai cultural nuances. The paper presents two studies using the dataset: (1) a zero-shot evaluation demonstrating performance gaps in culturally-specific tasks and (2) an instruction tuning study that shows fine-tuning on WangchanThaiInstruct improves performance compared to using translated datasets. The authors also provide a reproducible dataset development process and release all artifacts, including the dataset, evaluation splits, and fine-tuned models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper addresses a clear gap in Thai language NLP resources by providing a human-authored instruction-following dataset that emphasizes cultural and domain-specific contexts. This goes beyond the typical translation-based approaches and existing closed-source resources like ThaiH6 and ThaiCLI (Kim et al., 2024). This constitutes a definite degree of novelty. However, human annotation for instruction-following datasets is not entirely novel in the broader NLP field (e.g., instruction datasets for English). The **real novelty lies in the *specific* adaptation and focus on Thai language and culture**, and providing a publicly available resource.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from addressing the need for better LLM alignment in low-resource and linguistically diverse settings. The zero-shot evaluation results clearly show that current models struggle with culturally and professionally specific Thai instructions. The instruction tuning study provides valuable practical guidance on how to improve LLM performance in this context. The finding that models fine-tuned on WangchanThaiInstruct outperform those using translated data has important implications for future LLM development in Thai and other similar languages. The release of all associated resources further amplifies the impact by providing baselines and enabling future research.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Human-authored data:**  A key strength is the commitment to creating a dataset *without* relying on LLM-generated or machine-translated content, which enhances authenticity.
    *   **Emphasis on cultural relevance:**  Focusing on cultural and domain-specific knowledge is a major strength.
    *   **Rigorous data development:**  The multi-stage quality control process (annotators, domain experts, AI researchers) seems thorough.
    *   **Comprehensive evaluation:**  The paper presents solid experimental results supporting the claims (zero-shot and fine-tuning studies).
    *   **Reproducibility:**  Releasing all resources is a major strength, making the work highly reproducible.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **LLM-as-a-judge reliance:** Reliance on LLM-as-a-judge for evaluation has inherent limitations, as the paper acknowledges, particularly if the underlying model's capabilities shift or the cultural comprehension is imperfect. The evaluation metric could be improved by incorporating more human evaluation alongside LLM-based measures.
    *   **Non-commercial use limitation:** The restrictive license (CC BY-NC) on the majority of the dataset might hinder commercial application, which could slow down practical deployment in some sectors. This restriction reduces, to some extent, the positive impact.

*   **Potential Influence:** WangchanThaiInstruct will likely become a valuable resource for researchers working on Thai NLP. It has the potential to:
    *   Drive development of more culturally-aware Thai LLMs.
    *   Encourage similar dataset creation efforts in other under-represented languages.
    *   Advance techniques for instruction tuning and evaluation in low-resource settings.
    *   Provide baseline models and insights for the Thai LLM community.

**Justification:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of Thai NLP by providing a high-quality, human-authored dataset for instruction following. Its focus on cultural relevance addresses a key limitation of existing resources. The experimental results and release of all artifacts make this a solid and reproducible piece of work. However, the dependence on the LLM-as-a-judge and the non-commercial license are limitations. The actual quantitative advancements shown by fine-tuning are solid, but they don't necessarily show disruptive performance.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The score of 7.5 reflects a solid contribution with clear value. The dataset is a genuine contribution to Thai language NLP. Its value lies in its human-centric and culturally-sensitive data creation for instruction-following, and the overall approach is rigorous and reproducible. The relatively moderate innovation level (human annotation is not new, even if applied particularly well here) and aforementioned limitations justify withholding the paper from a significantly higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### EMNLP: Educator-role Moral and Normative Large Language Models Profiling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15250v1)
- **Authors**: Yilin Jiang, Mingzi Zhang, Sheng Jin, Zengyi Yu, Xiangjie Kong, Binghao Tu
- **Abstract**: Simulating Professions (SP) enables Large Language Models (LLMs) to emulate professional roles. However, comprehensive psychological and ethical evaluation in these contexts remains lacking. This paper introduces EMNLP, an Educator-role Moral and Normative LLMs Profiling framework for personality profiling, moral development stage measurement, and ethical risk under soft prompt injection. EMNLP extends existing scales and constructs 88 teacher-specific moral dilemmas, enabling profession-oriented comparison with human teachers. A targeted soft prompt injection set evaluates compliance and vulnerability in teacher SP. Experiments on 12 LLMs show teacher-role LLMs exhibit more idealized and polarized personalities than human teachers, excel in abstract moral reasoning, but struggle with emotionally complex situations. Models with stronger reasoning are more vulnerable to harmful prompt injection, revealing a paradox between capability and safety. The model temperature and other hyperparameters have limited influence except in some risk behaviors. This paper presents the first benchmark to assess ethical and psychological alignment of teacher-role LLMs for educational AI. Resources are available at https://e-m-n-l-p.github.io/.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided EMNLP paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EMNLP, an Educator-role Moral and Normative LLMs Profiling framework designed to comprehensively evaluate Large Language Models (LLMs) simulating teacher roles.  EMNLP assesses LLMs across three key dimensions: personality measurement (using extended CPST and HEXACO-60 scales), moral ethical judgment (using novel teacher-specific moral dilemmas), and harmful content risk (through targeted soft prompt injection). The experiments, conducted on 12 LLMs, reveal that teacher-role LLMs exhibit idealized personalities, excel in abstract moral reasoning, but struggle with emotionally complex situations and are vulnerable to harmful prompt injection, particularly in models with stronger reasoning capabilities. It further investigates the impacts of model hyperparameters, such as temperature, on the behavior of the LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel framework specifically tailored to evaluate LLMs in the context of teacher role simulation.  While individual components (personality scales, moral dilemmas, prompt injection) are not entirely new, their combination and adaptation to the *education* domain, along with the inclusion of hyperparameter influence, constitutes a significant advancement.  The paper addresses a clear gap, as previous ethical and psychological evaluations of LLMs in specific professions have largely overlooked education. The creation of teacher-specific moral dilemmas appears to be a substantial contribution.

* **Significance:**  The study has significant implications for the development and deployment of educational AI.  The findings highlight crucial areas of concern, such as the idealized personalities of LLMs, their struggles with emotional nuances, and their unexpected vulnerability to harmful prompts, even when highly capable in reasoning. The discovery of a "competence-compliance tension," where more capable reasoning models become more vulnerable to exploitation, is particularly insightful. The work contributes to a better understanding of the ethical and practical challenges associated with using LLMs in education and offers a starting point for building safer and more aligned educational AI systems. The benchmark provides a valuable resource for future research.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The framework's three-pronged approach provides a more holistic assessment than previous studies that focused on single aspects.
    * **Domain-Specificity:**  Tailoring the evaluation to the specific ethical and psychological demands of the teaching profession is a key strength.
    * **Empirical Validation:** The experiments on a diverse set of LLMs offer valuable insights into their behavior in teacher roles.
    * **Identifies Key Challenges:** The paper clearly pinpoints the idealized personalities, the struggle with emotional complexity and competence-compliance tension.
    * **Well-structured and well-written:** The paper is generally well-organized and clearly articulates the research questions, methods, and findings.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Scope of LLMs:** While 12 LLMs are included, the range of model architectures and sizes within each category (reasoning and non-reasoning) could be broader for a more robust generalizability. Expanding to more diverse and recent models could strengthen the findings.
    * **English-Centric:** The study is exclusively conducted in English, potentially limiting its applicability to multilingual or culturally diverse educational settings. The framework could benefit from cross-lingual validation.
    * **Likert-Scale Limitations:** The reliance on Likert scales for personality assessment may introduce socially desirable responding bias in LLMs. More advanced assessment methods could mitigate this issue.
    * **Manual Prompt Engineering:** The soft prompt injection relies on manually crafted prompts, which might not capture the full range of potential vulnerabilities. Automated prompt generation techniques could enhance the robustness of the evaluation.
    * **Expert Annotation Subjectivity:**  While expert ratings provide nuanced evaluations, there is an inherent subjectivity in these assessments.  Inter-rater reliability analysis would strengthen the robustness of this component.

* **Potential Influence:** The EMNLP framework has the potential to become a standard benchmark for evaluating LLMs in educational roles. The identified challenges can guide future research and development efforts aimed at building safer, more effective, and ethically aligned educational AI. The competence-compliance tension highlights an important direction for future AI research.

**Justification:**

The paper is well-motivated, rigorously executed, and offers significant insights into the ethical and psychological aspects of using LLMs in education.  The novelty lies in the combination of existing and adapted techniques into a cohesive framework. The identification of a competence-compliance trade-off is particularly valuable. However, the limited scope of the models tested, the English-centric nature, and the inherent limitations of Likert scales and manual prompt engineering prevent the paper from achieving a higher score.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Explainable Knowledge Distillation for Efficient Medical Image Classification
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15251v1)
- **Authors**: Aqib Nazir Mir, Danish Raza Rizvi
- **Abstract**: This study comprehensively explores knowledge distillation frameworks for COVID-19 and lung cancer classification using chest X-ray (CXR) images. We employ high-capacity teacher models, including VGG19 and lightweight Vision Transformers (Visformer-S and AutoFormer-V2-T), to guide the training of a compact, hardware-aware student model derived from the OFA-595 supernet. Our approach leverages hybrid supervision, combining ground-truth labels with teacher models' soft targets to balance accuracy and computational efficiency. We validate our models on two benchmark datasets: COVID-QU-Ex and LCS25000, covering multiple classes, including COVID-19, healthy, non-COVID pneumonia, lung, and colon cancer. To interpret the spatial focus of the models, we employ Score-CAM-based visualizations, which provide insight into the reasoning process of both teacher and student networks. The results demonstrate that the distilled student model maintains high classification performance with significantly reduced parameters and inference time, making it an optimal choice in resource-constrained clinical environments. Our work underscores the importance of combining model efficiency with explainability for practical, trustworthy medical AI solutions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores the use of explainable knowledge distillation (KD) to create efficient and interpretable models for medical image classification, specifically for COVID-19 and lung cancer detection using chest X-ray (CXR) images and histopathology images.  The approach involves training high-capacity teacher models (VGG19, Visformer-S, AutoFormer-V2-T) and distilling their knowledge into a smaller, hardware-aware student model (OFA-595).  A hybrid loss function, combining ground truth labels with soft targets from the teacher, is used during distillation. Interpretability is addressed using Score-CAM visualizations to understand the models' decision-making process. The authors demonstrate that the distilled student model achieves high performance with significantly reduced computational cost, making it suitable for resource-constrained clinical settings.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper presents a practical and well-structured approach combining knowledge distillation (KD) with explainable AI (XAI) techniques for medical imaging.  The use of feature-based KD along with Score-CAM is a notable aspect. While KD itself is not new, the specific combination with XAI and the focus on efficient deployment within a medical context provides some novelty. The application of these methods across diverse datasets (CXR and histopathology) further enhances the novelty. However, the novelty of the architecture EEEA-Net-C2 and how it contributes to the teacher-student model is unclear. The usage of Score-CAM, a relatively well-established technique, doesn't represent a major methodological breakthrough.

**Significance:** The significance of the work lies in its potential to make deep learning more accessible and trustworthy in clinical settings. The focus on resource-constrained environments and the integration of explainability are crucial for real-world deployment. The paper addresses the "black box" problem of deep learning, which is a major barrier to adoption in healthcare. By demonstrating that a lightweight model can achieve comparable accuracy to larger models while also providing interpretable visualizations, the authors contribute to building trust among medical professionals. The multi-dataset validation shows promise for generalizability.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the challenges of deploying complex deep learning models in resource-limited clinical environments and the importance of model interpretability.
*   **Well-Defined Methodology:** The approach is clearly outlined, with detailed descriptions of the teacher and student models, the distillation process, and the XAI techniques.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The authors perform a thorough evaluation on multiple datasets, comparing the performance of different teacher-student configurations.
*   **Visualizations:** The use of Score-CAM visualizations provides valuable insights into the models' decision-making process.
*   **Focus on Practicality:** The emphasis on model efficiency and deployment in resource-constrained environments makes the work highly relevant to real-world applications.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Novelty:** The core techniques (KD, Score-CAM) are not entirely new, and the combination, while practical, may not be a significant methodological breakthrough.
*   **Explanation Depth:** While Score-CAM provides visual explanations, the paper lacks a deeper analysis of *why* specific regions are highlighted and how these relate to underlying medical knowledge.
*   **Generalizability Concerns:**  The limitations section acknowledges potential overfitting to the specific datasets used.  More rigorous evaluation on unseen datasets would strengthen the findings.
*   **Edge Deployment Details:**  While the paper aims for edge deployment, it lacks detailed discussion about specific hardware platforms, optimization strategies, and performance metrics relevant to real-world edge devices (e.g., power consumption, latency under varying workloads).
*   **EEEA-Net-C2 details:** There is no citation for this model. The use of this model is unclear and is not clear if the model is novel in the context of the provided research.

**Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more efficient and trustworthy AI solutions for medical image analysis. It highlights the importance of combining model compression with explainability to bridge the gap between research and clinical practice. The work could inspire further research in this area, leading to the development of more sophisticated KD and XAI techniques tailored to medical imaging applications.

**Score:** 6/10

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a solid, practical application of existing techniques (KD and Score-CAM) to an important problem (efficient and interpretable medical image classification). While the novelty is limited, the comprehensive evaluation, clear methodology, and focus on real-world deployment are valuable. The paper addresses a clear need within the medical field and offers a promising direction for future research. The weaknesses relating to limited novelty, lack of deeper explanation, generalizability concerns, EEEA-Net-C2 details and absence of edge deployment details, detract from a higher score. More in-depth analysis and consideration of deployment constraints would be beneficial to its overall impact. The missing EEEA-Net-C2 citation and the lack of detailed methodology on that approach reduces the validity of the research.

- **Classification**: eess.IV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Conflict-Aware Soft Prompting for Retrieval-Augmented Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15253v1)
- **Authors**: Eunseong Choi, June Park, Hyeri Lee, Jongwuk Lee
- **Abstract**: Retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) enhances the capabilities of large language models (LLMs) by incorporating external knowledge into their input prompts. However, when the retrieved context contradicts the LLM's parametric knowledge, it often fails to resolve the conflict between incorrect external context and correct parametric knowledge, known as context-memory conflict. To tackle this problem, we introduce Conflict-Aware REtrieval-Augmented Generation (CARE), consisting of a context assessor and a base LLM. The context assessor encodes compact memory token embeddings from raw context tokens. Through grounded/adversarial soft prompting, the context assessor is trained to discern unreliable context and capture a guidance signal that directs reasoning toward the more reliable knowledge source. Extensive experiments show that CARE effectively mitigates context-memory conflicts, leading to an average performance gain of 5.0\% on QA and fact-checking benchmarks, establishing a promising direction for trustworthy and adaptive RAG systems.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper.

**Summary**

The paper "Conflict-Aware Soft Prompting for Retrieval-Augmented Generation" (CARE) addresses a significant challenge in Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG): the conflict between external knowledge retrieved and the internal parametric knowledge of Large Language Models (LLMs).  When the retrieved context contradicts the LLM's existing knowledge, conventional RAG often fails, leading to incorrect generations.  The authors propose CARE, a novel RAG architecture consisting of a context assessor and a base LLM. The context assessor encodes external context into compact "memory embeddings" and is trained using grounded/adversarial soft prompting. This training aims to enable the assessor to discern unreliable context and provide a guidance signal to the LLM, directing it toward the more reliable knowledge source (either retrieved or internal).  Experimental results across various QA and fact-checking benchmarks demonstrate that CARE effectively mitigates context-memory conflicts, leading to performance improvements.

**Critical Evaluation**

*Novelty:* The idea of addressing context-memory conflict is not entirely new; several prior works have attempted this using adaptive retrieval, decoding strategies, and robust training. The key novelty of CARE lies in the following:

1.  *Context Assessor with Soft Prompting:*  Instead of directly fine-tuning the base LLM (risking catastrophic forgetting) or making hard retrieval decisions, CARE employs a dedicated context assessor trained via *soft prompting*. This allows for a more nuanced representation of the context's reliability, rather than a binary decision.
2.  *Grounded/Adversarial Training Strategy:*  The specific training strategy used for the context assessor is novel. The combination of grounded (using positive contexts when the LLM fails) and adversarial (using hard negative contexts when the LLM succeeds) soft prompting is a thoughtful way to teach the assessor to identify and handle conflicting information.
3.   *Preserving Generality*: CARE doesn't require fine tuning the based LLM.

*Significance:* The paper addresses a crucial weakness of RAG systems that limits their trustworthiness and robustness.  Context-memory conflict is a practical problem in real-world applications where retrieved information can be noisy or inaccurate. By improving the LLM's ability to resolve such conflicts, CARE contributes to building more reliable and adaptive RAG systems. The performance gains reported on several datasets support the effectiveness of the approach.  Furthermore, the design choices of CARE (using soft prompting, avoiding direct LLM fine-tuning) aim to preserve the LLM's general capabilities, which is a desirable feature for RAG systems that need to operate in diverse domains.

*Strengths:*

*   Clear problem definition and motivation.
*   Well-designed architecture with a novel training strategy.
*   Extensive experiments on multiple datasets.
*   Demonstrated performance improvements over strong baselines.
*   The ablation study provides insights into the contribution of different components.

*Weaknesses:*

*   Limited Scope: The experiments primarily focus on the top-1 retrieved passage scenario to isolate the context-memory conflict. While this is a reasonable starting point, it would be valuable to see how CARE performs with multiple retrieved passages (top-K), as this is more representative of real-world RAG systems. While the authors show results of using top-3 retrieved passages in the appendix, further exploration would enhance the paper.
*   Dependency on Correctness Signals: The training strategy relies on knowing whether the LLM is initially correct or incorrect. While this is simulated through a closed-book setting, the accuracy of this proxy is not perfect and could be a source of noise in the training process.
*   The paper doesn't deeply explore the failure cases or provide a more qualitative analysis of how the context assessor makes its decisions.
* The experiments don't account for situations when the retrieval module is entirely wrong (i.e., retrieves context irrelevant to the question).

*Potential Influence:*

CARE has the potential to influence the development of more robust and trustworthy RAG systems. The idea of using a dedicated context assessor with soft prompting could be adopted by other researchers and practitioners. The grounded/adversarial training strategy can be generalized to other conflict resolution problems in NLP. While the initial experiments are promising, further research is needed to explore CARE's applicability in more complex RAG settings, such as multi-document retrieval and multi-hop reasoning.

*Justification for Score:*

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to address a critical challenge in RAG. The design choices are sound, the experiments are extensive, and the results are promising. However, the limitations related to scope (primarily top-1 retrieval) and the reliance on correctness signals prevent a higher score. Given the contributions and potential impact, a score of 7 is justified.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Deep Think with Confidence
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15260v1)
- **Authors**: Yichao Fu, Xuewei Wang, Yuandong Tian, Jiawei Zhao
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown great potential in reasoning tasks through test-time scaling methods like self-consistency with majority voting. However, this approach often leads to diminishing returns in accuracy and high computational overhead. To address these challenges, we introduce Deep Think with Confidence (DeepConf), a simple yet powerful method that enhances both reasoning efficiency and performance at test time. DeepConf leverages model-internal confidence signals to dynamically filter out low-quality reasoning traces during or after generation. It requires no additional model training or hyperparameter tuning and can be seamlessly integrated into existing serving frameworks. We evaluate DeepConf across a variety of reasoning tasks and the latest open-source models, including Qwen 3 and GPT-OSS series. Notably, on challenging benchmarks such as AIME 2025, DeepConf@512 achieves up to 99.9% accuracy and reduces generated tokens by up to 84.7% compared to full parallel thinking.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Deep Think with Confidence":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DeepConf, a novel test-time method for improving the efficiency and performance of large language models (LLMs) on reasoning tasks. DeepConf leverages the model's internal confidence signals to dynamically filter out low-quality reasoning traces during or after generation.  It avoids the computational overhead associated with generating numerous traces, as is the case with self-consistency methods, while also addressing the diminishing returns that those methods often exhibit.  The approach requires no additional model training or hyperparameter tuning, and the authors demonstrate DeepConf's effectiveness across a range of reasoning tasks and open-source LLMs. The paper shows improved accuracy and significant reductions in generated tokens compared to full parallel thinking, particularly on challenging benchmarks like AIME 2025. DeepConf has both offline (analyzing existing traces) and online (stopping generation of poor traces) modes.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of using model-internal confidence to prune reasoning traces is not entirely new. Prior work has explored using token-level and sequence-level confidence estimates for quality assessment and filtering. However, DeepConf introduces a specific *local* confidence-aware filtering approach, based on the "lowest group confidence", which is innovative in its simplicity and effectiveness. The idea of local confidence is a contribution beyond global confidence estimates, as well as early stopping, which has been previously used in combination with self-consistency. Also, the ability to use both offline and online is a plus.
* **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical challenge in deploying LLMs for reasoning: the high computational cost of test-time scaling methods. DeepConf offers a pragmatic and easily implementable solution that can be integrated into existing serving frameworks. By reducing the number of generated tokens without sacrificing (and sometimes improving) accuracy, DeepConf significantly reduces the inference overhead, thus potentially making reasoning with LLMs more accessible and practical. The substantial reduction in generated tokens (up to 84.7% on AIME 2025) makes it quite significant. The approach also addresses the limitations of self-consistency which treats all traces equally, which allows low quality traces to disrupt the process, and that generating full traces before evaluation is computationally inefficient.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Simplicity:** The method is conceptually simple and easy to implement.
    * **Effectiveness:** The experimental results demonstrate significant improvements in both accuracy and efficiency.
    * **Generality:** The method is applicable to a wide range of reasoning tasks and LLMs, as shown by the diverse experimental setup.
    * **Practicality:**  The method doesn't require retraining or hyperparameter tuning and can be easily integrated into existing serving pipelines.
    * **Thorough Evaluation:** The authors perform extensive experiments and ablations.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Theoretical Justification:** The paper provides a solid empirical evaluation, but lacks a detailed theoretical analysis of *why* the lowest group confidence is such an effective heuristic. It would be useful to understand the underlying properties of language models that make this approach work.
    * **Potential for Over-pruning:**  While the experiments show good results, aggressive pruning could potentially lead to the premature termination of promising reasoning paths, especially in cases where the model initially struggles but eventually finds the correct solution. The adaptive sampling does help to mitigate this, but more discussion is warranted.
    * **Limited Focus on Failure Modes:** The paper shows generally positive results, but doesn't deeply analyze cases where DeepConf fails or even hurts performance. Further investigation into these failure modes could lead to improvements in the method.

* **Potential Impact:** DeepConf has the potential to significantly impact how LLMs are deployed for reasoning tasks, particularly in resource-constrained environments. It could enable more widespread use of test-time scaling methods by reducing their computational cost.

**Justification for Score:**

While the basic idea of confidence-based filtering isn't entirely new, the specific implementation of DeepConf, particularly the "lowest group confidence" heuristic and its online application, is a significant and practical contribution. The experimental results are compelling, demonstrating substantial gains in efficiency and accuracy. However, the lack of deeper theoretical analysis and a more thorough investigation of failure modes limits the overall impact. Therefore, a score of 8 is justified. The score is high because of the practical value and compelling results, but also leaves room for future work to further refine and understand the method.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### M-$LLM^3$REC: A Motivation-Aware User-Item Interaction Framework for Enhancing Recommendation Accuracy with LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15262v1)
- **Authors**: Lining Chen, Qingwen Zeng, Huaming Chen
- **Abstract**: Recommendation systems have been essential for both user experience and platform efficiency by alleviating information overload and supporting decision-making. Traditional methods, i.e., content-based filtering, collaborative filtering, and deep learning, have achieved impressive results in recommendation systems. However, the cold-start and sparse-data scenarios are still challenging to deal with. Existing solutions either generate pseudo-interaction sequence, which often introduces redundant or noisy signals, or rely heavily on semantic similarity, overlooking dynamic shifts in user motivation. To address these limitations, this paper proposes a novel recommendation framework, termed M-$LLM^3$REC, which leverages large language models for deep motivational signal extraction from limited user interactions. M-$LLM^3$REC comprises three integrated modules: the Motivation-Oriented Profile Extractor (MOPE), Motivation-Oriented Trait Encoder (MOTE), and Motivational Alignment Recommender (MAR). By emphasizing motivation-driven semantic modeling, M-$LLM^3$REC demonstrates robust, personalized, and generalizable recommendations, particularly boosting performance in cold-start situations in comparison with the state-of-the-art frameworks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes M-LLM³REC, a motivation-aware user-item interaction framework that uses large language models (LLMs) to enhance recommendation accuracy, especially in cold-start scenarios. The framework comprises three modules:  Motivation-Oriented Profile Extractor (MOPE), Motivation-Oriented Trait Encoder (MOTE), and Motivational Alignment Recommender (MAR). MOPE extracts motivational representations from limited user interactions using product descriptions and contextual information. MOTE abstracts items into motivational traits. MAR aligns user motivational profiles with item traits for recommendation. The authors claim M-LLM³REC demonstrates robust, personalized, and generalizable recommendations, particularly boosting performance in cold-start situations compared to state-of-the-art methods. The paper emphasizes deep motivational signal extraction from limited user interactions using LLMs, as opposed to solely relying on behavioral sequence length or semantic similarity.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its explicit focus on modeling user *motivation* for recommendation, particularly in sparse data scenarios. While LLMs have been used in recommendation before, framing cold-start as a motivation extraction problem and creating a specific architecture (MOPE, MOTE, MAR) to address this is a reasonably novel contribution.  Existing LLM-based recommenders often focus on either augmenting behavioral sequences or exploiting semantic similarity of items, neglecting the dynamic shifts in user motivation.  The Motivation-to-trait alignment approach is also a unique contribution that is more than just a fancy LLM reranking.

*   **Significance:** Addressing the cold-start problem remains a critical challenge in recommender systems. If the proposed framework genuinely improves performance in these scenarios while maintaining generalizability, it has the potential to significantly impact how recommender systems are designed, especially for platforms with many new users or emerging items. The interpretability provided by aligning user motivations with item traits also adds value. The claim that the proposed method focuses primarily on improving recommendation rather than improving efficiency which is a key feature in modern technology.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clearly defined problem:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing approaches in cold-start scenarios.
    *   **Well-structured framework:** The modular design of M-LLM³REC enhances its transparency and extensibility.
    *   **Motivation-focused approach:** The emphasis on motivation-driven semantic modeling is a strong conceptual contribution.
    *   **Experimental results:** The paper claims to demonstrate state-of-the-art performance on benchmark datasets.
    *   **Addresses Interpretability:** The alignment between motivations and item traits helps to improve the explanations of the model.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on LLMs:** The entire framework depends on the capabilities of LLMs. While LLMs are powerful, they are also prone to biases, inaccuracies, and high computational costs. The practicality of M-LLM³REC in real-world scenarios depends heavily on the availability and cost-effectiveness of LLMs. It would be interesting to test other SOTA LLMs besides GPT to show the generalizability of the methodology.
    *   **Prompt engineering:** LLMs are highly sensitive to prompt design, which means the reported performance might be influenced by the specific prompts used. The paper mentions the approach that was taken when it came to prompt and model selection.
    *   **Lack of ablation studies**: It is unclear that the improvements that are claimed are a direct result of the methodology that is proposed in the paper.

*   **Impact:**  If validated by further research, M-LLM³REC could influence the design of future recommendation systems, particularly those targeting cold-start and sparse-data situations. The emphasis on motivation-aware modeling might inspire new research directions in the field. Also the trade-off between efficiency and performance as it relates to the choice of model.

*   **Concerns:** The study relies on benchmark dataset and may not generalize to real work applications. How does the recommendation system deal with users with conflicting motivations? More information is needed for this aspect.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **6.5** to this paper. While the idea of motivation-aware recommendation using LLMs is promising and the framework is well-structured, several factors limit its overall impact. First, the results come exclusively from bench mark datasets, lacking generalizability to real-world applications. Second, the dependence on LLMs makes it susceptible to bias, inaccuracies, and high computational costs.

Score: 6.5

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 6/10

### TComQA: Extracting Temporal Commonsense from Text
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15274v1)
- **Authors**: Lekshmi R Nair, Arun Sankar, Koninika Pal
- **Abstract**: Understanding events necessitates grasping their temporal context, which is often not explicitly stated in natural language. For example, it is not a trivial task for a machine to infer that a museum tour may last for a few hours, but can not take months. Recent studies indicate that even advanced large language models (LLMs) struggle in generating text that require reasoning with temporal commonsense due to its infrequent explicit mention in text. Therefore, automatically mining temporal commonsense for events enables the creation of robust language models. In this work, we investigate the capacity of LLMs to extract temporal commonsense from text and evaluate multiple experimental setups to assess their effectiveness. Here, we propose a temporal commonsense extraction pipeline that leverages LLMs to automatically mine temporal commonsense and use it to construct TComQA, a dataset derived from SAMSum and RealNews corpora. TComQA has been validated through crowdsourcing and achieves over 80\% precision in extracting temporal commonsense. The model trained with TComQA also outperforms an LLM fine-tuned on existing dataset of temporal question answering task.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TComQA, a new approach for automatically extracting temporal commonsense knowledge from text using large language models (LLMs).  The authors propose a three-stage pipeline: 1) generating relevant questions from text given a target temporal property (e.g., duration, frequency), 2) validating the generated questions for relevance and correctness to ensure they actually tap into temporal commonsense, and 3) answering the validated questions using an LLM to extract the desired temporal commonsense. They fine-tune LLMs for each stage, create a dataset from SAMSum and RealNews using their pipeline, and demonstrate the effectiveness of their approach through crowdsourcing and evaluations on temporal question answering.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its **automated pipeline** for extracting temporal commonsense.  While extracting commonsense knowledge and using LLMs for QA are established research areas, the specific combination of question generation, question validation, and answer extraction tailored for *temporal* commonsense is a unique contribution. The novelty hinges on how well the individual components are designed and integrated, and whether the overall pipeline truly provides a significant improvement over existing methods of collecting and using temporal common sense knowledge. The authors also contribute two new metrics to measure quality of extracted questions. The contribution is good since a framework to automate extraction temporal commonsense is indeed a new paradigm.

* **Significance:** The significance is tied to the importance of temporal reasoning in NLP tasks.  Temporal commonsense is crucial for understanding events, resolving ambiguities in language, and creating contextually appropriate responses.  If TComQA proves effective, it could provide a valuable resource for improving various NLP applications, including dialogue systems, information retrieval, and event understanding. Building a curated QA dataset and improving LLM's ability to perform temporal reasoning also increases the practical value of LLMs. The paper provides a viable framework for developing a QA pair based on temporal commonsense which is the important contribution.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Well-defined pipeline:** The pipeline is clearly articulated and easy to understand. The modularity (question generation, validation, answer extraction) makes it easier to analyze and improve each component.
    * **Focus on question validation:** The inclusion of a question validation step is a crucial element for ensuring that the extracted information genuinely reflects temporal commonsense and not just surface-level linguistic patterns.
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents a good range of evaluation metrics. The study uses appropriate models in each step in the extraction pipeline and performs ablation studies to validate its value.
    * **Tackles a Hard Problem:**  Extracting *temporal* commonsense is inherently challenging due to its often implicit nature and the variability of temporal ranges.
    * **Dataset Generation:**  The approach addresses the scalability problem of manual annotation in commonsense data creation.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Dependency on LLMs:** The system's performance is highly dependent on the capabilities of the underlying LLMs.  The evaluation shows better numbers for Llama2 but the paper should investigate how TComQA would fare with models in different architecture. The authors could explore prompt engineering techniques for LLMs that can be leveraged by TComQA.
    * **Limited Dataset Types:** The dataset sources (SAMSum and RealNews) might introduce biases.  Exploring diverse sources could enhance the generalizability of the extracted knowledge. The quality of the TComQA dataset hinges on the accuracy of question generation and answer extraction, both of which rely on the LLMs. While crowdsourcing is used to validate the final dataset, a more in-depth analysis of the error types and their sources within the pipeline is needed.
    * **Potential for Bias in Crowdsourcing:**  Crowdsourcing, though helpful, is not immune to bias. There is a scope to look at different ways of eliciting label from people (ex: pairwise comparision).

* **Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7** to this paper.

*   The **novelty** of the automated pipeline, combined with the focus on *temporal* commonsense and the new validation metrics, warrants a good score. The creation of the dataset is also value addition.

*   The **significance** is moderate-to-high, as better temporal reasoning can benefit many NLP applications.

*   The **strengths** of the well-defined pipeline, question validation, comprehensive evaluation, and addressing a hard problem contribute to a positive assessment.

*   However, the **weaknesses** regarding the LLM dependence, dataset limitations, the lack of a deep error analysis, and the potential for bias in crowdsourcing prevent a higher score.  While the paper presents promising results, further investigation into the robustness and generalizability of the approach is needed to fully realize its potential. There needs to be a focus on a more diverse set of questions during training and inference so that TComQA is flexible.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### AmbiSQL: Interactive Ambiguity Detection and Resolution for Text-to-SQL
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15276v1)
- **Authors**: Zhongjun Ding, Yin Lin, Tianjing Zeng
- **Abstract**: Text-to-SQL systems translate natural language questions into SQL queries, providing substantial value for non-expert users. While large language models (LLMs) show promising results for this task, they remain error-prone. Query ambiguity has been recognized as a major obstacle for LLM-based Text-to-SQL systems, leading to misinterpretation of user intent and inaccurate SQL generation. We demonstrate AmbiSQL, an interactive system that automatically detects query ambiguities and guides users through intuitive multiple-choice questions to clarify their intent. Our approach introduces a fine-grained ambiguity taxonomy for identifying ambiguities that affect database element mapping and LLM reasoning, then incorporates user feedback to rewrite ambiguous questions. Evaluation on an ambiguous query dataset shows that AmbiSQL achieves 87.2% precision in ambiguity detection and improves SQL exact match accuracy by 50% when integrated with Text-to-SQL systems. Our demonstration showcases the significant performance gains and highlights the system's practical usability. Code repo and demonstration are available at: https://github.com/JustinzjDing/AmbiSQL.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the AMBISQL paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AMBISQL, an interactive system designed to improve the accuracy of Text-to-SQL systems by addressing the problem of query ambiguity. AMBISQL employs a two-stage process: (1) Ambiguity Identification, where a taxonomy of ambiguity types (DB-related and LLM-related) is used along with in-context learning to identify potentially ambiguous phrases in user questions; and (2) Iterative Refinement, where users are presented with multiple-choice clarification questions based on the detected ambiguities. The system then rewrites the query based on user feedback and passes it to an underlying Text-to-SQL system for SQL generation. The paper demonstrates that integrating AMBISQL with an existing Text-to-SQL system significantly improves SQL exact match accuracy. The system is evaluated on a dataset constructed from BIRD and TAG benchmarks, achieving high precision in ambiguity detection and a notable increase in SQL generation accuracy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its comprehensive taxonomy of ambiguity types that incorporates both database element mapping and LLM reasoning aspects. While previous work has addressed query ambiguity in Text-to-SQL, AMBISQL's taxonomy is arguably more granular and explicitly acknowledges the role of LLM-specific ambiguities.  The integration of interactive clarification, guided by a taxonomy, with a LLM-powered Text-to-SQL system, is a notable contribution.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical challenge in Text-to-SQL systems: how to handle query ambiguity that leads to inaccurate SQL generation. AMBISQL offers a practical approach for improving the usability and reliability of Text-to-SQL systems in real-world scenarios. The demonstrated improvements in exact match accuracy are significant, highlighting the potential of interactive ambiguity resolution. The work acknowledges the limitations of relying solely on LLMs for resolving ambiguity and demonstrates the value of incorporating user feedback.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Taxonomy:** The ambiguity taxonomy is well-defined and addresses a broader range of ambiguity types than many existing approaches.
    *   **Interactive Approach:**  The interactive clarification process allows users to guide the system towards a more accurate interpretation of their intent.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The paper presents compelling experimental results, demonstrating significant improvements in SQL generation accuracy.
    *   **Practical Implementation:** The described system is readily deployable as a preprocessing module for existing Text-to-SQL systems.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependency on User Input:**  The effectiveness of AMBISQL depends on the user's ability to accurately answer the clarification questions. While the multiple-choice format mitigates this to some extent, it's still a potential point of failure. The quality of clarity questions is therefore important.
    *   **Potential for Increased Interaction Overhead:**  While the paper doesn't explicitly address this, the interactive clarification process could add overhead for the user, especially for complex queries with multiple ambiguities. It is important to ensure a streamlined workflow that does not deter users.
    *   **Dataset Limitations:**  Although the paper constructs an ambiguity test set, there needs to be an evaluation of this with more datasets. There is also the potential for bias in dataset selection.
*   **Potential Influence:** AMBISQL has the potential to influence the development of more robust and user-friendly Text-to-SQL systems. The taxonomy of ambiguity types could serve as a valuable framework for future research in this area.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper is grounded in previous work, its contribution lies in its comprehensive taxonomy and its effective integration of interactive clarification with LLM-based Text-to-SQL systems.  The demonstrated improvements in SQL generation accuracy are compelling. However, the reliance on user input and the potential for increased interaction overhead are limitations. The significance of this work is that it bridges the gap from pure automated methods and allows user's intent to be better captured which contributes to a better result.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.DB
- **Score**: 7/10

### Adversarial Attacks against Neural Ranking Models via In-Context Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15283v1)
- **Authors**: Amin Bigdeli, Negar Arabzadeh, Ebrahim Bagheri, Charles L. A. Clarke
- **Abstract**: While neural ranking models (NRMs) have shown high effectiveness, they remain susceptible to adversarial manipulation. In this work, we introduce Few-Shot Adversarial Prompting (FSAP), a novel black-box attack framework that leverages the in-context learning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate high-ranking adversarial documents. Unlike previous approaches that rely on token-level perturbations or manual rewriting of existing documents, FSAP formulates adversarial attacks entirely through few-shot prompting, requiring no gradient access or internal model instrumentation.
