By conditioning the LLM on a small support set of previously observed harmful examples, FSAP synthesizes grammatically fluent and topically coherent documents that subtly embed false or misleading information and rank competitively against authentic content. We instantiate FSAP in two modes: FSAP-IntraQ, which leverages harmful examples from the same query to enhance topic fidelity, and FSAP-InterQ, which enables broader generalization by transferring adversarial patterns across unrelated queries. Our experiments on the TREC 2020 and 2021 Health Misinformation Tracks, using four diverse neural ranking models, reveal that FSAP-generated documents consistently outrank credible, factually accurate documents. Furthermore, our analysis demonstrates that these adversarial outputs exhibit strong stance alignment and low detectability, posing a realistic and scalable threat to neural retrieval systems. FSAP also effectively generalizes across both proprietary and open-source LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Few-Shot Adversarial Prompting (FSAP), a novel black-box attack framework that leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) for generating high-ranking adversarial documents. Unlike previous attacks relying on token-level perturbations, FSAP formulates attacks entirely through few-shot prompting, needing no gradient access or internal model instrumentation. The LLM is conditioned on a small set of previously observed harmful examples to synthesize fluent documents that subtly embed false information and rank competitively against authentic content. Two modes of FSAP are presented: FSAPIntraQ leverages harmful examples from the same query, and FSAPInterQ transfers adversarial patterns across unrelated queries. Experiments on the TREC Health Misinformation Tracks demonstrate FSAP-generated documents outrank credible documents, exhibit strong stance alignment, and low detectability. The method effectively generalizes across both proprietary and open-source LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents a significant and timely contribution to the field of adversarial information retrieval. The core novelty lies in the use of in-context learning of LLMs to generate adversarial documents. Prior work has largely focused on manipulating existing documents or crafting query-specific attacks. FSAP offers a fundamentally different approach, leveraging the generative capabilities of LLMs to create entirely new adversarial content, making it potentially more scalable and difficult to detect.

**Strengths:**

*   **Novel Approach:** The use of few-shot prompting to generate adversarial documents is a novel and compelling idea. It moves beyond simple token or sentence manipulations to more sophisticated content generation.
*   **Realistic Threat Model:** FSAP operates under a realistic black-box threat model, requiring no gradient access or internal model instrumentation. This makes the attack more practical and applicable to real-world scenarios.
*   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experimental results are comprehensive and convincing, demonstrating the effectiveness of FSAP in outranking credible documents across various neural ranking models and datasets. The analysis of stance alignment and detectability further strengthens the findings.
*   **Generalizability:** The demonstration that FSAP generalizes across different LLMs (both proprietary and open-source) highlights the robustness and transferability of the approach.
*   **Addresses a Relevant Problem:** The paper tackles a critical problem in information retrieval: the vulnerability of ranking models to adversarial attacks, particularly in sensitive domains like health.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Reliance on Harmful Examples:** While the paper argues the approach is scalable, it relies on having some initial examples of harmful query-document pairs. The process of obtaining such examples in practice might be difficult or require significant human effort. The source of those initial adversarial document is left unclear.
*   **Evaluation Limited to Health Domain:** The evaluation is focused solely on the TREC Health Misinformation Tracks. While this is a relevant domain, it is important to assess the generalizability of FSAP to other domains and types of information retrieval tasks.
*   **Absence of Comprehensive Defense Strategies:** The paper focuses primarily on the attack itself. While it analyzes detectability, it does not propose or evaluate specific defense mechanisms to mitigate the threat posed by FSAP.
*   **Limited Discussion on Ethical Implications:** Generating and deploying adversarial attacks, even for research purposes, raises ethical concerns. The paper could benefit from a more thorough discussion of the ethical implications of FSAP and potential safeguards to prevent its misuse.
*   **Comparison to More Recent LLM Counterfactual Generation Techniques:**  The LLM research landscape is evolving very rapidly. While the cited works are appropriate, the paper would benefit from a comparison to newer LLM-based counterfactual generation techniques and attack strategies that have been introduced even in the last few months.

**Significance:**

The paper's findings have significant implications for the robustness and trustworthiness of information retrieval systems. The demonstration that LLMs can be effectively used to generate adversarial content that evades detection highlights the need for developing more robust ranking models and defense mechanisms. FSAP provides a valuable tool for researchers to study the vulnerabilities of NRMs and develop strategies to mitigate these risks.

**Score:** 7.5

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to generating adversarial documents using LLMs. The empirical results are strong, and the analysis is thorough. However, the paper could be improved by addressing the limitations outlined above, including expanding the evaluation to other domains, exploring defense strategies, and providing a more comprehensive discussion of the ethical implications. Furthermore, it lacks a more complete assessment compared to more recent advances and techniques in the rapidly changing LLM landscape. Addressing these limitations would significantly enhance the impact and significance of the work.

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Multiple Memory Systems for Enhancing the Long-term Memory of Agent
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15294v1)
- **Authors**: Gaoke Zhang, Bo Wang, Yunlong Ma, Dongming Zhao, Zifei Yu
- **Abstract**: An agent powered by large language models have achieved impressive results, but effectively handling the vast amounts of historical data generated during interactions remains a challenge. The current approach is to design a memory module for the agent to process these data. However, existing methods, such as MemoryBank and A-MEM, have poor quality of stored memory content, which affects recall performance and response quality. In order to better construct high-quality long-term memory content, we have designed a multiple memory system (MMS) inspired by cognitive psychology theory. The system processes short-term memory to multiple long-term memory fragments, and constructs retrieval memory units and contextual memory units based on these fragments, with a one-to-one correspondence between the two. During the retrieval phase, MMS will match the most relevant retrieval memory units based on the user's query. Then, the corresponding contextual memory units is obtained as the context for the response stage to enhance knowledge, thereby effectively utilizing historical data. Experiments on LoCoMo dataset compared our method with three others, proving its effectiveness. Ablation studies confirmed the rationality of our memory units. We also analyzed the robustness regarding the number of selected memory segments and the storage overhead, demonstrating its practical value.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a Multiple Memory System (MMS) designed to improve the long-term memory capabilities of agents powered by Large Language Models (LLMs).  The MMS is inspired by cognitive psychology theories, specifically Tulving's memory system classification and the encoding specificity principle. The system processes short-term memory into multiple long-term memory fragments (keywords, cognitive perspectives, episodic memory, and semantic memory).  It then constructs retrieval memory units (for relevance matching) and contextual memory units (for knowledge enhancement during response generation).  Experiments on the LoCoMo dataset demonstrate improved retrieval and generation performance compared to existing methods like Naive RAG, MemoryBank, and A-MEM. The paper also includes ablation studies to validate the design of the memory units and analysis of robustness and overhead.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Inspired by Cognitive Psychology:**  A key strength is the foundation in cognitive psychology theories.  Drawing on Tulving's memory system classification and the encoding specificity principle provides a more theoretically grounded approach to memory design compared to purely engineering-driven methods.  This offers a principled way to structure and process memory content.
*   **Multi-Fragment Approach:** The decomposition of short-term memory into multiple fragments (keywords, cognitive perspectives, episodic, semantic) is a good way to capture different aspects of the information.  This is more sophisticated than simply storing summaries or keywords. The separation of retrieval and contextual units addresses the needs of retrieval and response stages separately.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents a thorough experimental evaluation on the LoCoMo dataset, including comparisons to strong baselines and ablation studies. The use of various metrics (Recall@N, F1, BLEU-1) provides a well-rounded assessment of performance. Robustness analysis regarding the number of memory segments and overhead is also good.
*   **Clear and Well-Written:** The paper is generally well-written and clearly explains the proposed method and experimental setup.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Novelty:** While the approach is theoretically motivated and shows performance gains, the individual components are not entirely novel.  Using LLMs to extract keywords, summaries, and different perspectives is a common practice.  The novelty mainly lies in the specific combination and the cognitive psychology underpinnings.
*   **Limited Generalization Concerns:** The experiments are conducted on a single dataset (LoCoMo).  It's important to assess how well the MMS generalizes to other datasets and tasks involving different types of dialogue and knowledge.  LoCoMo is dialogue-focused, so performance on tasks requiring more complex reasoning or external knowledge integration needs further investigation.
*   **Reliance on LLMs:** The MMS heavily relies on LLMs for memory fragment extraction and processing. The performance of the MMS is therefore intricately tied to the quality and biases of the underlying LLMs. The paper does not deeply analyze how the LLM choices affect the performance and robustness of the MMS itself. If all these processes depend heavily on the LLM's specific capabilities, it would limit the portability or broader usability of MMS.
*   **Scalability Concerns:** The analysis of overhead (token and latency) is limited. While the authors claim practical value, a more detailed discussion of the scalability of MMS to very large datasets and real-time applications is needed.  How does the latency increase as the number of memory fragments grows?

**Significance and Potential Impact:**

The paper makes a contribution to the growing field of memory-enhanced LLM agents. By incorporating cognitive psychology principles, the MMS offers a more structured and principled approach to long-term memory management. The improved performance on the LoCoMo dataset suggests that the method can be effective in enhancing dialogue agents. The research provides insights into how to leverage different types of memory fragments and design effective memory architectures for LLMs. The focus on retrieval units and contextual units will inform future research.

**Justification of Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7/10**.

*   **Justification for 7:**
    *   The integration of cognitive psychology is a strong point.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate improvements over baselines.
    *   The method is clearly explained.
    *   The novelty is somewhat incremental.
    *   More generalization experiments are needed.
    *   Dependency on LLM capabilities and scalability issues require further analysis.

The score reflects a solid contribution that improves upon existing methods with a principled approach, but that requires further validation and exploration to fully realize its potential impact.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### MLLMRec: Exploring the Potential of Multimodal Large Language Models in Recommender Systems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15304v1)
- **Authors**: Yuzhuo Dang, Xin Zhang, Zhiqiang Pan, Yuxiao Duan, Wanyu Chen, Fei Cai, Honghui Chen
- **Abstract**: Multimodal recommendation typically combines the user behavioral data with the modal features of items to reveal user's preference, presenting superior performance compared to the conventional recommendations. However, existing methods still suffer from two key problems: (1) the initialization methods of user multimodal representations are either behavior-unperceived or noise-contaminated, and (2) the KNN-based item-item graph contains noisy edges with low similarities and lacks audience co-occurrence relationships. To address such issues, we propose MLLMRec, a novel MLLM-driven multimodal recommendation framework with two item-item graph refinement strategies. On the one hand, the item images are first converted into high-quality semantic descriptions using an MLLM, which are then fused with the textual metadata of items. Then, we construct a behavioral description list for each user and feed it into the MLLM to reason about the purified user preference containing interaction motivations. On the other hand, we design the threshold-controlled denoising and topology-aware enhancement strategies to refine the suboptimal item-item graph, thereby enhancing the item representation learning. Extensive experiments on three publicly available datasets demonstrate that MLLMRec achieves the state-of-the-art performance with an average improvement of 38.53% over the best baselines.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "MLLMRec: Exploring the Potential of Multimodal Large Language Models in Recommender Systems" proposes a novel multimodal recommendation framework that leverages Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) to improve recommendation accuracy. The core idea is to use MLLMs to reason about user preferences by converting item images into semantic descriptions, fusing them with textual metadata, and then using these descriptions in a prompt to extract user preferences from historical interaction sequences.  The paper also introduces two graph refinement strategies: threshold-controlled denoising and topology-aware enhancement to improve the item-item graph learning.  The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of their approach on three public datasets, showing significant improvements over existing state-of-the-art methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the innovative use of MLLMs for user preference reasoning directly from multimodal data (images and text) and user interaction sequences *before* the user representation construction.  Existing works typically use LLMs to augment existing features or build user representations through other means (e.g., collaborative filtering). This "reasoning before embedding" approach is a key differentiator. The graph refinement strategies, while based on existing graph learning techniques, are tailored to the specific challenges of multimodal recommendation, particularly the noisy edges and missing co-occurrence relationships within item-item graphs. Combining these two aspects is also a novel contribution.
* **Significance:** The paper's findings are significant for several reasons:

    *   It demonstrates the potential of MLLMs to enhance recommendation systems beyond simple feature augmentation.
    *   It addresses the critical problem of noisy and inaccurate user representations in multimodal recommendation by directly reasoning about user preferences.
    *   It provides effective graph refinement strategies to improve item representation learning in multimodal settings.
    *   The significant performance gains over state-of-the-art baselines on multiple datasets highlight the practical value of the proposed approach. The ablation studies are particularly insightful, demonstrating the contribution of each component of the framework. The transfer of the denoising & enhancement graph refinement techniques to other models validates their usefulness.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed approach.
    *   The experimental evaluation is comprehensive, with thorough comparisons to state-of-the-art baselines and ablation studies.
    *   The code availability promotes reproducibility and further research.
    *   The analysis of hyperparameter sensitivity is valuable for practical implementation.
    *   The paper addresses a very important and challenging problem in recommendation systems and offers an effective solution.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Computational cost: The paper uses a reasonably large MLLM (Gemma3-27b).  While the paper focuses on recommendation accuracy, it does not fully analyze the computational cost of using MLLMs, which may be a barrier to adoption in some resource-constrained settings. A discussion of trade-offs between accuracy and computational cost would strengthen the paper. It doesn't sufficiently explore other possible architectures of LLMs beyond the chosen Gemma, like more recent Mistral family variants.
    *   Limited dataset diversity: While the paper uses three Amazon datasets, these are all from the same source and may not fully represent the diversity of real-world recommendation scenarios. Evaluating the approach on other datasets with different characteristics would increase the generalizability of the findings.
    *   Dependency on pre-trained models: The performance of MLLMRec depends on the quality of the pre-trained MLLM and the Sentence-Transformer. While this is common in modern NLP research, it's important to acknowledge this dependency and discuss the potential impact of using different pre-trained models.

* **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of multimodal recommendation by demonstrating a novel and effective approach to user preference reasoning and item representation learning. The proposed framework can be adopted by other researchers and practitioners to improve the accuracy and personalization of recommendation systems. The graph refinement techniques are general and could be applied to other graph-based recommendation models. The use of user preference extraction from historical behavior is something that could be used independently in other approaches.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

The paper demonstrates a strong contribution with a novel architecture and impressive performance gains and good ablation study.  The use of MLLMs for direct user preference reasoning, combined with effective graph refinement strategies, is a significant advancement.  However, the paper could be strengthened by a more thorough analysis of the computational cost, evaluation on more diverse datasets, a deeper discussion of the impact of different LLM architectures, and further examination of dependence on pre-trained model choices.  While the paper addresses an important problem with a practical solution, the limited scope of its evaluation prevents it from receiving a higher score. It remains a solid and valuable contribution nonetheless.

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Coarse-to-Fine Grounded Memory for LLM Agent Planning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15305v1)
- **Authors**: Wei Yang, Jinwei Xiao, Hongming Zhang, Qingyang Zhang, Yanna Wang, Bo Xu
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs) have driven growing interest in LLM-based agents for complex planning tasks. To avoid costly agent training, many studies adopted memory mechanism that enhances LLM with offline experiences or online trajectory analysis. However, existing works focus on single-granularity memory derived from dynamic environmental interactions, which are inherently constrained by the quality of the collected experiences. This limitation, in turn, constrain the diversity of knowledge and the flexibility of planning. We propose Coarse-to-Fine Grounded Memory (\Ours{}), a novel framework that grounds coarse-to-fine memories with LLM, thereby fully leverage them for flexible adaptation to diverse scenarios. \Ours{} grounds environmental information into coarse-grained focus points to guide experience collection in training tasks, followed by grounding of actionable hybrid-grained tips from each experience. At inference, \Ours{} retrieves task-relevant experiences and tips to support planning. When facing environmental anomalies, the LLM grounds the current situation into fine-grained key information, enabling flexible self-QA reflection and plan correction.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Coarse-to-Fine Grounded Memory (CFGM), a novel framework designed to improve the planning capabilities of Large Language Model (LLM)-based agents in complex environments. CFGM addresses limitations in existing memory mechanisms that rely on single-granularity memory derived solely from environmental interactions, which can be constrained by the quality of collected experiences.  CFGM grounds coarse-grained focus points derived from environmental information, then distills actionable hybrid-grained tips from experiences.  During inference, CFGM retrieves relevant experiences and tips. When encountering environmental anomalies, it grounds the current situation into fine-grained key information, enabling flexible self-QA reflection and plan correction.  Experimental results on AlfWorld, Webshop, and ScienceWorld demonstrate that CFGM outperforms existing memory-enhanced LLM agents.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The core novelty lies in the multi-granularity grounding approach.  Existing methods primarily focus on single-granularity memories. The idea of using LLM knowledge to guide the memory collection and utilization process is also reasonably novel. The framework integrates focus points, experience tips, and fine-grained reflection in a coherent manner. However, the individual components (experience replay, self-reflection) are not entirely new concepts in reinforcement learning or LLM agent research; the specific configuration and integration are the key innovations.

*   **Significance:**  The paper addresses a genuine limitation of current LLM agent systems: the reliance on the quality of experience and potential for reasoning derailment. The significant performance gains demonstrated across diverse environments suggest that CFGM is a practical approach for enhancing LLM agent performance. The ability to handle environmental anomalies and improve exploration efficiency are also important contributions. A practical benefit is that it preserves compatibility with powerful closed-source models.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the weaknesses of existing memory-enhanced LLM agents.
    *   **Well-Defined Framework:**  CFGM is well-explained and illustrated with clear figures.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The experiments are conducted on three diverse benchmarks with a strong set of baselines. The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of individual components.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experimental results demonstrate significant performance improvements compared to existing methods.
    *   **Addresses Practical Issues:** The framework enhances exploration and improves error correction.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Component Overlap:** The paper could better clarify the unique contributions of each component. While the integration is novel, the novelty of focus points, experience tips, and fine-grained reflection could be more clearly articulated. It would have been useful to test individual contributions with greater stringency.
    *   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:**  The paper provides empirical evidence but lacks deeper theoretical justification for the effectiveness of the coarse-to-fine approach. A theoretical justification might highlight potential improvements to the model by using various sources of information.
    *   **Reliance on GPT-4/GPT-40:** The performance of CFGM heavily relies on the power of GPT-4-turbo and GPT-40. While the model generalization study is promising, it doesn't fully address the concern that the framework may not be as effective with less capable LLMs. Future work might examine the method's robustness with smaller open source models.
    *   **Limited Scope in the Ablation Study:** The authors use limited metrics. Using metrics, such as "reasoning steps", would greatly increase the scope of the ablation study and allow for deeper insights into each component of the CFGM framework.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The idea of grounding coarse-to-fine memories with LLMs could have a significant impact on the field. It provides a structured way to leverage LLMs' internal knowledge for better memory management and error correction.  The framework could inspire future research on developing more robust and adaptable LLM-based agents.

**Overall:**

CFGM presents a compelling approach for enhancing LLM agent planning capabilities. The coarse-to-fine grounding mechanism is a novel and effective way to leverage LLM knowledge and improve memory management. The strong empirical results and practical benefits of the framework contribute significantly to the field. While there are some weaknesses regarding theoretical analysis and reliance on powerful LLMs, the overall contribution is substantial.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### VideoEraser: Concept Erasure in Text-to-Video Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15314v1)
- **Authors**: Naen Xu, Jinghuai Zhang, Changjiang Li, Zhi Chen, Chunyi Zhou, Qingming Li, Tianyu Du, Shouling Ji
- **Abstract**: The rapid growth of text-to-video (T2V) diffusion models has raised concerns about privacy, copyright, and safety due to their potential misuse in generating harmful or misleading content. These models are often trained on numerous datasets, including unauthorized personal identities, artistic creations, and harmful materials, which can lead to uncontrolled production and distribution of such content. To address this, we propose VideoEraser, a training-free framework that prevents T2V diffusion models from generating videos with undesirable concepts, even when explicitly prompted with those concepts. Designed as a plug-and-play module, VideoEraser can seamlessly integrate with representative T2V diffusion models via a two-stage process: Selective Prompt Embedding Adjustment (SPEA) and Adversarial-Resilient Noise Guidance (ARNG). We conduct extensive evaluations across four tasks, including object erasure, artistic style erasure, celebrity erasure, and explicit content erasure. Experimental results show that VideoEraser consistently outperforms prior methods regarding efficacy, integrity, fidelity, robustness, and generalizability. Notably, VideoEraser achieves state-of-the-art performance in suppressing undesirable content during T2V generation, reducing it by 46% on average across four tasks compared to baselines.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "VideoEraser: Concept Erasure in Text-to-Video Diffusion Models" introduces a novel training-free framework for preventing text-to-video (T2V) diffusion models from generating videos with undesirable concepts. The framework, called VideoEraser, consists of two stages: Selective Prompt Embedding Adjustment (SPEA) and Adversarial-Resilient Noise Guidance (ARNG). SPEA identifies and adjusts token embeddings likely to activate the targeted concept, while ARNG steers latent noise away from the target during the diffusion process and enhances robustness. The authors conduct extensive evaluations across object erasure, artistic style erasure, celebrity erasure, and explicit content erasure tasks. Results show that VideoEraser outperforms existing methods regarding efficacy, integrity, fidelity, robustness, and generalizability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a solid combination of existing ideas, but with several novel adaptations making it significant for T2V concept erasure. SPEA builds upon prompt engineering and embedding manipulation techniques from the text domain but tailors them for the T2V setting. ARNG is presented as a novel noise guidance approach, improving robustness to adversarial attacks and enhancing video consistency. The combination of these two stages, and its training-free nature, is novel and relevant. A major novelty lies in the comprehensive evaluation framework that goes beyond just efficacy, which most existing methods lack.

*   **Significance:** The problem of concept erasure in generative models is highly relevant, given concerns about misuse, copyright, and ethical content generation. The paper addresses a real challenge in T2V models. A training-free solution offers a significant practical advantage over fine-tuning approaches due to the resource demands of T2V training. The comprehensive evaluation metrics provide a more complete understanding of the trade-offs inherent in concept erasure techniques (efficacy vs. integrity, fidelity, etc.).
    The experimental section is quite thorough. It covers a good range of tasks, demonstrates superior performance compared to established baselines (SAFREE and negative prompting) and also tests the generalizability of the proposed methods across multiple state-of-the-art T2V models. The ablation study provides insights into each component of the framework.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Training-free approach: This makes the method readily adaptable and practical to use.
    *   Comprehensive evaluation: The authors define and evaluate several key metrics, providing a holistic view of the method's performance.
    *   Robustness: ARNG is designed to make the framework resistant to adversarial prompts.
    *   Generalizability: Demonstrated across several T2V architectures.
    *   Clear presentation: The paper is well-written and explains the proposed method and experimental setup in detail.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Computational Overhead: As noted by the authors, the method introduces some computational overhead compared to standard generation. While not a fine-tuning approach, the 1.4x increase in processing time should be considered.
    *   Abstract Concepts: Authors acknowledge less effective performance with broader, abstract content/style.
    *   Dependence on External Tools: The reliance on other object detection models to measure content in videos adds uncertainty, depending on their performance.
    *   Incremental Improvement: While the evaluations are comprehensive and comparisons fair, the individual algorithmic steps of VideoEraser could be viewed as being more evolutionary than revolutionary.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence the development of safer and more responsible T2V diffusion models. Its training-free nature, robustness to adversarial prompts, and comprehensive evaluation framework will be valuable for future research. The framework can be employed in downstream applications as well, such as content moderation pipelines.

**Justification for Score:**

While the core ideas build on existing concepts, the adaptation to the T2V domain, the robust adversarial defense, training-free approach, and especially the holistic evaluation framework make this paper a strong contribution. The limitations are clearly acknowledged. Given these points, a score of **8** is warranted. The work pushes the field forward and provides practical and methodological contributions to the problem of concept erasure in T2V models.
Score: 8
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### RETAIL: Towards Real-world Travel Planning for Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15335v1)
- **Authors**: Bin Deng, Yizhe Feng, Zeming Liu, Qing Wei, Xiangrong Zhu, Shuai Chen, Yuanfang Guo, Yunhong Wang
- **Abstract**: Although large language models have enhanced automated travel planning abilities, current systems remain misaligned with real-world scenarios. First, they assume users provide explicit queries, while in reality requirements are often implicit. Second, existing solutions ignore diverse environmental factors and user preferences, limiting the feasibility of plans. Third, systems can only generate plans with basic POI arrangements, failing to provide all-in-one plans with rich details. To mitigate these challenges, we construct a novel dataset \textbf{RETAIL}, which supports decision-making for implicit queries while covering explicit queries, both with and without revision needs. It also enables environmental awareness to ensure plan feasibility under real-world scenarios, while incorporating detailed POI information for all-in-one travel plans. Furthermore, we propose a topic-guided multi-agent framework, termed TGMA. Our experiments reveal that even the strongest existing model achieves merely a 1.0% pass rate, indicating real-world travel planning remains extremely challenging. In contrast, TGMA demonstrates substantially improved performance 2.72%, offering promising directions for real-world travel planning.
- **Summary**: Here is a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper "RETAIL: Towards Real-world Travel Planning for Large Language Models".

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the gap between current travel planning systems based on Large Language Models (LLMs) and the complexities of real-world scenarios. It identifies three key limitations: reliance on explicit user queries (vs. implicit needs), lack of dynamic environmental awareness and preference handling, and oversimplified travel plans lacking detailed POI information. To overcome these, the authors introduce a new dataset called RETAIL, designed to support implicit query understanding, environmental awareness, plan revision, and all-in-one travel plans with rich POI details. Furthermore, they propose a topic-guided multi-agent framework called TGMA to enhance real-world travel plan generation. The paper presents experimental results demonstrating the challenges faced by existing models and the improved performance achieved by TGMA.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces several novel aspects:
    *   **RETAIL Dataset:** The dataset fills a significant gap in the existing resources by incorporating implicit queries, environmental awareness, and detailed POI information. This is a valuable contribution as it pushes travel planning research towards more realistic scenarios. The dataset's statistics (size, types of queries, details of POIs) and the construction methodology support its value.
    *   **TGMA Framework:** The topic-guided multi-agent framework is a unique approach to travel planning, enabling more coherent and adaptive dialogue management and plan generation. The breakdown into Intent Detection, Overall Plan and Detailed Plan agents is a reasonable and well-structured approach.
    *   **Problem Definition:** Clearly articulating the three key challenges in current travel planning systems is a significant contribution in itself, helping the research community to focus on the critical issues.

*   **Significance:** The paper is significant because it highlights the limitations of existing LLM-based travel planning systems, demonstrating that even strong models perform poorly in real-world settings. The proposed solutions - RETAIL and TGMA - offer promising directions for future research. The improved performance of TGMA compared to existing baselines supports its effectiveness. The ablation study further helps to understand the contribution of each components in the framework.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined problem statement.
    *   Novel and valuable RETAIL dataset addressing key limitations.
    *   Well-structured TGMA framework with clear roles for each agent.
    *   Clear experimental setup and evaluation metrics.
    *   Demonstrated improved performance compared to strong baselines.
    *   The fine-tuning analysis demonstrates that the performance of smaller model can be significantly enhanced using Dialog-SFT.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The dataset creation and annotation process could benefit from further details, especially concerning the criteria used to determine "Feasibility" and "realism" of travel plans. More examples of RETAIL would also be beneficial.
    *   The experimental evaluation, while comprehensive, could delve deeper into the qualitative aspects of the generated travel plans. Error analysis and examples of plans generated by TGMA compared to baselines would add further insights.
    *   The reliance on GPT-4 for certain aspects of the system raises concerns about reproducibility and reliance on proprietary technology. Exploring alternative or open-source LLMs could enhance the practicality and accessibility of the work.
    *   The comparison with existing travel planning datasets could be more rigorous. A more formal evaluation of the dataset (e.g., coverage of common planning tasks) would strengthen the contribution.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field by shifting focus towards more realistic travel planning scenarios. The RETAIL dataset can serve as a valuable resource for future research, and the TGMA framework provides a solid foundation for developing more sophisticated travel planning systems. The work encourages the community to think beyond explicit queries and incorporate environmental factors and user preferences in a more dynamic and detailed manner.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper addresses a pertinent issue with significant real-world implications, presents a novel dataset and framework that show promising results, and provides a comprehensive evaluation. While some aspects could be strengthened, the paper contributes meaningfully to the field and offers valuable insights and resources for future research. Therefore, a score of 7 is warranted.

Score: 7
- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### DiagECG: An LLM-Driven Framework for Diagnostic Reasoning via Discretized ECG Tokenization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15338v1)
- **Authors**: Jinning Yang, Wen Shi
- **Abstract**: Electrocardiography plays a central role in cardiovascular diagnostics, yet existing automated approaches often struggle to generalize across clinical tasks and offer limited support for open-ended reasoning. We present DiagECG, a novel framework that integrates time-series and language modeling by enabling large language models to process 12-lead ECG signals for clinical text generation tasks. Our approach discretizes continuous ECG embeddings into symbolic tokens using a lead-independent encoder and quantization module. These tokens are then used to extend the vocabulary of LLM, allowing the model to handle both ECG and natural language inputs in a unified manner. To bridge the modality gap, we pretrain the model on an autoregressive ECG forecasting task, enabling the LLM to model temporal dynamics using its native language modeling capabilities. Finally, we perform instruction tuning on both ECG question answering and diagnostic report generation. Without modifying the core model, DiagECG achieves strong performance across tasks while maintaining generalization to out-of-distribution settings. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of each component and highlight the potential of integrating symbolic ECG representations into LLMs for medical reasoning.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary, novelty/significance evaluation, and justified score for the "DiagECG: An LLM-Driven Framework for Diagnostic Reasoning via Discretized ECG Tokenization" paper.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DiagECG, a novel framework designed to enable Large Language Models (LLMs) to reason about and generate clinical text based on 12-lead Electrocardiogram (ECG) signals. The key idea is to bridge the modality gap between continuous ECG signals and discrete language by discretizing ECG embeddings into symbolic tokens.  The framework comprises: (1) a lead-wise encoder with temporal inception enhancement blocks; (2) a fixed-scale quantizer (FSQ) to convert continuous embeddings into symbolic tokens; and (3) an autoregressive pretraining objective using ECG tokens, enabling LLMs to model temporal dynamics. The method is instruction-tuned for both ECG question answering (ECG-QA) and diagnostic report generation, demonstrating strong performance and zero-shot generalization.

**Novelty and Significance Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novel approach to ECG-LLM integration:**  The paper's main contribution is the discretized tokenization approach, moving away from continuous embeddings or complex contrastive alignment strategies. This facilitates seamless integration with LLMs, as the ECG data is presented in a format native to language models.
    *   **Lead-wise encoder:** The lead-wise encoder appears well-designed to preserve fine-grained temporal features, enhancing interpretability and fidelity of the representations. The TIE blocks are also a good feature for capturing long-range dependencies in the signal.
    *   **Autoregressive pretraining:** The autoregressive pretraining step allows the LLM to learn temporal dependencies within ECG data without requiring paired ECG-text data for alignment, which is a significant advantage in terms of data efficiency.
    *   **Strong experimental results:**  The experiments convincingly demonstrate the effectiveness of the method, achieving state-of-the-art performance on multiple ECG-QA and report generation datasets, with evidence of robust zero-shot generalization. The ablation studies clearly show the contribution of each component.
    *   **Clear and well-written:** The paper is well-structured and clearly explains the different components of the framework.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental novelty:** While the overall framework is novel, each component builds upon existing techniques. The inception blocks, quantization, and autoregressive pretraining have all been used in other contexts.  The core novelty lies in *how* they are combined and applied to the ECG-LLM integration problem.
    *   **Offline Processing:** As noted in the conclusion, the current implementation does not support real-time ECG analysis.
    *   **Limited Interpretability beyond visualisations:** While attention maps are provided, deeper interpretability of why the model makes particular decisions on specific waveforms could be explored further. What are the exact features that drive each decision.

*   **Significance:**
    *   **Improved ECG-LLM integration:**  The proposed method offers a more direct and efficient way to integrate ECG signals with LLMs compared to previous approaches that rely on complex alignment or continuous embeddings.
    *   **Potential for clinical applications:** The ability to perform open-ended question answering and generate diagnostic reports from ECG signals has significant potential for clinical decision support and automation.
    *   **Generalizable approach:** The symbolic tokenization approach could potentially be applied to other types of physiological signals beyond ECG, making it a valuable contribution to the broader field of time-series-LLM integration.

**Justification:**

The paper addresses a relevant and challenging problem: effectively integrating ECG signals with LLMs for clinical reasoning. The discretized tokenization approach is a clever way to bridge the modality gap and enables more seamless LLM processing. The strong experimental results and ablation studies validate the effectiveness of the proposed framework. While each component is based on existing techniques, the integration and application to ECG analysis is novel. The potential for clinical applications and generalization to other physiological signals further enhances the significance of the work. The primary weakness is the limited interpretability of the models decisions beyond visualisations of attention.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### An Empirical Study on How Video-LLMs Answer Video Questions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15360v1)
- **Authors**: Chenhui Gou, Ziyu Ma, Zicheng Duan, Haoyu He, Feng Chen, Akide Liu, Bohan Zhuang, Jianfei Cai, Hamid Rezatofighi
- **Abstract**: Taking advantage of large-scale data and pretrained language models, Video Large Language Models (Video-LLMs) have shown strong capabilities in answering video questions. However, most existing efforts focus on improving performance, with limited attention to understanding their internal mechanisms. This paper aims to bridge this gap through a systematic empirical study. To interpret existing VideoLLMs, we adopt attention knockouts as our primary analytical tool and design three variants: Video Temporal Knockout, Video Spatial Knockout, and Language-to-Video Knockout. Then, we apply these three knockouts on different numbers of layers (window of layers). By carefully controlling the window of layers and types of knockouts, we provide two settings: a global setting and a fine-grained setting. Our study reveals three key findings: (1) Global setting indicates Video information extraction primarily occurs in early layers, forming a clear two-stage process -- lower layers focus on perceptual encoding, while higher layers handle abstract reasoning; (2) In the fine-grained setting, certain intermediate layers exert an outsized impact on video question answering, acting as critical outliers, whereas most other layers contribute minimally; (3) In both settings, we observe that spatial-temporal modeling relies more on language-guided retrieval than on intra- and inter-frame self-attention among video tokens, despite the latter's high computational cost. Finally, we demonstrate that these insights can be leveraged to reduce attention computation in Video-LLMs. To our knowledge, this is the first work to systematically uncover how Video-LLMs internally process and understand video content, offering interpretability and efficiency perspectives for future research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents an empirical study on how Video-LLMs (Large Language Models for Video) process and answer video-related questions. It uses attention knockouts – selectively disabling certain attention mechanisms within the model – to understand the internal workings of these models. The authors investigate the impact of three types of attention: language-to-video, video temporal, and video spatial attention, using global and fine-grained settings. Their key findings include: video information extraction primarily occurs in early layers (two-stage process), language-guided retrieval plays a more significant role than intra- and inter-frame self-attention, and certain intermediate layers are critical outliers.  The authors then demonstrate how these insights can be used to reduce attention computation in Video-LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**  The paper's primary novelty lies in its systematic application of attention knockouts to analyze the internal mechanisms of Video-LLMs. While attention knockouts are a known technique in LLM interpretability, its extension and rigorous application to the video domain is valuable.  The distinction between global and fine-grained settings, and the three specific attention knockouts explored (temporal, spatial, language-to-video), provides a structured framework for analysis. Identifying a clear two-stage processing pattern within Video-LLMs and highlighting the dominance of language-guided retrieval over more computationally expensive self-attention mechanisms contributes to the field. The application of these insights to reduce attention computation also adds practical value.

**Significance:** Understanding the internal workings of Video-LLMs is essential for improving their explainability, efficiency, and overall design. This paper offers practical insights into the following:
*   **Efficiency:** Knowing that early layers handle visual encoding and that language-guided retrieval is crucial helps optimize model architectures for faster inference.
*   **Interpretability:** Deciphering which layers contribute most to specific tasks aids in model debugging and understanding failure modes.
*   **Model Development:** The dominance of language-guided retrieval suggests avenues for improving spatio-temporal modeling by strengthening self-attention mechanisms or incorporating more efficient alternatives.

**Strengths:**

*   **Well-defined methodology:** The paper clearly describes the attention knockout techniques and the experimental setup (global vs. fine-grained).
*   **Systematic approach:** The study examines a range of Video-LLMs and benchmarks.
*   **Actionable insights:** The findings are not just theoretical; the paper demonstrates how they can be used to improve efficiency.
*   **Good clarity:** The paper is well-written and explains the experiments and results in a clear and understandable manner.
*   **Addresses a gap in the literature:** As the authors point out, there's a relative scarcity of interpretability studies for Video-LLMs compared to image-based MLLMs.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Scope of analysis:**  The study primarily focuses on attention mechanisms. While crucial, other factors contributing to Video-LLM behavior are not deeply investigated. A more holistic view could reveal even deeper insights.
*   **Generalizability:** While the paper tests several models, the findings might not perfectly generalize to all Video-LLM architectures. There's some model dependency inherent in the findings, particularly related to the layer-level outliers identified.
*   **Limited investigation of the identified "critical outliers":** The paper identifies important "outlier layers" but only provides limited insight into *why* these layers are so impactful. A more detailed investigation into the specific operations or features within these layers would strengthen the analysis.

**Overall:**

The paper represents a significant step towards understanding the internal mechanisms of Video-LLMs. It provides a solid empirical framework and offers valuable insights for future research and development. While some limitations exist, the strengths outweigh the weaknesses.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale for the score:**  The paper demonstrates clear novelty by methodically applying attention knockouts to Video-LLMs, a relatively unexplored area. Its significance is demonstrated by providing actionable insights that enhance the efficiency and interpretability of these models. While the scope of analysis could be broader and the generalizability further validated with a wider range of architectures, the study makes a valuable contribution to the field. Furthermore, an interesting avenue for future work would be to understand *why* specific layers act as "critical outliers". Therefore, considering both strengths and weaknesses, a score of 7.5 seems justified.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### A Survey on Large Language Model Benchmarks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15361v1)
- **Authors**: Shiwen Ni, Guhong Chen, Shuaimin Li, Xuanang Chen, Siyi Li, Bingli Wang, Qiyao Wang, Xingjian Wang, Yifan Zhang, Liyang Fan, Chengming Li, Ruifeng Xu, Le Sun, Min Yang
- **Abstract**: In recent years, with the rapid development of the depth and breadth of large language models' capabilities, various corresponding evaluation benchmarks have been emerging in increasing numbers. As a quantitative assessment tool for model performance, benchmarks are not only a core means to measure model capabilities but also a key element in guiding the direction of model development and promoting technological innovation. We systematically review the current status and development of large language model benchmarks for the first time, categorizing 283 representative benchmarks into three categories: general capabilities, domain-specific, and target-specific. General capability benchmarks cover aspects such as core linguistics, knowledge, and reasoning; domain-specific benchmarks focus on fields like natural sciences, humanities and social sciences, and engineering technology; target-specific benchmarks pay attention to risks, reliability, agents, etc. We point out that current benchmarks have problems such as inflated scores caused by data contamination, unfair evaluation due to cultural and linguistic biases, and lack of evaluation on process credibility and dynamic environments, and provide a referable design paradigm for future benchmark innovation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper information:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a comprehensive survey of Large Language Model (LLM) benchmarks, categorizing 283 representative benchmarks into three groups: general capabilities, domain-specific, and target-specific. It analyzes these benchmarks based on data sources, formats, evaluation methods, and metrics, highlighting their design motivations and limitations. The authors identify problems within existing benchmarks, including inflated scores due to data contamination, unfair evaluation arising from cultural/linguistic biases, and lack of evaluation on process credibility and dynamic environments. The paper concludes by offering a design paradigm for future benchmark innovation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** While survey papers are common, the scope and categorization of the benchmarks in this paper appear reasonably novel, given the rapidly expanding landscape of LLMs. The thorough analysis of data contamination, biases, and lack of dynamic evaluation represents a significant contribution, particularly for a field where benchmarks are crucial for progress.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in providing a structured overview of a very fragmented and rapidly evolving field. By systematically categorizing and evaluating existing benchmarks, the paper offers researchers a valuable resource for understanding the current state-of-the-art and identifying areas needing improvement. The insights into the limitations of current benchmarks (data contamination, cultural biases, lack of dynamic environments) are critical and timely, potentially influencing the design of future evaluation methodologies. The recommendation for a design paradigm for new benchmarks is impactful.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensiveness:**  The sheer number of benchmarks covered is a strength, providing a broad overview.
    *   **Structured Analysis:** The categorization and detailed analysis of benchmarks based on design principles, data characteristics, and limitations are valuable for researchers and practitioners.
    *   **Identification of Key Issues:**  Highlighting the problems of data contamination, bias, and lack of dynamic evaluation are important for guiding future research and ensuring the fairness and reliability of LLM evaluations.
    *   **Future Directions:** The suggested design paradigm for future benchmark innovation is potentially impactful and could help to promote the development of more robust and meaningful evaluations of LLMs.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Descriptive Nature:**  While comprehensive, the paper is largely descriptive. A stronger paper might have included a more in-depth comparative analysis between different benchmarks within each category, perhaps using some quantitative metrics to compare their properties.

    *   **Limited Practical Guidance:**  While the "design paradigm" is mentioned, the paper could benefit from more concrete examples of how to address the identified limitations. For instance, specific techniques for mitigating data contamination or creating more dynamic evaluation environments could be included.

    *   **Rapid Evolution:** The field of LLMs is evolving rapidly.  Some benchmarks might become obsolete quickly. Therefore, the paper's long-term relevance will depend on how well its proposed framework can adapt to new models and evaluation challenges.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper represents a significant contribution to the LLM field, given its comprehensive scope, structured analysis, and identification of critical challenges in benchmark design. However, the somewhat descriptive nature of the paper and limited practical guidance prevent it from reaching the very highest impact level. Thus, I assign the paper a score of 7.

**Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Unveiling Trust in Multimodal Large Language Models: Evaluation, Analysis, and Mitigation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15370v1)
- **Authors**: Yichi Zhang, Yao Huang, Yifan Wang, Yitong Sun, Chang Liu, Zhe Zhao, Zhengwei Fang, Huanran Chen, Xiao Yang, Xingxing Wei, Hang Su, Yinpeng Dong, Jun Zhu
- **Abstract**: The trustworthiness of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) remains an intense concern despite the significant progress in their capabilities. Existing evaluation and mitigation approaches often focus on narrow aspects and overlook risks introduced by the multimodality. To tackle these challenges, we propose MultiTrust-X, a comprehensive benchmark for evaluating, analyzing, and mitigating the trustworthiness issues of MLLMs. We define a three-dimensional framework, encompassing five trustworthiness aspects which include truthfulness, robustness, safety, fairness, and privacy; two novel risk types covering multimodal risks and cross-modal impacts; and various mitigation strategies from the perspectives of data, model architecture, training, and inference algorithms. Based on the taxonomy, MultiTrust-X includes 32 tasks and 28 curated datasets, enabling holistic evaluations over 30 open-source and proprietary MLLMs and in-depth analysis with 8 representative mitigation methods. Our extensive experiments reveal significant vulnerabilities in current models, including a gap between trustworthiness and general capabilities, as well as the amplification of potential risks in base LLMs by both multimodal training and inference. Moreover, our controlled analysis uncovers key limitations in existing mitigation strategies that, while some methods yield improvements in specific aspects, few effectively address overall trustworthiness, and many introduce unexpected trade-offs that compromise model utility. These findings also provide practical insights for future improvements, such as the benefits of reasoning to better balance safety and performance. Based on these insights, we introduce a Reasoning-Enhanced Safety Alignment (RESA) approach that equips the model with chain-of-thought reasoning ability to discover the underlying risks, achieving state-of-the-art results.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MultiTrust-X, a comprehensive benchmark designed to evaluate, analyze, and mitigate trustworthiness issues in Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). The benchmark utilizes a three-dimensional framework covering five aspects of trustworthiness: truthfulness, robustness, safety, fairness, and privacy.  It introduces two novel risk types related to multimodality: multimodal risks and cross-modal impacts. The benchmark includes 32 tasks, 28 datasets, and evaluations of 30 different MLLMs. The authors also analyze the effectiveness of 8 mitigation strategies and propose a novel approach called Reasoning-Enhanced Safety Alignment (RESA) to improve MLLM trustworthiness by incorporating chain-of-thought reasoning.  Experimental results highlight vulnerabilities in current MLLMs, reveal limitations of existing mitigation methods, and demonstrate RESA's effectiveness in improving trustworthiness while preserving general utility.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper makes a significant contribution by providing a holistic benchmark that goes beyond existing, often narrow, evaluations of MLLM trustworthiness. The introduction of "multimodal risks" and "cross-modal impacts" is a key advancement, recognizing that multimodality introduces unique challenges not present in unimodal LLMs. The categorization of mitigation methods within a machine learning system framework is also a novel and useful way to structure analysis. The development of RESA, while building on existing reasoning-based techniques, is tailored to address the specific trustworthiness concerns of MLLMs.
*   **Significance:** Trustworthiness is a critical concern for the responsible deployment of MLLMs. By providing a comprehensive benchmark, the paper enables more rigorous evaluations and facilitates the development of better mitigation strategies. The findings highlighting the gap between trustworthiness and general capabilities, as well as the unintended consequences of many existing mitigation strategies, are important for guiding future research directions. RESA's success in closing the trustworthiness gap while preserving general utility presents a promising avenue for further exploration. The work directly addresses a significant bottleneck for real-world adoption of multimodal AI systems.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Framework:** The three-dimensional framework provides a structured and detailed approach to assessing MLLM trustworthiness.
    *   **Novel Risk Types:**  Identifying and defining multimodal and cross-modal risks expands the scope of trustworthiness evaluation.
    *   **Extensive Evaluation:** The benchmark includes a large number of tasks, datasets, and models, making the evaluation results robust.
    *   **Mitigation Analysis:** The detailed analysis of existing mitigation methods provides valuable insights into their effectiveness and limitations.
    *   **Practical Solution:**  RESA offers a concrete approach for improving MLLM trustworthiness based on the analysis.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Subjective Metrics:** The benchmark relies on subjective metrics for some tasks, which may introduce bias and variability. Although the authors attempted to alleviate this concern by comparing human and GPT-4 ratings, further investigation would make the benchmark more robust.
    *   **Generalization of RESA:** The results for RESA are promising but further experiments are needed to prove the generalizability of RESA to different MLLM architectures, training paradigms and datasets.
    *   **Complexity:** Due to the comprehensive and complex nature of the benchmark, fully replicating the experiments and datasets could be challenging for other researchers.
    *   **Evolving Landscape:** The field of MLLMs is rapidly evolving, so it is inevitable that the benchmark will require updates to remain current.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has a high potential to influence future research in MLLM trustworthiness.  The MultiTrust-X benchmark will likely become a standard tool for evaluating new MLLMs and mitigation methods. The insights from the analysis of existing methods and the success of RESA will inspire new approaches to improving MLLM trustworthiness.  The work pushes the field towards a more holistic and responsible development of multimodal AI systems.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper's contribution is significant and timely. It is well-motivated, rigorously executed, and provides important insights into the challenges and potential solutions for building trustworthy MLLMs. The comprehensive benchmark, novel risk types, and successful mitigation strategy are major strengths.  The work is also very clearly written and well organized. The main limitation is the subjectivity of some of the metrics, which, if addressed, would further strengthen the contribution.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Confidence-Modulated Speculative Decoding for Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15371v1)
- **Authors**: Jaydip Sen, Subhasis Dasgupta, Hetvi Waghela
- **Abstract**: Speculative decoding has emerged as an effective approach for accelerating autoregressive inference by parallelizing token generation through a draft-then-verify paradigm. However, existing methods rely on static drafting lengths and rigid verification criteria, limiting their adaptability across varying model uncertainties and input complexities. This paper proposes an information-theoretic framework for speculative decoding based on confidence-modulated drafting. By leveraging entropy and margin-based uncertainty measures over the drafter's output distribution, the proposed method dynamically adjusts the number of speculatively generated tokens at each iteration. This adaptive mechanism reduces rollback frequency, improves resource utilization, and maintains output fidelity. Additionally, the verification process is modulated using the same confidence signals, enabling more flexible acceptance of drafted tokens without sacrificing generation quality. Experiments on machine translation and summarization tasks demonstrate significant speedups over standard speculative decoding while preserving or improving BLEU and ROUGE scores. The proposed approach offers a principled, plug-in method for efficient and robust decoding in large language models under varying conditions of uncertainty.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Confidence-Modulated Speculative Decoding for Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Confidence-Modulated Adaptive Speculative Decoding (CM-ASD), a new speculative decoding framework for large language models (LLMs).  CM-ASD addresses limitations of traditional speculative decoding by dynamically adjusting the drafting length and verification thresholds based on the drafter model's confidence. Confidence is estimated using entropy, logit margins, and softmax margins. The framework aims to improve decoding efficiency, reduce rollbacks, and enhance robustness under varying input complexities and model uncertainties. Experiments on machine translation and summarization tasks demonstrate speedups compared to standard speculative decoding while preserving or improving BLEU/ROUGE scores. The paper claims the method is a principled, plug-in approach applicable to existing Transformer-based models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using confidence to dynamically adjust drafting length and verification criteria is a worthwhile extension to existing speculative decoding methods. While speculative decoding is not new, the adaptive confidence-based approach to dynamically modulate drafting aggressiveness is a definite advance. The exploration of different confidence measures (entropy, margin, softmax) and their ensemble is also valuable. However, the individual components are inspired by techniques found in areas like active learning and control systems, and combining them for speculative decoding is where novelty lies. It is an incremental improvement, but still notable.

*   **Significance:**  The potential impact is considerable. Improving decoding speed without sacrificing output quality is crucial for deploying LLMs in latency-sensitive applications. The claimed plug-in nature and compatibility with existing models make it practically appealing. The experiments, conducted on standard datasets and models, showcase tangible improvements in speed and quality, suggesting real-world applicability.  However, the reported ROUGE score reduction on Abstractive Summarization should be highlighted; a thorough error analysis would provide more insight into this phenomenon.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The adaptive nature of CM-ASD is a clear advantage over static drafting lengths in existing methods.
    *   The use of model confidence (quantified by entropy or logit margins) is a principled approach to guide drafting.
    *   The framework is compatible with existing Transformer architectures, making it readily deployable.
    *   The experimental results show promising speedups while maintaining or improving generation quality.
    *   The ablations clarify the contribution of each design choice.
    *   The analysis of latency vs. throughput offers practical insights.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The improvement in ROUGE scores (summarization) is not as significant as the improvement in speed. In addition, a drop in performance when used on Abstractive Summarization is a concern. A deeper dive into the cause of this would be appreciated.
    *   The experimental evaluation focuses mainly on speed and BLEU/ROUGE. A more comprehensive analysis of factors like "hallucination" rates, output diversity, and fairness might reveal further insights.
    *   The selection of hyperparameters (*α*, *Thase*, *γ*, weights of ensemble confidence) requires careful tuning, making its ease of use less attractive.

*   **Justification of Score:**
    *CM-ASD presents a meaningful, incremental advance in speculative decoding. While speculative decoding frameworks have previously offered significant benefits in terms of accelerated inference, this enhancement demonstrates clear improvements that benefit practical implementation. Although further studies and analysis are crucial for verifying and understanding the effects of CM-ASD in distinct experimental conditions, it does represent a considerable advance. The adaptive confidence-aware strategy demonstrated in CM-ASD improves prior frameworks by accounting for uncertainties, and the experimental results are supportive.
    *CM-ASD's focus on a straightforward architectural implementation that requires little or no need for retraining makes it particularly intriguing, as does the integration with already deployed, trustworthy LLM pipelines. However, the absence of extensive assessments in a range of domains and a limited consideration of possible trade-offs, indicate further improvements are required.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### TrackRec: Iterative Alternating Feedback with Chain-of-Thought via Preference Alignment for Recommendation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15388v1)
- **Authors**: Yu Xia, Rui Zhong, Zeyu Song, Wei Yang, Junchen Wan, Qingpeng Cai, Chi Lu, Peng Jiang
- **Abstract**: The extensive world knowledge and powerful reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs) have attracted significant attention in recommendation systems (RS). Specifically, The chain of thought (CoT) has been shown to improve the performance of LLMs on complex reasoning tasks for RS. However, due to the fact that LLMs often suffer from hallucination issues, there is no guarantee that their reasoning CoT is effective. A key challenge is to further enhance the recommendation capabilities of LLMs through effective CoT reasonings. Therefore, we propose \textbf{TrackRec}, a framework designed to enhance reasoning capabilities of LLMs for RS. TrackRec specifically focuses on accurately inferring recommendation CoT \textbf{(RecCoT)} for user preference using the knowledge from LLMs. This RecCoT can serve both as an explanation for the LLM's completion of recommendation tasks and as auxiliary features to assist recommendation models in accomplishing recommendation tasks. TrackRec consists of a RecCoT generator $(G)$ and a RecCoT validator $(V)$. Furthermore, we design alternating feedback learning mechanism that $G$ undergoes direct preference optimization via feedback from $V$ to produce increasingly accurate RecCoT aligned with $V$'s standards. Meanwhile, $V$ is fine-tuned using the inference feedback from $G$ to enhance its validation capabilities in alignment with recommendation tasks. Through iterative alternating feedback learning between $G$ and $V$, TrackRec continuously improves the user preference analysis capability of $G$ and the validation capacity of $V$. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of our approach, showing that it surpasses state-of-the-art methods. Moreover, TrackRec has been deployed on a lagre advertising platform with hundreds of millions of users, achieving substantial gains.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the TrackRec paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TrackRec, a novel framework designed to enhance the reasoning capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) in recommendation systems (RS). It addresses the challenge of unreliable Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning in LLMs due to hallucination issues. TrackRec features a RecCoT generator (G) that infers user preferences and a RecCoT validator (V) that assesses the generated CoT. The core innovation lies in an iterative alternating feedback learning mechanism where G is trained to produce more accurate RecCoT based on feedback from V, while V is fine-tuned to better validate RecCoT based on the inferences from G.  The framework includes distillation (using a larger LLM) and Rec-tuning to align with user preferences. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of TrackRec through experiments on public and industrial datasets, showing it surpasses state-of-the-art methods and achieves substantial gains when deployed on a large advertising platform.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the iterative alternating feedback mechanism between the RecCoT generator and validator. While using LLMs for recommendations and CoT prompting are not entirely new, the specific design of this alternating feedback loop with preference alignment to refine both the CoT generation and validation processes is a significant contribution. Additionally, using S-DPO (Softmax-DPO) for preference alignment based on feedback from validator V is also a novel approach. The idea of initializing the generator with distillation from a larger model is a useful practical contribution. However, the core components (LLMs, CoT, reinforcement learning) are individual elements that are not themselves novel; their combination and interaction within the TrackRec architecture is where the novelty resides.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial problem in applying LLMs to recommendation: the inherent unreliability and potential for hallucination in LLM-generated reasoning. By explicitly aligning LLM reasoning with actual user preferences through the alternating feedback loop, TrackRec improves recommendation accuracy and robustness. The successful deployment on a large-scale advertising platform and the reported gains (2.3% revenue increase, 1.6% conversion rate improvement) underscore the practical significance of the work. The results on long-tail items are also significant. These aspects highlight the practical impact of the work, especially given the challenges of cold-start and data sparsity in real-world recommendation scenarios.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The iterative alternating feedback learning mechanism is well-designed and conceptually sound.
    *   The use of both public and industrial datasets provides strong evidence of the framework's generalizability and practical value.
    *   The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of each component (preference alignment, iterative learning, distillation, Rec-tuning).
    *   The clear articulation of the problem, the proposed solution, and the experimental setup enhances the paper's readability and credibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the deployment results are impressive, the details of the deployment environment and the comparison with the existing production system could be expanded for greater transparency.
    *   The model assumes behavioral data, and therefore will not be useful when dealing with new users that do not have this data.
    *   More in-depth analysis of failure cases (scenarios where TrackRec does not perform well) would be beneficial to further understand the limitations of the approach.
    *   Some results aren't statistically significant.

*   **Potential Influence:** TrackRec has the potential to influence future research in several ways:
    *   It provides a blueprint for effectively integrating LLMs into recommendation systems by addressing the challenge of unreliable reasoning.
    *   The iterative alternating feedback learning mechanism can be adapted to other tasks where LLM reasoning needs to be aligned with external data or user preferences.
    *   The work highlights the importance of using practical deployment as a way to guide the development of LLM-based recommendation solutions.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

TrackRec presents a substantial contribution to the field of recommendation systems by effectively addressing the challenge of unreliable LLM-generated reasoning. The iterative alternating feedback learning mechanism is a novel and well-designed solution. The successful deployment on a large-scale advertising platform is a clear demonstration of its practical value. While the approach builds upon existing techniques, it combines them in a novel and effective manner to produce a significant improvement in real-world performance. Although there are minor weaknesses, the overall contribution and potential impact justify a high score.

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 8/10

### Exploiting Vocabulary Frequency Imbalance in Language Model Pre-training
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.15390v1)
- **Authors**: Woojin Chung, Jeonghoon Kim
- **Abstract**: Large language models are trained with tokenizers, and the resulting token distribution is highly imbalanced: a few words dominate the stream while most occur rarely. Recent practice favors ever-larger vocabularies, but the source of the benefit is unclear. We conduct a controlled study that scales the language model's vocabulary from 24K to 196K while holding data, compute, and optimization fixed. We first quantify the complexity of tokenized text, formalized via Kolmogorov complexity, and show that larger vocabularies reduce this complexity.
